# Ordinance on the Reduction of Carbon Emissions

English is not an official language of the Swiss Confederation. This translation is provided for information purposes only and has no legal force.

(CO<sub>2</sub> Ordinance)

of 30 November 2012 (Last amended on 1 January 2026)

The Swiss Federal Council,

on the basis of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act of 23 December 2011[^ingress-1] (CO<sub>2</sub> Act),

ordains:

[^ingress-1]: [SR 641.71](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8148)

## Chapter 1 General Provisions

### Section 1 Greenhouse Gases

#### Art. 1

<sup>1</sup>

This Ordinance regulates the reduction of emissions of the following greenhouse gases:

- a. carbon dioxide (CO<sub>2</sub>);
- b. methane (CH<sub>4</sub>);
- c. nitrous oxide (N<sub>2</sub>O);
- d.[^1-1] partially halogenated hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs);
- e. perfluorocarbons (PFCs);
- f. sulphur hexafluoride (SF<sub>6</sub>);
- g. nitrogen trifluoride (NF<sub>3</sub>).

<sup>2</sup> The warming effect of greenhouse gases on the climate is converted into CO<sub>2</sub> equivalents (CO<sub>2</sub>eq). The values are listed in Annex 1.

[^1-1]: Correction of 10 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 640](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/640)).

### Section 2 Definitions

#### Art. 2

[^2-1]

In this Ordinance:

- a. *rated thermal input* means the maximum possible supply of heating energy per unit of time for an installation;
- b. *total rated thermal input* means the sum of the rated thermal inputs of an installation operator’s fixed installations that are taken into account in the emissions trading system (ETS);
- c. *total output*: means the sum of the delivered electrical and thermal nominal output of a fossil-thermal power plant or a combined heat and power plant (CHP plant);
- d. *overall efficiency* means the ratio of total output to the rated thermal input of a fossil-thermal power plant or a CHP plant in accordance with the manufacturer’s specifications;
- e. *ETS participant* means operators of installations and operators of aircraft that participate in the Swiss ETS;
- f.[^2-2] *partner state* means a state with which Switzerland has entered into an international agreement or declaration of intent on conducting climate projects in that state.

[^2-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^2-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 3 Domestic Share and Benchmark Values for Emission Reductions in Individual Sectors

[^chap1-sec3-1]

[^chap1-sec3-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 2a

**Domestic share**

At least two thirds of the reduction in greenhouse gas emissions required to meet the reduction targets set out in Article 3 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act shall be achieved through measures in Switzerland.

#### Art. 3

**Benchmark values for individual sectors**

In the sectors listed below, emissions in 2030 may not exceed the following proportions of emissions generated in 1990:

- a. in the Buildings sector: 50 per cent;
- b. in the Transport sector: 75 per cent;
- c. in the Industry sector: 65 per cent;
- d. in the Other sector: 75 per cent.

### Section 5 Attestations for Projects and Programmes for Reducing Emissions or Increasing Carbon Sequestration in Switzerland and Abroad

[^chap1-sec5-1][^chap1-sec5-2]

[^chap1-sec5-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 October 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^chap1-sec5-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 4b

**Principles**[^4b-1]

Emission reductions and increases in carbon sink capacity through projects and programmes are taken into account in Switzerland if they are verified by a national attestation or an international attestation in accordance with Article 6 paragraph 2 or 4 of the Paris Agreement of 12 December 2015[^4b-2].

[^4b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^4b-2]: [SR 0.814.012](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/3479)

#### Art. 5

**Requirements**[^5-1]

<sup>1</sup>

National and international attestations (attestations) shall be issued for projects and programmes for emission reductions or increasing the carbon sink capacity in Switzerland and abroad respectively provided:

- a. Annexes 2*a* and 3 do not preclude them;
- b.

  it is credibly and comprehensibly demonstrated that the project:

  - 1\. would not be economically feasible over its term without revenues from the sale of the attestations,
  - 2\. meets the current state of the art,
  - 3\. provides for measures that lead to additional emission reductions or an increase in the carbon sink capacity as measured against the reference scenario defined in Article 6 paragraph 2 letter d,
  - 4\. complies with the other applicable legal provisions,
  - 5\. contributes to sustainable development at the location abroad and this contribution is confirmed by the partner state;

- c.

  the emission reductions or the increased carbon sink capacity:

  - 1\.[^5-2] are verifiable and quantifiable and confirmed either by measurements or by a scientific model the plausibility of which is shown by measurements,
  - 2\. are not greenhouse gas emissions that are recorded by the ETS,
  - 3\.[^5-3] were not achieved by an operator with a reduction obligation under Article 66 paragraph 1 that at the same time is applying for attestations under Article 12; attestations are issued for emission reductions from projects and programmes by such an operator provided that the reductions achieved in the greenhouse gas efficiency target under Article 67 or the measures target under Article 68 are not included;
  - 4\. are calculated so that there can be no significant ,verestimates of the eligible emission reductions or eligible increase in the carbon sink capacity;

- d. the beginning of the implementation of the project or programme does not predate the submission of an application in accordance with Article 7 by more than three months;
- e. the project or programme has not yet ended;
- f. implementing the project or the programme does not lead to a leakage of emissions; and
- g.[^5-4] the applicant can prove that it is entitled to the emission reductions or the increase in carbon sink capacity, provided it is not itself a beneficiary of the project.

<sup>2</sup>

For projects and programmes that store carbon, attestations shall be issued if they meet the requirements in Annex 19 in addition to those in paragraph 1, whereby:

- a. irrespective of the duration of the project, the permanence of the carbon capture under Annex 19 letter a is adequately guaranteed until at least 30 years after it starts to take effect and is clearly demonstrated; and
- b. in addition to the storage sites listed in Annex 19 letter d, geological storage may also take place in a storage site that has been recognised by the partner states under a multilateral agreement.[^5-5]

<sup>3</sup> The time when the applicant makes a significant financial commitment to a third party or itself takes organisational measures relevant to the project or programme is deemed the start of implementation.

[^5-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^5-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5-5]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 5a

**Programmes**

<sup>1</sup>

Projects can be grouped together into a programme if:[^5a-1]

- a.[^5a-2] they have a common purpose in addition to reducing emissions or increasing the carbon sink capacity;
- b.[^5a-3] a technology or a group of related technologies is specified in the programme description and all projects use this technology;
- c. they fulfil the inclusion criteria specified in the programme description that guarantee that the projects[^5a-4] meet the requirements of Article 5;
- d. implementation has not yet begun; and
- e.[^5a-5] they have been implemented within one country.

<sup>2</sup> Projects may be included in existing programmes if they meet the conditions of paragraph 1.[^5a-6]

<sup>3</sup> Programmes that only comprise one planned project on expiry of the first crediting period shall be continued as projects under Article 5.[^5a-7]

[^5a-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^5a-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^5a-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5a-4]: Term in accordance with No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.
[^5a-5]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5a-6]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^5a-7]: Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).

#### Art. 5b

**Scientific support**[^5b-1]

<sup>1</sup> In the case of projects or programmes whose effect in accordance with Article 5 paragraph 1 letter c number 1 cannot be quantified with sufficient precision, the applicant shall take measures to support the project according to scientific principles (scientific support).

<sup>2</sup>

The applicant shall submit a plan for the scientific support to the Federal Office for the Environment (FOEN). The plan shall in particular contain details of:

- a. the aim and the issue;
- b. the current state of the art, including the statistical data used to determine the imprecision of measurability;
- c. the procedure and evaluation;
- d. the specialist qualifications of the persons providing scientific support;
- e. the independence and potential conflicts of interest of the persons providing scientific support.

<sup>3</sup> The scientific support shall be terminated if the effect of the project or programme has been quantified with sufficient precision. The FOEN shall decide when scientific support is to be terminated.[^5b-2]

<sup>4</sup> The results of the scientific support shall be published, subject to preservation of manufacturing and business secrecy.

[^5b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^5b-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 6

**Validation of projects and programmes**[^6-1]

<sup>1</sup> Anyone wishing to apply for attestations for a project or programme must have it validated at their own expense by a validator approved by the FOEN.

<sup>2</sup>

A description of the project or programme is to be submitted to the validator. This must in particular include information about:

- a. the measures for reducing emissions or to increase the carbon sink capacity;
- b. the technologies used;
- c. the delimitation from other climate and energy policy instruments;
- d. the hypothetical progression of greenhouse gas emissions if the measures in the project or programme for reducing emissions or increasing the carbon sink capacity had not been taken (reference scenario);
- e. total expected annual emission reductions or increase in the carbon sink capacity and the underlying calculation method;
- f. the organisation of the project or programme;
- g. the anticipated investment and operating costs and expected revenues;
- h. the financing;
- i. the monitoring plan, in which the start date of the monitoring is defined and the methods for accounting for emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity are described;
- j. the duration of the project or the programme;
- k. for programmes, additionally: the purpose, the criteria for including the project in the programme, the administration of the project and one example of a project per specified technology;
- l. for projects or programmes with scientific support, additionally in the monitoring plan in accordance with letter i: a plan in accordance with Article 5*b*;
- m. for projects or programmes on increasing the carbon sink capacity additionally in the monitoring plan in accordance with letter i: the procedure for proving that permanence in accordance with Article 5 paragraph 2 is guaranteed;
- n.

  for projects or programmes outside Switzerland additionally:

  - 1\. the anticipated contribution to sustainable development at the location using indicators that show the contributions to the United Nations sustainable development goals and which can be reviewed objectively,
  - 2\. a strategy for financial sustainability that covers the long-term operation and maintenance of the technology after the end of the credit period, and
  - 3\.[^6-2] the results of the consultation with relevant interest groups and the opportunity to provide feedback on the implementation of the project or programme.

<sup>3</sup> In the case of projects and programmes in Switzerland relating to a local heating network and in the case of landfill gas projects and programmes, the description of the details required by paragraph 2 letters d, e and i is made in accordance with the requirements of Annexes 3*a* and 3*b* respectively.

<sup>4</sup> The applicant may arrange for the FOEN to carry out a preliminary assessment of a project outline. If the FOEN has carried out a preliminary assessment of the project outline, the project outline and the results of the preliminary assessment must be submitted to the validator in addition to the information in paragraph 2.

<sup>5</sup> The validator examines the information specified in paragraph 2 and whether the project meets the requirements of Article 5 or the programme meets the requirements of Articles 5 and 5*a*. Where necessary, it shall carry out inspections. The applicant and the FOEN shall be given reasonable notice of such inspections.[^6-3]

<sup>6</sup> The validator summarises the results of the examination in a validation report.

<sup>7</sup> The FOEN shall specify the form of the description of the project or programme and of the validation report.

[^6-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^6-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^6-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 7

**Application for assessing whether a project or programme qualifies for issuing attestations**[^7-1]

<sup>1</sup> Any person who wishes to request attestations for a project or a programme must submit via the validation body an application to the FOEN for assessing whether it qualifies for issuing attestations. The application shall include the project or programme description and the validation report.[^7-2]

<sup>2</sup> In the case of projects or programmes outside Switzerland, the application shall also include the decision on the qualification of the project or programme by the partner state.

<sup>3</sup> The FOEN may request additional information from the applicant if required for evaluating the application.

[^7-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^7-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 8

**Decisions on the qualification of a project or programme for issuing attestations**[^8-1]

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall decide whether a project or programme qualifies for issuing attestations on the basis of the application and, if applicable, the additional information in accordance with Article 7 paragraph 3.

<sup>2</sup> If, in the case of projects or programmes outside Switzerland, the partner state imposes a restriction in the licence for the project or programme on the permitted use of the emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity, this restriction shall be taken into account in the decision.

<sup>3</sup> The decision is valid from the start of the implementation of the project or programme until 31 December 2030 at the latest (crediting period).

[^8-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 8a

**Note in the land register**[^8a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The restriction on use as a biological or geological carbon store must be noted in the land register at the FOEN’s request. The foregoing does not apply to storing carbon in construction materials.

<sup>2</sup>

The FOEN shall request the deletion of the note in the land register if:

- a. the project or programme has ended, but at the earliest 30 years after it starts to take effect; or
- b. the stored carbon is released on the property concerned before that time.

<sup>3</sup> The owner of the property concerned shall bear the costs of the entry, amendment and deletion of the note.

<sup>4</sup> The cantons shall notify the FOEN immediately if the property concerned is used for a different purpose.

[^8a-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 8b

**Extension of the crediting period**[^8b-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The crediting period shall be extended for projects or programmes in Switzerland that qualify before 1 January 2022 until 31 December 2030 at the latest if the applicant:

- a. has the project or programme revalidated; and
- b. submits an application for extension to the FOEN no later than six months before the end of the crediting period.

<sup>2</sup> The FOEN shall approve the extension if the essential requirements of Articles 5 and 5*a* are still met.

[^8b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 9

**Monitoring report and verification of the monitoring report**[^9-1]

<sup>1</sup> The applicant shall collect all the data required in accordance with the monitoring plan to prove the emission reductions or increase in the carbon sink capacity and shall record them in a monitoring report.

<sup>2</sup> The applicant shall have the monitoring report verified at its own expense by a FOEN-approved verification body. It may not be verified by the same body that validated the report on the previous occasion.

<sup>3</sup> The verification body shall examine whether the verified emission reductions or increase in the carbon sink capacity meet the requirements of Article 5. In the case of programmes, it shall also examine whether the projects meet the inclusion criteria of Article 5*a* paragraph 1 letter c. It may limit the verification to a single representative project in the programme.

<sup>3bis</sup> Where necessary, it shall carry out inspections. The applicant and the FOEN shall be given reasonable notice of such inspections.[^9-2]

<sup>4</sup> The verification body shall record the results of the verification in a verification report.

<sup>5</sup> The monitoring report, the underlying measurement data and related verification report shall cover a period of a maximum of four years. The verification body must submit these to the FOEN within one year of the end of this period at the latest. The emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity must be indicated for each calendar year.[^9-3]

<sup>6</sup> For projects and programmes with scientific support, the monitoring reports, the related verification reports and the results of scientific support must be submitted to the FOEN every year. The quantification of the emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity must be reassessed every year.

<sup>7</sup> For projects and programmes to which a reduction obligation target under Article 31 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act applies, the monitoring reports and the verification reports must be submitted to the FOEN for each year by 31 August of the following year. The number of attestations applied for that relate to installations of an operator with a reduction obligation must be reported immediately to the FOEN and to the operator who has entered into this reduction obligation.[^9-4]

<sup>8</sup> In the case of carbon-storage projects or programmes, a monitoring report and a verification report must be submitted to the FOEN for 2030 irrespective of their duration.

<sup>9</sup> The FOEN shall specify the form of the monitoring and verification reports.

[^9-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^9-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^9-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).
[^9-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 10

**Issuing of attestations**[^10-1]

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall check the monitoring report and the corresponding verification report. Where required in order to issue attestations, the FOEN shall conduct further enquiries relating to the applicant.

<sup>2</sup> When issuing international attestations, it shall also check that the partner state recognises the transfer of emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity. If required in order to issue international attestations, the FOEN shall also conduct further enquiries in the partner state.

<sup>3</sup> It shall decide whether to issue attestations on the basis of the information mentioned in paragraphs 1 and 2.

<sup>4</sup> Attestations for projects and programmes shall be issued for the extent to which emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity have demonstrably been achieved up to the end of the crediting period.

<sup>5</sup> For projects that have not yet been implemented in programmes, no attestations shall be issued if an amendment of applicable statutory provisions leads to a situation where the measures for reducing emissions or increasing the carbon sink capacity planned in the programme must be implemented.

<sup>6</sup> The attestations shall be issued to the extent of the proven entire annual reduction in emissions or increase in the carbon sink capacity.

<sup>6bis</sup> Two per cent of the international attestations issued shall be retired and shall not count towards achieving the reduction targets. Attestations retired by the partner state shall be taken into account.[^10-2]

<sup>7</sup> Emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity that are attributable to non-refundable payments from the Confederation, cantons or communes for promoting renewable energies, energy efficiency or climate protection are only attested to the applicant if it proves that the responsible public bodies have not otherwise claimed the emission reductions. Emission reductions that are attributable to funds obtained on the basis of Article 19 of the Energy Act of 30 September 2016[^10-3] (EnA) are not attested.

<sup>8</sup> The attestations issued provide settlement for the added ecological value of the emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity. If the added ecological value has already been offset, no attestations are issued.

[^10-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^10-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^10-3]: [SR 730.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8495)

#### Art. 11

**Substantial modifications to the project or programme**[^11-1]

<sup>1</sup> Substantial modifications of the project or programme carried out after the decision has been made regarding qualification or the extension of the crediting period must be reported to the FOEN with the next monitoring report.[^11-2]

<sup>2</sup>

A modification to the project or programme is in particular deemed substantial if:

- a. the actual emission reductions or increase in the carbon sink capacity deviate from the expected annual emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity specified in the project or programme description by more than 20 per cent;
- b. the actual investment or operating costs or revenues deviate from the values specified in the project or programme description by more than 20 per cent;
- c. a change in technology takes place; or
- d. the system boundaries of a project or programme are changed.

<sup>3</sup> If necessary, the FOEN shall order a revalidation. Emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity achieved after a substantial modification are only attested in accordance with a new decision on qualification in accordance with Article 8.

<sup>4</sup> In the case of projects and programmes outside Switzerland, a new decision by the partner states on qualification is required.

<sup>5</sup> The FOEN shall approve the substantial modification if the requirements in accordance with the Articles 5 and 5*a* continue to be met.

<sup>6</sup> After revalidation, from the time the substantial modification is made the crediting period lasts until 31 December 2030 at the latest.

[^11-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^11-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 11a

**Validation and verification bodies**[^11a-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The FOEN shall approve validation and verification bodies on request provided they:

- a. demonstrate that they have the required professional abilities related to the validation or verification of offsetting projects;
- b. apply quality assurance processes; and
- c. carry out their tasks independently.

<sup>2</sup> If a validation or verification body no longer meets the requirements of paragraph 1, the FOEN shall order measures. If these measures are not implemented in a satisfactory manner, accreditation may be withdrawn.

[^11a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 11b

**International attestations in accordance with Article 6 paragraph 4 of the Paris Agreement**[^11b-1]

<sup>1</sup> Anyone wishing to have international attestations in accordance with Article 6 paragraph 4 of the Paris Agreement of 12 December 2015[^11b-2] recognised in Switzerland requires a letter of authorisation from the FOEN. The FOEN shall determine the form of the application.

<sup>2</sup>

The FOEN shall issue the letter of authorisation provided that:

- a. Annex 2*a* does not preclude the issuing of international attestations for the project or programme; and
- b. the project or programme was registered after 1 January 2021 and recognised in accordance with the mechanism set out in Article 6 paragraph 4 of the Paris Agreement.

[^11b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^11b-2]: [SR 0.814.012](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/3479)

### Section 5*a* Attestations for Installation Operators

[^chap1-sec5a-1]

[^chap1-sec5a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)). Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 12

**Attestations for installation operators with reduction obligations[^12-2]**[^12-1]

<sup>1</sup>

Installation operators with reduction obligations under Article 66 paragraph 1 to which an emission target under Article 67 applies and which are not carrying out any projects or programmes in accordance with Article 5 or 5*a* that result in emission reductions covered by the emissions target shall on application be issued with attestations for domestic emission reductions for the years 2013–2021 provided:[^12-3]

- a. the installation operator credibly reports that its emissions target will be reached without crediting emission-reduction certificates;
- b.[^12-4]

  the installation’s greenhouse gas emissions in the relevant year have been reduced when compared with the reduction pathway determined in accordance with Article 67 by the following percentages:

  - 1\. in the period 2013–2020: by more than 5 per cent,
  - 2\. in 2021: by more than 10 per cent; and

- c. for emission-reduction measures, the installation operator has received no non-refundable payments from the Confederation, cantons or communes for promoting renewable energies, energy efficiency or climate protection or from surcharges obtained in accordance with Article 35 EnA[^12-5] for geothermal power, biomass and waste from biomass; excepted from this are installation operators that had already registered for the receipt of such funds before the amendment of 8 October 2014[^12-6] came into force.[^12-7]

<sup>1bis</sup> The application for the issue of attestations must be submitted to the FOEN by 31 December 2023.[^12-8]

<sup>2</sup> Attestations are issued for emission reductions to the extent of the difference between the reduction pathway under deduction of the relevant percentage under paragraph 1 letter b and the greenhouse gas emissions in the relevant year, for the last time in 2021.[^12-9]

<sup>3</sup> …[^12-10]

#### Art. 12a

**Attestations for installation operators with a target agreement regarding the progression of energy consumption[^12a-2]**[^12a-1]

<sup>1</sup>

Installation operators that have agreed with the Confederation on targets for the progression of energy consumption and also have commitments to reduce carbon emissions (target agreement with an emissions target), without being exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy for this purpose, are issued attestations for domestic emission reductions on application for 2013–2021 if:[^12a-3]

- a. the target agreement with an emissions target meets the requirements of Article 67 paragraphs 1–3, is validated at the operator’s own expense by a FOEN-approved validator and has been assessed by the FOEN as qualified;
- b. the operator submits a monitoring report annually no later than 31 May in accordance with Article 72;
- c.[^12a-4]

  the installations’ carbon emissions during the preceding three years have in each year fallen short of the agreed reduction pathway in the target agreement with an emissions target by the following percentages:

  - 1\. in the period 2013–2020: in each year by more than 5 per cent,
  - 2\. in 2021: by more than 10 per cent; and

- d. for emission-reduction measures, the operator has received no non-refundable payments from the Confederation, cantons or communes for promoting renewable energies, energy efficiency or climate protection or from surcharges obtained in accordance with Article 35 paragraph 1 EnA[^12a-5] for geothermal power, biomass and waste from biomass; excepted from this are operators that had already registered for the receipt of such funds before the amendment of 8 October 2014[^12a-6] came into force.[^12a-7]

<sup>1bis</sup> The application for the issue of attestations must be submitted to the FOEN by 31 December 2023.[^12a-8]

<sup>2</sup> The validated target agreement with emissions target must be submitted to the FOEN no later than 31 May of the year in which attestations are being applied for.

<sup>3</sup> Substantial and permanent changes under Article 73 as well as changes under Article 78 must be reported to the FOEN. If necessary, the FOEN orders a revalidation.

<sup>4</sup> Attestations are issued for emission reductions to the extent of the difference between the reduction pathway under deduction of the relevant percentage under paragraph 1 letter c and the greenhouse gas emissions in the relevant year, for the last time in 2021.[^12a-9]

### Section 5*b* Administration of Attestations and Data Protection

[^chap1-sec5b-1]

[^chap1-sec5b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).

#### Art. 13

**Administration of attestations and data**[^13-1]

<sup>1</sup> Operators and persons that have applied for issuing attestations must at the same time provide the FOEN the account to which the attestations should be issued. Attestations are issued and administered in the Emissions Trading Registry in accordance with Articles 57–65.[^13-2]

<sup>2</sup>

The following data and documents are managed in a FOEN-administered database:

- a. first names, surnames and contact information of the applicant[^13-3], the validation body and verification body;
- b. the number of attestations issued;
- c. the core data for the project or programme; and
- d.[^13-4] the project and programme description, the validation, monitoring and verification reports and the related data.

<sup>3</sup> On request, the holder of an attestation is granted access to the data described in paragraph 2 letters a and b in connection with the attestation. Access to the data and documents described in paragraph 2 letters c and d may be granted subject to the preservation of manufacturing and trade secrecy.

[^13-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^13-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^13-3]: Term in accordance with No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). This amendment has been made throughout the text.
[^13-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 14

**Publication of information**[^14-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The FOEN may, subject to preservation of manufacturing and trade secrecy, publish:

- a.[^14-2] descriptions of projects and programmes for reducing emissions or increasing the carbon sink capacity;
- b.[^14-3] validation reports in accordance with Article 6 paragraph 6;
- c. monitoring reports in accordance with Article 9 paragraph 1;
- d. verification reports in accordance with Article 9 paragraph 4;
- e.[^14-4] the decisions in accordance with Articles 8 paragraph 1 and 10 paragraph 3[^14-5].

<sup>2</sup> Before publication, the FOEN shall provide the applicant with the documentation specified in paragraph 1. It shall require the applicant to indicate the information that in the applicant’s opinion is subject to manufacturing and trade secrecy.[^14-6]

[^14-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).

[^14-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^14-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).

### Section 5*c* Statement of Emissions for Flight Services

[^chap1-sec5c-1]

[^chap1-sec5c-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 14a

<sup>1</sup> From 1 January 2027, anyone offering scheduled or non-scheduled air travel promoted through sales advertisements in printed products or in visual electronic media must state the CO<sub>2</sub>-equivalent emissions expected to be generated by the flight to the destination airport clearly, legibly and in figures in the advertisement. Aircraft operators must make the appropriate data available to the providers of such air travel services.[^14a-1]

<sup>2</sup>

This obligation applies to flight services:

- a. from aerodromes in Switzerland;
- b. from Basel-Mulhouse Airport, provided the flight is operated under Swiss traffic laws.

<sup>3</sup> The emissions expected to be generated by the flight shall be calculated based on current scientific knowledge. The calculation method used must be presented to the FOEN upon request.

<sup>4</sup> Other greenhouse gas emissions generated by operating aircraft in the upper troposphere and the lower stratosphere must be taken into account.

<sup>5</sup> Where an emissions calculator or environmental labelling system that does not take account of other greenhouse gas emissions and their impact is used, this must be stated on sales advertisements.

[^14a-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### Section 6 Coordination of Adaptation Measures

#### Art. 15

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN coordinates the measures specified in Article 8 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>2</sup> It thereby takes account of the cantons’ measures.

<sup>3</sup> The cantons regularly inform the FOEN about their measures.

## Chapter 2 Technical Measures for Reducing Carbon Emissions from Buildings

#### Art. 16

**Reporting[^16-1]**

<sup>1</sup> The cantons regularly report to the FOEN on technical measures for reducing carbon emissions from buildings.

<sup>2</sup>

The report must contain information regarding:

- a. the CO<sub>2</sub> measures taken and planned and their effectiveness; and
- b. the progression of carbon emissions from buildings within the canton.

<sup>3</sup> On request, the cantons shall make available to the FOEN all necessary documents that form the basis of the report.

[^16-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 16a

**Information on heat generation systems**[^16a-1]

In accordance with Article 9 paragraph 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act, the essential information on heat generation systems for heating and hot water in new buildings and for their replacements in old buildings is as follows:

- a. the energy source used for the heat generation system;
- b. the nominal output of the heat generation system or the district heating connection;
- c. the heating requirement (Qh);
- d. the year in which the heat generation system was commissioned;
- e. the date of entry in the Federal Register of Buildings and Dwellings;
- f. in the case of district heating: the Federal Building Identifier (EGID) for the building in which the main heat generation system or heat supplier is located.

[^16a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

## Chapter 3 Measures for Reducing Carbon Emissions from Vehicles

### Section 1 General Provisions

#### Art. 17

[^17-1]

[^17-1]: Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 17a

**Passenger cars**[^17a-1]

<sup>1</sup> The provisions of this Chapter apply to passenger cars in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 2 letter a of the Ordinance of 19 June 1995[^17a-2] on the Technical Requirements for Road Vehicles (RVTRO).

<sup>2</sup>

They do not apply to:

- a. special-purpose vehicles in accordance with Annex I Part A number 5 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858[^17a-3];
- b. military vehicles as defined in Article 4 letter a of the Ordinance of 11 February 2004 on Military Road Traffic (MRTO)[^17a-4] that are used for military purposes.[^17a-5]

[^17a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^17a-2]: [SR 741.41](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8648)
[^17a-3]: Regulation (EU) 2018/858 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 30 May 2018 on the approval and market surveillance of motor vehicles and their trailers, and of systems, components and separate technical units intended for such vehicles, amending Regulations (EC) No 715/2007 and (EC) No 595/2009 and repealing Directive 2007/46/EC, OJ L 151 of 14.6.2018, p.1; last amended by Regulation (EU) 2019/2144, OJ L 325 of 16.12.2019, p.1.
[^17a-4]: [SR 510.710](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7360)
[^17a-5]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 17b

**Delivery vans**[^17b-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The provisions of this Chapter apply to the following types of delivery van:

- a. delivery vans in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 2 letter e RVTRO[^17b-2] with a maximum permissible weight not exceeding 3.50t;
- b. vehicles with an emission-free drive system and a maximum permissible weight of between 3.50t and 4.25t that apart from their weight meet the definition of a delivery van and whose weight in excess of 3.50t is caused solely by the additional weight of the emission-free drive system.

<sup>2</sup>

They do not apply to:

- a. special-purpose vehicles in accordance with Annex I Part A number 5 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858[^17b-3];
- b. military vehicles as defined in Article 4 letter a of the Ordinance of 11 February 2004 on Military Road Traffic (MRTO)[^17b-4] that are used for military purposes.

[^17b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^17b-2]: [SR 741.41](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8648)
[^17b-3]: See footnote to Art. 17*a* para. 2.
[^17b-4]: [SR 510.710](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7360)

#### Art. 17c

**Light articulated vehicles**[^17c-1]

<sup>1</sup> The provisions of this Chapter apply to articulated vehicles in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 2 letter i RVTRO with a gross vehicle weight not exceeding 3.50t.

<sup>2</sup>

They do not apply to:

- a. special-purpose vehicles in accordance with Annex I Part A number 5 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858[^17c-2];
- b. military vehicles as defined in Article 4 letter a MRTO[^17c-3] that are used for military purposes.[^17c-4]

[^17c-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^17c-2]: See footnote to Art. 17*a* para. 2.
[^17c-3]: [SR 510.710](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7360)
[^17c-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 17c<sup>bis</sup>

**Heavy-duty vehicles**[^17cbis-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The provisions of this Chapter apply to the following heavy-duty vehicles:

- a.

  lorries in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 2 letter f RVTRO[^17cbis-2]:

  - 1\.[^17cbis-3] with an axle configuration of 4 × 2 and a maximum laden mass of more than 16t, or
  - 2\. with an axle configuration of 6 × 2;

- b.

  articulated vehicles in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 2 letter i RVTRO:

  - 1\.[^17cbis-4] with an axle configuration of 4 × 2 and a maximum laden mass of more than 16t, or
  - 2\. with an axle configuration of 6 × 2.

<sup>2</sup> In the case of vehicles with multi-stage type approval in accordance with Article 3 number 8 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858[^17cbis-5], the composition of the base vehicle is the determining factor.

<sup>3</sup>

The provisions of this Chapter do not apply to:

- a. refuse collection vehicles;
- b. vehicles exempted under Article 25 RVTRO;
- c. military vehicles as defined in Article 4 letter a MRTO[^17cbis-6] that are used for military purposes;
- d. vehicles cleared through customs before July 2019.

[^17cbis-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^17cbis-2]: [SR 741.41](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8648)
[^17cbis-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).
[^17cbis-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).
[^17cbis-5]: See footnote to Art. 17*a* para. 2.
[^17cbis-6]: [SR 510.710](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7360)

#### Art. 17d

**Using vehicles on the road for the first time**[^17d-1]

<sup>1</sup> Vehicles that are used on the road in Switzerland for the first time are those that are registered for use in Switzerland for the first time and whose use specified in their initial registration corresponds to their actual use by the end user.

<sup>2</sup> First use on the road in a customs enclave in accordance with Article 3 paragraph 3 of the Customs Act of 18 March 2005[^17d-2] (CustA) and in Liechtenstein is deemed use on the road in Switzerland. Use on the road in a customs enclave in accordance with Article 3 paragraph 2 CustA, with the exception of Liechtenstein, is deemed use on the road abroad.

<sup>3</sup>

The following are not regarded used on the road for the first time:

- a. vehicles registered for use abroad more than twelve months before the customs declaration in Switzerland; or
- b.

  vehicles registered for use abroad more than six months but no more than 12 months before the customs declaration in Switzerland and which have covered 5000 km or more at the following times:

  - 1\. at the time of the customs declaration,
  - 2\. at the time of first registration for use if the number of kilometres covered was not recorded at the time of the customs declaration.[^17d-3]

<sup>4</sup> …[^17d-4]

[^17d-1]: Originally Art. 17*a*. Inserted by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).
[^17d-2]: [SR 631.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7812)
[^17d-3]: Amended by No I of the O 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^17d-4]: Repealed by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, with effect from 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 17e

**Reference year**[^17e-1]

The reference year is the calendar year in which achievement of the individual target is verified.

[^17e-1]: Originally Art. 17*a*, then Art. 17*a*<sup>bis</sup>. Inserted by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)) and amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 17f

[^17f-1]

[^17f-1]: Originally Art. 17*b*. Inserted by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)). Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 2 Importers and Manufacturers

#### Art. 17g

**Importers**[^17g-1]

<sup>1</sup> Any person who has a vehicle certified by the Swiss Federal Office of Energy (SFOE) in accordance with Article 23 paragraph 4 or who provides the SFOE with the data in accordance with Article 23*a* paragraph 1 letter b is deemed an importer in accordance with Article 11 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>2</sup> If the vehicle is not certified, the importer is the person or entity recorded as such in the Vehicle Admission Information System in accordance with Article 89*a* of the Road Traffic Act of 19 December 1958[^17g-2].

<sup>3</sup> If the vehicle is not certified and the importer is not recorded in the Vehicle Admission Information System, the importer is the person or entity named as such in the customs declaration.

[^17g-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^17g-2]: [SR 741.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8613)

#### Art. 18

**Large importer**[^18-1]

<sup>1</sup>

An importer is deemed to be a large importer in a reference year with respect to the vehicles in question if the new fleet comprises at least the following number of vehicles on 31 December of the reference year:

- a. 50 passenger cars;
- b. six vans or light articulated vehicles; or
- c.[^18-2] two heavy-duty vehicles.

<sup>2</sup> If an importer’s fleet of new vehicles in the previous year comprised the number of vehicles specified in paragraph 1 or more, the importer shall be treated provisionally as a large importer in the reference year for the vehicles in question.

<sup>3</sup> If an importer's fleet of new vehicles in the previous year comprised fewer vehicles than those specified in paragraph 1, the importer may apply to the SFOE to be treated provisionally as a large importer in the reference year in relation to the vehicles in question from the date of approval of the application.[^18-3]

<sup>4</sup> If, on 31 December of the reference year, the fleet of new vehicles referred to in paragraph 2 or 3 comprises fewer vehicles than those specified in paragraph 1, the importer shall be deemed to be a small importer in relation to the vehicles concerned in the reference year.

[^18-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^18-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^18-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 19

[^19-1]

[^19-1]: Repealed by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, with effect from 1 Jan. 2023 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 20

**Small importer**[^20-1]

An importer is deemed to be a small importer in a reference year with respect to the vehicles in question if the new fleet comprises fewer than the number given in Article 18 paragraph 1 on 31 December of the reference year.

[^20-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 21

**Manufacturer**

Depending on the number of vehicles first registered by a manufacturer in the year preceding the reference year, the provisions of this chapter applicable to either large importers or small importers shall apply mutatis mutandis to that manufacturer in the reference year.

#### Art. 22

**Emission pools**

<sup>1</sup> Importers and manufacturers that wish to be treated as an emission pool must apply to the SFOE by 31 December of the year before the reference year to be treated as an emission pool for a duration of one to five years.[^22-1]

<sup>2</sup> An emission pool must appoint a representative.

[^22-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 22a

**Agreement to take over vehicles**[^22a-1]

<sup>1</sup> An importer may agree with a large importer that the latter will take over vehicles from the importer and thereby enter into all the obligations under this Chapter in respect of these vehicles.

<sup>2</sup> The importer or large importer must notify the SFOE of this before the vehicles concerned are registered for use on the road for the first time.[^22a-2]

<sup>3</sup> Vehicles may only be assigned to another importer once. It is not possible to revoke an assignment.[^22a-3]

[^22a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).
[^22a-2]: Amended by No I of the O 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^22a-3]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

### Section 3 Bases for Assessments

#### Art. 23

**Obligations of importers of passenger cars, delivery vans and light articulated vehicles[^23-2]**[^23-1]

<sup>1</sup> Before a vehicle is registered for use on the road for the first time, importers of passenger cars, delivery vans and light articulated vehicles must provide the Federal Roads Office (FEDRO) with the data required for its allocation to the importer and for the calculation of any penalty.[^23-3]

<sup>2</sup>

If by 31 January of the year following the reference year large importers provide the SFOE with the data for the following vehicles based on the certificate of conformity (COC) in accordance with Article 36 or 37 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858[^23-4], the SFOE shall use these data instead of the data in accordance with paragraph 1 to calculate any penalty due:

- *a*. vehicles with a type approval or a data sheet in accordance with Articles 3 and 3*a* of the Ordinance of 19 June 1995[^23-5] on the Type Approval of Road Vehicles (RVTAO);
- b. vans and light articulated vehicles with a multi-stage type approval in accordance with Article 3 number 8 of Regulation (EU) 2018/858.

<sup>3</sup> In order to check the data provided in accordance with paragraph 2, the SFOE may require the large importer to provide a duplicate or copy of the COC.

<sup>4</sup> Small importers must have a vehicle certified by the SFOE before it is registered for use on the road for the first time.

[^23-1]: Amended by No I of the O 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^23-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^23-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^23-4]: See footnote to Art. 17*a* para. 2.
[^23-5]: [SR 741.511](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8669)

#### Art. 23a

**Obligations of importers of heavy-duty vehicles**[^23a-1]

<sup>1</sup>

Before a vehicle is registered, importers of heavy-duty vehicles must provide the following authority with the data required to assign the vehicle to the importer:

- a. FEDRO: if the vehicle has a type approval or a data sheet in accordance with Article 23 paragraph 2 letter a or a COC in electronic form;
- b. SFOE: if the vehicle does not have any of the documents listed under letter a.

<sup>2</sup> They must inform the SFOE by 31 March of the year following the reference year of the data required to calculate any penalty.

[^23a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 24

**Sources of data used for the calculation of the target and the average carbon emissions of the new car fleet**[^24-1]

The data used for the calculation of the individual target and for the calculation of the average carbon emissions of the new car fleet shall originate from a document issued by the vehicle manufacturer, by a governmental authority or by a RVTAO[^24-2] inspection centre or a foreign inspection centre listed in Annex 2, which is equivalent to the COC.

[^24-1]: Amended by No I of the O 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).
[^24-2]: [SR 741.511](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8669)

#### Art. 25

**Determining the carbon emissions of passenger cars, delivery vans and light articulated vehicles[^25-2]**[^25-1]

<sup>1</sup> To determine the carbon emissions of passenger cars, delivery vans and light articulated vehicles, the emissions according to the Worldwide Harmonised Light Vehicles Test Procedure (WLTP) in accordance with Annex XXI of Regulation (EU) 2017/1151[^25-3] are used.[^25-4]

<sup>2</sup> For vehicles for which no values determined in accordance with the WLTP are available, the carbon emissions are calculated in accordance with Annex 4.

<sup>3</sup> If the carbon emissions cannot be calculated in accordance with Annex 4, passenger cars shall be assumed to produce emissions of 350g CO<sub>2</sub>/km and vans and light articulated vehicles 400g CO<sub>2</sub>/km.

[^25-1]: Amended by No I of the O 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).
[^25-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^25-3]: Commission Regulation (EU) 2017/1151 of 1 June 2017 supplementing Regulation (EC) No 715/2007 of the European Parliament and of the Council on type-approval of motor vehicles with respect to emissions from light passenger and commercial vehicles (Euro 5 and Euro 6) and on access to vehicle repair and maintenance information, amending Directive 2007/46/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, Commission Regulation (EC) No 692/2008 and Commission Regulation (EU) No 1230/2012 and repealing Commission Regulation (EC) No 692/2008, OJ L 175, 7.7.2017, p. 1; last amended by Regulation (EU) 2023/443, OJ L 66, 2.3.2023, p. 1.
[^25-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 25a

**Determining the carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle**[^25a-1]

<sup>1</sup>

When determining the carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle, the following applies:

- a. the vehicle is attributed to a vehicle sub-group in accordance with Annex I clause 1 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242[^25a-2];
- b. the emission value in grams per tonne-kilometre, calculated in accordance with Annex I point 2.2 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242.

<sup>2</sup>

If the carbon emissions cannot be determined in accordance with paragraph 1, the following emission values shall be assumed:

- a. for vehicles that are not purely electrically powered: 1.1 times the base value of the corresponding vehicle sub-group in accordance with Annex 4*a* number 3.3:
- b. for vehicles that are purely electrically powered: 0 grams per tonne-kilometre.

[^25a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^25a-2]: Regulation (EU) 2019/1242 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 June 2019 setting CO<sub>2</sub> emission performance standards for new heavy-duty vehicles and amending Regulations (EC) No 595/2009 and (EU) 2018/956 of the European Parliament and of the Council and Council Directive 96/53/EC, OJ L 198, 25.7.2019, p. 202.

### Section 4 Taking Account of Carbon Emission Reductions and Exemptions

[^chap3-sec4-1]

[^chap3-sec4-1]: Originally before Art. 30. Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 26

**Reductions from eco-innovations**

If the average carbon emissions of a new vehicle fleet of passenger vehicles, vans or light articulated vehicles of a large importer or the carbon emissions of any vehicle of a small importer are reduced by using eco-innovations, the reduction shall be taken into account up to a maximum of 7g CO<sub>2</sub>/km.

#### Art. 26a

[^26a-1]

[^26a-1]: Repealed by No III of the O of 21 May 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 382](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/382)).

#### Art. 26b

**Reduction by means of renewable synthetic fuels**

<sup>1</sup> The reduction in carbon emissions achieved through the use of renewable synthetic fuels and taken into account in the average carbon emissions of a new vehicle fleet of a large importer or any vehicle of a small importer shall be calculated in accordance with Annex 4*b*.

<sup>2</sup>

Renewable synthetic fuels pursuant to Article 11*a* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act are renewable fuels that:

- a. have been produced from renewable energy sources other than biomass; and
- b. are used to power vehicles.

<sup>3</sup>

Applications to take account of a reduction in carbon emissions must be submitted to the SFOE by the following dates:

- a. for large importers of passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles: by 31 January of the year following the reference year;
- b. for small importers of passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles: before registration for use;
- c. for importers of heavy-duty vehicles: by 31 March of the year following the reference year.

#### Art. 26c

**Exemptions for low-emission and zero-emission vehicles**

1 If the proportion of low-emission and zero-emission passenger cars, vans and articulated vehicles in the new vehicle fleet of a large importer, calculated in accordance with Annex 4c number 1.1.3, exceeds the following percentages in the years 2025–2027 and 2030, a reduction shall be made in accordance with paragraph 3 when calculating the average carbon emissions of the new vehicle fleet in the relevant reference year:

- a.

  for passenger vehicles:

  - 1\. in the reference year 2025: 23 per cent,
  - 2\. in the reference year 2026: 24 per cent,
  - 3\. in the reference year 2027: 25 per cent;

- b.

  for vans and light articulated vehicles:

  - 1\. in the reference year 2025: 8 per cent,
  - 2\. in the reference year 2026: 9 per cent,
  - 3\. in the reference year 2027: 10 per cent,
  - 4\. in the reference year 2030: 30 per cent.

<sup>2</sup>

If the proportion of zero-emission heavy-duty vehicles in a new vehicle fleet of a large importer exceeds the following percentages in the years 2025–2027 and 2030, the calculation of the average carbon emissions of the new vehicle fleet shall be reduced by the amount specified in paragraph 3:

- a. in the reference years 2025–2027: 6 per cent
- b. in the reference year 2030: 10 per cent

<sup>3</sup>

The reduction shall correspond to the amount by which the percentage is exceeded, but shall not be higher than:

- a.

  for passenger vehicles:

  - 1\. in the reference year 2025: 7 per cent,
  - 2\. in the reference year 2026: 6 per cent,
  - 3\. in the reference year 2027: 5 per cent;

- b.

  for vans and light articulated vehicles:

  - 1\. in the reference year 2025: 7 per cent,
  - 2\. in the reference year 2026: 6 per cent,
  - 3\. in the reference year 2027: 5 per cent,
  - 4\. in the reference year 2030: 5 per cent;

- c. for heavy-duty vehicles: 3 per cent.

### Section 5 Calculating Carbon Emissions and Individual Targets, and Calculating and Imposing the Penalty

[^chap3-sec5-1][^chap3-sec5-2]

[^chap3-sec5-1]: Originally before Art. 35.
[^chap3-sec5-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 27

**Calculating the average carbon emissions of the new fleet of a large importer**[^27-1]

The average carbon emissions of a fleet of new vehicles of a large importer are calculated as follows:

- a. a new fleet of passenger vehicles, vans or light articulated vehicles: in accordance with Annex 4*c* number 1.1;
- b. a new fleet of heavy-duty vehicles: in accordance with Annex 4*c* number 1.2.

[^27-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 27a

**Calculating the carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle**[^27a-1]

The carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle are calculated in accordance with Annex 4*c* number 2.

[^27a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 28

**Individual target**[^28-1]

The individual target for carbon emissions of the fleet of new vehicles of a large importer or of the individual vehicle of a small importer is calculated in accordance with Annex 4*a*.

[^28-1]: Amended by No I of the O 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).

#### Art. 29

**Penalty amounts**

<sup>1</sup>

DETEC shall determine the amounts in accordance with Article 13 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act each year for the following reference year in Annex 5. It shall use as a basis the following amounts applicable in the European Union:

- a. for passenger vehicles, vans and light articulated vehicles: amounts in accordance with Article 8 of Regulation (EU) 2019/631[^29-1];
- b. for heavy-duty vehicles: amounts in accordance with Article 8 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242[^29-2].[^29-3]

<sup>2</sup> For the conversion into Swiss francs, the average daily exchange rate for selling francs in the twelve months prior to 30 June in the year before the reference year applies.

[^29-1]: Regulation (EU) 2019/631 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 setting CO<sub>2</sub> emission performance standards for new passenger cars and for new light commercial vehicles, and repealing Regulations (EC) No 443/2009 and (EU) No 510/2011 (recast), OJ L 111, 25.4.2019, p. 13.
[^29-2]: See footnote to Art. 25*a* para. 1 let. a.
[^29-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 30

**Penalty for large importers[^30-1]**

<sup>1</sup> If the average carbon emissions of the fleet of new vehicles of a large importer exceed the individual target, the SFOE shall impose a penalty.[^30-2]

<sup>2</sup>

Emissions exceeding the individual target are rounded down as follows to calculate the penalty:

- a. for passenger vehicles, vans and light articulated vehicles: to a hundredth of a gram CO<sub>2</sub>/km;
- b. for heavy-duty vehicles: to a hundredth of a gram CO<sub>2</sub> /tkm.[^30-3]

<sup>3</sup> If a large importer fails to pay the penalty on time, they shall owe default interest. The Federal Department of Finance (FDF) shall fix the interest rate.[^30-4]

<sup>4</sup> …[^30-5]

[^30-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^30-2]: Amended by No I of the O 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).
[^30-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^30-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^30-5]: Repealed by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, with effect from 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).

#### Art. 31

**Quarterly advance payments**

<sup>1</sup> The SFOE shall send each large importer a quarterly list of vehicles first placed on the market in the current reference year, and of the average carbon emissions and the individual target for its fleets of new vehicles.

<sup>2</sup>

It may invoice large importers for quarterly advance payments for any sanction in the reference year, in particular if:

- a. the average carbon emissions of a fleet of new vehicles exceed the individual target in the reference year by more than 5g CO<sub>2</sub>/km;
- b. the large importer is based abroad;
- c. the large importer is currently subject to debt enforcement proceedings or has existing certificates of loss.[^31-1]

<sup>3</sup> The SFOE shall calculate the amount of the advance payments on the basis of the data in paragraph 1. Advance payments already made are taken into account in preparing the invoice.

<sup>4</sup> If the payments made exceed the penalty owed for the entire year for the fleet of new vehicles, the SFOE shall refund the difference together with the reimbursement interest thereon. .[^31-2]

[^31-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^31-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).

****Arts 32** and **33**[^3233-1]**

[^3233-1]: Repealed by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, with effect from 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).

#### Art. 34

**Security**

<sup>1</sup> If a large importer is in arrears with the payment of an invoice, the SFOE may order it to be treated as a small importer until the amount owed has been paid in full.

<sup>2</sup> If the SFOE considers the payment of the penalty or interest on arrears to be at risk, it may order that it be secured in the form of a cash deposit or a bank guarantee.

#### Art. 35

**Penalty for small importers[^35-2]**[^35-1]

<sup>1</sup> If the carbon emissions of a small importer's vehicle exceed the individual target, then SFOE shall impose a penalty.[^35-3]

<sup>1bis</sup> In the case of passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles, the penalty must be paid before the vehicle is first registered.[^35-4]

<sup>2</sup> Article 30 paras 2 and 3 apply mutatis mutandis.

<sup>3</sup> …[^35-5]

[^35-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^35-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^35-5]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

## Chapter 4 Emissions Trading System

### Section 1 Installation Operators

[^chap4-sec1-1]

[^chap4-sec1-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 40

**Installation operators required to participate[^40-1]**

<sup>1</sup> An installation operator is required to participate in the ETS if it is engaged in an activity listed in Annex 6.[^40-2]

<sup>2</sup> An installation operator that wishes to engage in an activity listed in Annex 6 for the first time must notify the FOEN no later than three months before the planned start to the activity.[^40-3]

<sup>3</sup> The notification must contain details of the activities in accordance with Annex 6 and the greenhouse gas emissions.[^40-4]

<sup>4</sup> The FOEN may request further details if these are necessary in order to assess the notification.[^40-5]

[^40-1]: Term in accordance with No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). This amendment has been made throughout the text. The correction of 4 March 2022 concerns the French text only ([AS 2022 150](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/150)).
[^40-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^40-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^40-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^40-5]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 41

**Exemption from the requirement to participate**[^41-1]

<sup>1</sup> An installation operator may apply each year by 1 June in accordance with Article 40 paragraph 1 to be exempted from the obligation to participate in the ETS with effect from the beginning of the following year if the greenhouse gas emissions of the installations in the preceding three years were less than 25,000 tonnes CO<sub>2</sub>eq per year.

<sup>1bis</sup> An installation operator under Article 40 paragraph 2 that credibly reports that the greenhouse gas emissions of the installations are permanently less than 25,000 tonnes CO<sub>2</sub>eq per year may apply for the exemption to the obligation to participate in the ETS with immediate effect.

<sup>1ter</sup> An operator of reserve power plants run on gas or other energy sources that produces electricity and feeds it into the grid when the reserve is called up in accordance with the Winter Reserve Ordinance of 25 January 2023[^41-2] may not apply for exemptions in accordance with paragraphs 1 and 1<sup>bis</sup>.[^41-3]

<sup>2</sup> The installation operator in accordance with paragraphs 1 and 1<sup>bis</sup> must continue to submit a monitoring plan (Art. 51) and a monitoring report (Art. 52) unless it has made a commitment to reduce greenhouse gas emissions in accordance with Article 31 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>3</sup> If the greenhouse gas emissions of the installations increase to more than 25,000 tonnes CO<sub>2</sub>eq during a year, then the operator must again participate in the ETS from the beginning of the following year. Emissions from emergency power groups and CHP plants that are caused by a reserve call for electricity production under the Winter Reserve Ordinance are not taken into account here.[^41-4]

[^41-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^41-2]: [SR 734.722](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/40050)
[^41-3]: Inserted by Annex No II 1 of the Winter Reserve Ordinance of 25 Jan. 2023, in force from 15 Feb. 2023 to 31 Dec. 2026 ([AS 2023 43](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/43)).
[^41-4]: Amended by No II 1 of the Winter Reserve Ordinance of 25 Jan. 2023, in force from 15 Feb. 2023 to 31 Dec. 2026 ([AS 2023 43](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/43)).

#### Art. 42

**Participation by application**

<sup>1</sup> An installation operator may participate in the ETS by application if the total rated thermal input of the installations is at least 10MW.[^42-1]

<sup>2</sup> If an operator anticipates that it will fulfil the participation conditions listed in paragraph 1 for the first time, it must submit the application no later than three months before the date of fulfilment.[^42-2]

<sup>2bis</sup> …[^42-3]

<sup>3</sup>

The application must contain information about:

- a.[^42-4] …
- b.[^42-5] the installed rated thermal inputs of the installations;
- c.[^42-6] the greenhouse gases emitted from the installations in the preceding three years.

<sup>4</sup> The FOEN may request additional information if required for assessing the application.

[^42-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^42-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^42-3]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)). Repealed by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, with effect from 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^42-4]: Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^42-5]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^42-6]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 43

**Installations not taken into account[^43-1]**

<sup>1</sup> In determining whether the conditions of Article 40 paragraph 1 or Article 42 paragraph 1 are met, and in calculating the extent to which the operator must surrender emission allowances annually to the Confederation, installations in hospitals are not taken into account.[^43-2]

<sup>2</sup>

The installation operator may request that the following installations are not taken into account:[^43-3]

- a. installations used exclusively for the research, development and testing of new products and processes;
- b.[^43-4] installations used primarily for the disposal of special waste in accordance with Article 3 letter c of the Waste Management Ordinance of 4 December 2015[^43-5] (ADWO).

<sup>3</sup> The CO<sub>2</sub> levy is not refunded for thermal fuels used in installations that are not taken into account.[^43-6]

[^43-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^43-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^43-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^43-4]: Amended by Annex 6 No 2 of the Waste Ordinance of 4 Dec. 2015, in force since 1 Jan. 2016 ([AS 2015 5699](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2015/891)).
[^43-5]: [SR 814.600](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9574)
[^43-6]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 43a

**Withdrawal**[^43a-1]

An installation operator may, no later than 1 June, apply to withdraw from the ETS with effect from the beginning of the following year if it permanently ceases to meet the conditions of Article 40 paragraph 1 or 42 paragraph 1.
