<sup>2</sup> The domestic share of carbon emissions to be compensated (offsetting rate) is at least 12 per cent from 2025.[^89-2]

<sup>3</sup>

The offsetting rate in total amounts to:

- a. for 2025: 25 per cent;
- b. for 2026: 30 per cent;
- c. for 2027: 35 per cent;
- d. for 2028: 40 per cent;
- e. for 2029: 45 per cent;
- f. for 2030: 50 per cent.[^89-3]

<sup>4</sup> The carbon emissions of each motor fuel are calculated using the emission factors listed in Annex 10.

<sup>5</sup> The FOEN shall review the offsetting rates in 2027, whereby it shall take into account current transport-related emissions and international attestation prices.[^89-4]

[^89-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^89-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^89-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^89-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 90

**Permissible offsetting measures**[^90-1]

<sup>1</sup> Certificates for emission reductions or increasing the carbon sink capacity may be surrendered in order to meet the offsetting obligation; international attestations for piped renewable gas from abroad may not be surrendered.[^90-2]

<sup>2</sup> If the surrendered attestations no longer meet the permanence requirements of Article 5 paragraph 2, they may be counted towards meeting the offsetting obligation.

<sup>3</sup> If attestations under paragraph 2 have already been counted towards meeting the offsetting obligation, they shall be marked accordingly and refunded to the person with offsetting obligations. The person with offsetting obligations must submit additional attestations that meet the requirements of Article 5 in the following year in the same quantity. Attestations that could be submitted at the time of the original surrender may be submitted later.

[^90-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^90-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 91

**Meeting the offsetting obligation**

<sup>1</sup> The person with offsetting obligations shall meet their offsetting obligation by 31 December of the year following the year in question.[^91-1]

<sup>2</sup> With regard to the offsetting obligation in Switzerland in 2030, solely those emission reductions or the increase in carbon sink capacity achieved in 2030 are credited.[^91-2]

<sup>3</sup> … [^91-3]

<sup>4</sup> To meet the offsetting obligation, the installation operator or person submits a detailed and transparent report on the costs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> offset.[^91-4]

<sup>5</sup>

The following data and documents are managed in a FOEN-administered database for each person with an offsetting obligation:

- a. the quantity of carbon emissions that must be offset;
- b. the number of attestations not yet used to meet the offsetting obligation;
- c. the costs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> offset.[^91-5]

[^91-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^91-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^91-3]: Repealed by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, with effect from 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^91-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^91-5]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)). Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

#### Art. 92

**Failure to meet an offsetting obligation**

<sup>1</sup> If an installation operator or person with offsetting obligations does not do so by the deadline, then the FOEN grants an appropriate grace period.

<sup>2</sup> If an installation operator or person with offsetting obligations does not do so within the grace period, then the FOEN rules on a penalty in accordance with Article 28*e*[^92-1] of the CO<sub>2</sub>Act.

<sup>3</sup> The payment deadline is 30 days from the issue of the ruling. If a payment is late, default interest is charged. The FDF shall fix the rate of interest.[^92-2]

<sup>4</sup> The deadline for surrendering attestations is 1 June of the following year.[^92-3]

[^92-1]: The reference was modified on 1 Jan. 2025 in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).
[^92-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^92-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 2 Supply and Blending of Low-Emission, Renewable and Synthetic Aviation Fuels

[^chap7-sec2-1]

[^chap7-sec2-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 92a

**Territorial scope of application**

The obligation under Article 28*f* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act to provide and blend low-emission, renewable and renewable synthetic aviation fuels applies at Geneva and Zurich Airports.

#### Art. 92b

**Proof of compliance with blending obligation**

A valid guarantee of origin must be provided as proof of compliance with the blending obligation under Article 28*f* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act by means of the use of low-emission, renewable and renewable synthetic aviation fuels placed on the market in Switzerland.

## Chapter 7*a* Crediting of Reduction Effect of Renewable Thermal and Motor Fuels

[^chap7a-1]

[^chap7a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 1 Principles

#### Art. 92c

<sup>1</sup>

The reduction effect of renewable thermal and motor fuels and low-emission aviation fuels can be credited as part of a measure under the CO<sub>2</sub> Act provided that:

- a. the thermal or motor fuels meet the requirements of the Ordinance of 2 April 2025[^92c-1] on the Placing on the Market of Renewable and Low-Emission Thermal and Motor Fuels (RFO); and
- b. there is a guarantee of origin in accordance with Section 2*a* EnO[^92c-2] assigned to the said measure under the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>2</sup> For installation operators and aircraft operators, the guarantee of origin is assigned to the said measure by the supplier of the thermal or motor fuel.

[^92c-1]: [SR 814.311.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/40316)
[^92c-2]: [SR 730.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8496)

### Section 2 Crediting of Reduction Effect of Piped Renewable Gas from Abroad

#### Art. 92d

**Applying for international attestations for piped renewable gas from abroad**

<sup>1</sup> An importer may apply for international attestations for the reduction effect of piped renewable gas from abroad.

<sup>2</sup> Anyone wishing to apply for international attestations for a reduction effect must have the project in question validated at their own cost by an auditing body recognised by the FOEN.

<sup>3</sup>

The application must contain the following documents:

- a. the partner state’s decision regarding the project;
- b. audit report by an FOEN-recognised auditing body attesting that the requirements under Article 15 paragraph 3 of the CO <sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>4</sup> The FOEN may require additional information from the applicant if required to assess the application.

#### Art. 92e

**Issuing of international attestations for piped renewable gas from abroad**

<sup>1</sup> On the basis of the application, the FOEN shall decide if the reduction effect of the piped renewable gas from abroad is suitable for international attestations to be issued.

<sup>2</sup> If a reduction effect is deemed suitable, the FOEN shall, upon request, assess the scope of the reduction measure claimed. If necessary, it will carry out further investigations.

<sup>3</sup> It shall issue international attestations provided that the partner country recognises the transfer of emission reductions for the reduction effect.

<sup>4</sup> The ecological added value of the reduction effect is recognised by the attestations issued. If the added ecological value has already been offset, no attestations are issued.

<sup>5</sup> It shall inform the applicant about the number of international attestations issued.

#### Art. 92f

**Requirements for crediting the reduction effect**

<sup>1</sup>

ETS participants and operators with a reduction obligation can have the reduction effect of piped renewable gas from abroad credited. They must demonstrate that:

- a. piped renewable gas from abroad appears on the invoices; and
- b. the FOEN has issued a sufficient number of international attestations for the reduction effect of piped renewable gas from abroad.

<sup>2</sup> Credited international attestations for the reduction effect of piped renewable gas from abroad are credited by the FOEN to the Swiss climate target.

## Chapter 8 CO<sub>2</sub> Levy

### Section 1 General Provisions

#### Art. 93

**Materials subject to the levy**

The production, extraction and import of the following are subject to the CO<sub>2</sub> levy:

- a. coal;
- b. other thermal fuels listed in Article 2 letter a[^93-1] of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act insofar as they are subject to a mineral oil tax under the Mineral Oil Tax Act of 21 June 1996[^93-2].

[^93-1]: The reference was amended on 1 Jan. 2025 in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).
[^93-2]: [SR 641.61](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8138)

#### Art. 94

**Rate of the levy**

<sup>1</sup>

The levy shall be increased as follows:

- a. from 1 January 2014: to 60 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub>, if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2012 exceed 79 per cent of 1990 emissions;
- b.

  from 1 January 2016:

  - 1\. to 72 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2014 exceed 76 per cent of 1990 emissions,
  - 2\. to 84 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2014 exceed 78 per cent of 1990 emissions;

- c.

  from 1 January 2018:

  - 1\. to 96 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2016 exceed 73 per cent of 1990 emissions,
  - 2\. to 120 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2016 exceed 76 per cent of 1990 emissions;

- d.[^94-1] from 1 January 2022: to 120 francs per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the carbon emissions from thermal fuels in 2020 exceed 67 per cent of the 1990 emissions.

<sup>2</sup> The CO<sub>2</sub> levy is imposed in accordance with the tariffs listed in Annex 11.

[^94-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).

#### Art. 95

**Declaration of payment of the levy**[^95-1]

Any installation operator or person that trades in thermal fuels in accordance with Article 93 must declare the quantity of thermal fuels on which the CO<sub>2</sub> levy is paid and the applicable rate of the levy on invoices submitted to buyers.

[^95-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).

### Section 2 Refund of the CO<sub>2</sub> Levy

#### Art. 96

**Right to a refund**

<sup>1</sup>

The following installation operators and persons may apply for a refund of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy:[^96-1]

- a. those exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy;
- b. those that operate CHP plants, which neither participate in the ETS nor are subject to a reduction obligation (Art. 32*a* para. 1 CO<sub>2</sub> Act); or
- c. those that have paid a levy on thermal fuels that were not used as a source of energy (Art. 32*c* CO<sub>2</sub> Act).[^96-2]

<sup>2</sup>

The following are exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy:

- a. installation operators participating in the ETS (Art. 17 CO<sub>2</sub> Act);
- b. *repealed*
- c. installation operators with reduction obligations (Arts 31 and 31*a* CO<sub>2</sub> Act).[^96-3]

[^96-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^96-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan.2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).
[^96-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 96a

[^96a-1]

[^96a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 96b

**Refund for operators of fossil-thermal power plants**[^96b-1]

<sup>1</sup> On request, an operator of fossil-thermal power plants shall be refunded the difference between the CO<sub>2</sub> levy paid on thermal fuels used during the application period and the minimum price in accordance with Article 17 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.[^96b-2]

<sup>1bis</sup> In order to provide evidence of the quantity of fuel consumed, the operator must keep records of the receipt, dispatch consumption and stock levels of the fuels.[^96b-3]

<sup>2</sup>

Fossil-thermal power plants are installations that produce either solely electricity or also heat simultaneously from fossil energy sources:

- a. that are participating in the ETS for the first time after the amendment of 13 November 2019 comes into force;
- b. that have a total output of at least one MW and an overall efficiency of less than 80 per cent;
- c. that sell electricity to third parties;
- d. that are operated at a location for at least two years or for more than 50 hours per year;
- e. that are not used exclusively for the research, development and testing of new products and processes; and
- f. that are not used primarily for the disposal of municipal or special waste under Article 3 letters a and c ADWO[^96b-4] respectively.

<sup>3</sup> When assessing the external costs in accordance with Article 17 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act, the FOEN considers the costs incurred in remedying damage caused by greenhouse gas emissions; in doing so, it takes account of the current state of scientific knowledge.[^96b-5]

<sup>4</sup> The operator must obtain confirmation of the amount of the fuel subject to a refund and of the partial refund to the FOEN by 30 June. The prices for the purchase of emission allowances in the preceding twelve months and the corresponding receipts must be submitted. The FOEN may demand other information as required to issue the confirmation.[^96b-6]

<sup>5</sup> If the operator does not submit any verifiable information on the amounts paid, a value of zero francs shall be assumed.[^96b-7]

<sup>6</sup> The operator may, within six months of the FOEN issuing the confirmation, apply to the FOCBS for the refund to be paid in the form prescribed by the FOCBS.[^96b-8]

<sup>7</sup> The confirmation from the FOEN and any invoices regarding CO<sub>2</sub> levy payments must be submitted to the FOCBS on request.[^96b-9]

<sup>8</sup>

The right to a refund shall be forfeited if:

- a. confirmation from the FOEN is not obtained by the deadline; or
- b. the application for a refund is not submitted to the FOCBS by the deadline.[^96b-10]

#### Art. 97

**Application for refund[^97-1]**

<sup>1</sup> An application for a refund must be submitted to the FOCBS in the form that the latter prescribes.[^97-2]

<sup>2</sup> It must include the type and quantity of acquired thermal fuels.[^97-3]

<sup>3</sup> The FOCBS[^97-4] may demand further evidence if required to determine the refund. In particular, the invoices for the CO<sub>2</sub> levy paid must be produced if it so requests.[^97-5]

[^97-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^97-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^97-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

This amendment has been made throughout the text.
[^97-5]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).

#### Art. 98

**Periodicity of the refund[^98-1]**

<sup>1</sup> An application for a refund may cover a period of 1 to 12 months.[^98-2]

<sup>2</sup> It must be submitted within six months of the end of the fiscal year in which the thermal fuel subject to the CO<sub>2</sub> levy is purchased.[^98-3]

<sup>3</sup> The right to a refund is forfeited if the application is not submitted by the deadline.

[^98-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^98-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^98-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 98a

**Refund for CHP plant operators that are neither participating in the ETS nor are subject to a reduction obligation**[^98a-1]

<sup>1</sup> On request, an installation operator that is neither a participant in the ETS nor subject to a reduction obligation and which operates CHP plants in accordance with Article 32*a* paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act shall be refunded 60 per cent of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy on the fuels used for electricity production for each CHP plant which has a rated thermal input of at least 0.5MW and no more than 20MW.

<sup>2</sup>

The CHP plant operator is entitled to a refund of the remaining 40 per cent of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy on the fuels used to produce electricity if:

- a. it allocates this amount to the measures set out in Article 32*b* paragraph 2 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act;
- b. the measure effectively serves to increase energy efficiency;
- c. it does not implement the measures in a plant whose operator is subject to a reduction obligation or is participating in the ETS;
- d. it does not claim the effect of the measures elsewhere; and
- e. it implements the measures within three subsequent years.

<sup>3</sup> The FOEN may, on request, extend the period referred to in paragraph 2 letter e by two years.

[^98a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 98b

**Application for a refund for CHP plant operators that are neither participating in the ETS nor subject to a reduction obligation[^98b-2]**[^98b-1]

<sup>1</sup>

A CHP plant operator that neither participates in the ETS nor is subject to a reduction obligation must submit the application for a refund to the FOEN by 30 June. It must submit the following information in particular:[^98b-3]

- a. the quantity of taxed fuels used for electricity production, calculated on the basis of the annual quantity of electricity shown on the guarantee of origin and of the calorific value of the energy source used;
- b. the guarantee of origin in accordance with Article 9 paragraph 1 EnA[^98b-4];
- c. information on the rated thermal input;
- d. the monitoring report;
- e. information on the annual development of the carbon emissions resulting from the measured production of electricity;
- f.[^98b-5] confirmation from the local canton that the emission threshold values in accordance with the Ordinance on Air Pollution Control of 16 December 1985[^98b-6] have been complied with;
- g. information on planned measures;
- h. and i.[^98b-7] …
- j.[^98b-8] confirmation that thermal fuels on which the levy was paid were used to operate the CHP plants, together with details of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy rate applied.

<sup>2</sup> …[^98b-9]

<sup>3</sup> The operator may apply to the FOCBS for repayment of the refund in the form prescribed by the FOCBS within six months of the date the FOEN issues the confirmation.[^98b-10]

<sup>3bis</sup> The confirmation from the FOEN of the invoices for the CO<sub>2</sub> levy paid must be submitted to the FOCBS if it so requests.[^98b-11]

<sup>4</sup> The monitoring report referred to in paragraph 1 letter d shall in particular contain information on the development of carbon emissions resulting from electricity production and a description of the measures and investments implemented. The FOEN specifies the form of the report.

#### Art. 98

***c*[^98c-1]Periodicity of refunds for CHP plant operators that are neither participating in the ETS nor subject to a reduction obligation[^98c-2]**

<sup>1</sup> Applications for a refund in accordance with Article 98*b* shall be submitted for a period of 12 months and apply to fuels consumed in the previous year or in the fiscal year that ended in the previous year.

<sup>2</sup> The refund is made by the FOCBS and covers 100 per cent of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy on the fuels used to produce electricity.

<sup>3</sup>

A claim for refund is forfeited if:

- a. confirmation from the FOEN is not obtained by the deadline; or
- b. the application for payment of the refund is not submitted to the FOCBS in due time.[^98c-3]
