#### Art. 98d

**Non-compliance with the investment obligation for operators of CHP plants that neither participate in the ETS nor are subject to a reduction obligation**[^98d-1]

<sup>1</sup> If an operator of CHP plants who neither participates in the ETS nor is subject to a reduction obligation fails to comply with the investment obligation under Article 32*b* paragraph 2 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act, the FOEN shall order repayment of 40 per cent of the refund received.

<sup>2</sup> The repaid amounts shall be considered revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.

[^98d-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 99

**Refund for fuels not used as a source of energy**

<sup>1</sup> Any person who has paid a levy on thermal fuels that were not used as a source of energy and wants to apply for a refund must prove the quantity that was not used as a source of energy. To this end, records (consumption control) must be maintained for the input, output and consumption of the thermal fuels as well as for warehouse stocks.

<sup>1bis</sup> The FOCBS may the refund the levy for thermal fuels not used as a source of energy based on the purchased quantity, provided the applicant’s operating conditions leave no doubt that the use was not related to energy production and the applicant provides the FOCBS with binding confirmation that using the thermal fuels was not related to energy production.[^99-1]

<sup>2</sup> The refund application must be submitted to the FOCBS in the prescribed form.

<sup>3</sup>

It must contain information about:

- a. the form of use not related to energy production;
- b. the quantity and type of thermal fuels that were not used as a source of energy;
- c.[^99-2] …

<sup>4</sup> The FOCBS may request additional evidence if required for the refund. Invoices for the CO<sub>2</sub> levy paid must be produced if it so requests.[^99-3]

[^99-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).
[^99-2]: Repealed by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^99-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)).

#### Art. 100

**Periodicity of the refund for fuels not used as a source of energy**

<sup>1</sup> A refund application may cover a period from 1 to 12 months.

<sup>2</sup> It must be submitted within six months of the end of the fiscal year in which the fuel was used or purchased.[^100-1]

<sup>3</sup> A claim for refund is forfeited if the application is not submitted in due time.[^100-2]

[^100-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^100-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 101

**Document retention**

All documents relevant to the refund are to be retained for five years and submitted to the FOCBS if requested.

#### Art. 102

**Minimum refund amount**[^102-1]

Amounts of less than 100 francs per request will not be refunded.

[^102-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 24 Nov. 2021, in force since 1 Jan. 2022 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)).

#### Art. 103

**Refund deferral**

If an installation operator or person referred to in Article 96 violates the obligation to cooperate under this Ordinance, then the FOCBS may, in agreement with the FOEN, defer refund of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.

## Chapter 9 Use of the Revenues from the CO<sub>2</sub> Levy

### Section 1 Calculating CO<sub>2</sub> Levy Revenue

[^chap9-sec1-1]

[^chap9-sec1-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 103a

<sup>1</sup> The revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy is calculated from the current revenue from the levy minus the remuneration of enforcement (Art. 132) and losses on receivables.

<sup>2</sup> Current revenue is defined as gross receipts from the levy minus any levy refunds and the share of the Principality of Liechtenstein in accordance with Article 6 paragraph 2 of the Agreement of 29 January 2010[^103a-1] on the Treaty between the Swiss Confederation and the Principality Liechtenstein on Environmental Levies.

[^103a-1]: [SR 0.641.751.411](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/2468)

### Section 1*a* Global Financial Assistance for the Long-Term Reduction of Carbon Emissions from Buildings

[^chap9-sec1a-1]

[^chap9-sec1a-1]: Originally: Section 1. Amended by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).

#### Art. 104

**Eligibility for global financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup>

Global financial assistance in accordance with Article 34 paragraph 2 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act is granted if:[^104-1]

- a. the requirements set out in Articles 55–60 of the Energy Ordinance of 1 November 2017[^104-2] (EnO) are met;
- b. the measures effectively reduce carbon emissions, including reducing power consumption in the winter period; and
- c. the measures are implemented in a harmonised manner throughout the cantons.

<sup>2</sup>

It shall not grant global financial assistance in particular for measures:

- a.[^104-3] that are implemented in installations whose operators is subject to a reduction obligation under the CO<sub>2</sub> Act or that is participating in the ETS;
- b. that are implemented within the framework of agreements with the Confederation in accordance with Article 4 paragraph 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act to achieve the statutory reduction target where no additional emission target is thereby achieved;
- c. that are already supported elsewhere by the Confederation or a private organisation in the climate sector, if no additional emission target is thereby achieved.

[^104-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^104-2]: [SR 730.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8496)
[^104-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 104a

**Supplementary contribution**[^104a-1]

The supplementary contribution under Article 34 paragraph 2 letter b of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act is based on the effectiveness of the cantonal funding programme and the amount of the cantonal credit. It comprises a minimum amount and an additional amount.

[^104a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 105

**Procedure**

The procedure is governed by Articles 63, 64 and 67 EnO[^105-1], whereby:

- a. the canton applying for global financial assistance must additionally declare its willingness to implement a programme with measures in accordance with Article 104;
- b. the SFOE forwards the application to the FOEN for information.

[^105-1]: [SR 730.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8496)

#### Art. 106

**Use of funds**

The canton must use at least 80 per cent the funds resulting from the Confederation’s global financial assistance and the loans provided by the canton for the relevant programme for energy and waste heat measures in accordance with Article 50 EnA[^106-1].

[^106-1]: [SR 730.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8495)

#### Art. 107

**Payment of global financial assistance**

Global financial assistance is paid annually to the cantons.

#### Art. 108

**Implementation costs**

<sup>1</sup> In return for implementing the measures, the canton is paid a lump sum from the funds available to the cantons for the long-term reduction in carbon emissions from buildings in accordance with Article 34 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act and paid out in the form of global financial assistance. The lump sum is five per cent of the funding amount allocated by the canton and allowable as the federal share.

<sup>2</sup> Out of the same funds, the SFOE is paid a maximum of one million francs per year for programme communication.

#### Art. 109

**Communication**

<sup>1</sup> The SFOE is responsible for the nationwide communication of the programme to reduce carbon emissions from buildings. It also lays down basic principles to ensure uniform communication across cantons.

<sup>2</sup> The cantons shall publicise the funding programme and draw appropriate attention to the fact that part of the funding comes from the proceeds of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.

#### Art. 110

**Reporting**

<sup>1</sup> Reporting is based on Article 59 EnO[^110-1].

<sup>2</sup> The report must provide adequate information on the emission reductions expected and achieved with the funding programme, in addition to the information in accordance with Article 59 paragraph 3 EnO per project funded and broken down by the individual measures.

<sup>3</sup> The SFOE shall forward the report to the FOEN for information purposes.

[^110-1]: [SR 730.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8496)

#### Art. 111

**Control**

Control of the correct use of the global financial assistance is governed by Article 60 EnO[^111-1].

[^111-1]: [SR 730.01](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8496)

#### Art. 111a

Repealed

### Section 1*b* Funding for Projects on the Direct Use of Geothermal Energy and of Projects for Developing Indirectly Exploitable Resources

[^chap9-sec1b-1][^chap9-sec1b-2]

[^chap9-sec1b-1]: Originally: Section 1*a*.
[^chap9-sec1b-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 112

**Eligibility**[^112-1]

The following are eligible:

- a.

  the following measures conducted in projects on the direct use of geothermal energy to provide heat (Art. 34*a* para. 1 let. a CO<sub>2</sub> Act), that meet the requirements of Annex 12:

  - 1\. prospecting,
  - 2\. development of geothermal reservoirs;

- b. projects to develop indirectly exploitable hydrothermal resources to provide heat (Art. 34*a* para. 1 let. b CO<sub>2</sub> Act), that meet the requirements of Annex 12*a* if the hydrothermal resources have been selected, discovered and characterised in the initial exploratory drilling process and direct use has proven impossible, in particular due to insufficient temperatures.

[^112-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 113

**Application**[^113-1]

<sup>1</sup> Applications for financial assistance must be submitted to the SFOE.[^113-2]

<sup>2</sup>

The application must meet the following requirements:

- a.

  for projects for the direct use of geothermal energy:

  - 1\. prospecting: the requirements of Annex 12 number 3.1,
  - 2\. development: the requirements of Annex 12 numbers 4.1 and 4.2;

- b. for projects to develop indirectly usable hydrothermal resources: the requirements of Annex 12*a* numbers 3.1 and 3.2.[^113-3]

<sup>2bis</sup> Evidence must be included that the applications for the authorisations and licences necessary have been submitted in full to the competent authorities and that project financing has been secured.[^113-4]

<sup>3</sup> The SFOE will consult a panel of up to six experts, independent of the project, to examine applications. In addition, the canton concerned may appoint a representative to the expert panel.

<sup>4</sup> The panel of experts evaluates the applications and makes a recommendation for the SFOE’s assessment of the project. The cantonal representative has no say in the recommendation to the SFOE. The panel of experts may call in further experts to carry out its tasks.

<sup>5</sup> If the conditions for granting financial assistance are met, the Confederation shall conclude an administrative contract with the applicant. In particular, it shall specify the conditions for repayment in accordance with Article 113*c*[^113-5].

[^113-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).
[^113-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^113-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^113-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^113-5]: The reference was amended on 1 May 2025 in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).

#### Art. 113a

**Funding amount**[^113a-1]

The funding amounts are as follows:

- a. for projects for the direct use of geothermal energy: maximum 60 per cent of the allowable investment costs; allowable costs are those listed in Annex 12 number 2;
- b. for projects to develop indirectly usable hydrothermal resources: maximum 40 per cent of the allowable investment costs; allowable costs are those listed in Annex 12*a* number 2;

[^113a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 113b

**Order of consideration**[^113b-1]

<sup>1</sup> If no funds or insufficient funds are available for a project, the SFOE shall place the project on a waiting list unless it clearly does not meet the eligibility criteria. The SFOE shall inform the applicant accordingly.

<sup>2</sup> When funds are again available, the SFOE shall first take into account the projects for the direct use of geothermal energy and then those to exploit hydrothermal resources that can be used indirectly to generate heat. The most advanced projects shall be taken into account. If several projects are equally advanced, the project for which the application was submitted in full earliest will be considered.

[^113b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 113c

**Reclaiming financial assistance**[^113c-1]

<sup>1</sup> Articles 28–30 of the Subsidies Act of 5 October 1990[^113c-2] (SubA) apply mutatis mutandis to reclaiming financial assistance. Financial assistance can also be reclaimed if the operations of the installation generate profits that subsequently make the financial assistance appear unnecessary.

<sup>2</sup> If the project is used for other purposes and a profit is made, the SFOE may demand a pro rata or full repayment of the financial assistance paid out.

<sup>3</sup>

The SFOE must be informed of any other use or sale regarding:

- a. the planned type of use;
- b. ownership and sponsorship;
- c. profits and their amount.

[^113c-1]: Originally: Art. 113*b*. Inserted by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).
[^113c-2]: [SR 616.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7794)

### Section 1*c* Funding of New or Substantially Expanded Installations to Produce Renewable Gases

[^chap9-sec1c-1]

[^chap9-sec1c-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 113d

**Eligibility**

<sup>1</sup> New or substantially expanded installations that produce gas by fermenting biomass and processing this to produce biomethane are eligible for funding.

<sup>2</sup>

New installations are:

- a. installations erected at a site for the first time and installations that substantially replace an existing installation;
- b. existing installations retrofitted from electricity generation to biomethane production.

<sup>3</sup> A substantial expansion of an installation involves structural measures that result in an increase in gross annual energy production of at least 25 per cent compared to the average of the last three full years of operation prior to the commissioning of the expansion.

<sup>4</sup> Biomethane is defined as processed biogas that complies with the gas quality requirements for high-calorific gas in Guideline G18, Gas Quality, issued by the Swiss Gas and Water Association (SVGW) in June 2022[^113d-1].

<sup>5</sup>

The following installations are not eligible for funding:

- a. installations that take part in the feed-in remuneration system under Article 19 EnA[^113d-2] or that receive a sliding market premium under Article 29*a* EnA or an operating costs contribution under Article 33*a* EnA for biomass installations;
- b. installations that have received other federal funding within the past 10 years;
- c. installations that do not meet their own heating energy needs with renewables.

[^113d-1]: Guideline G18 can be obtained for a fee from the SVGW at [www.svgw.ch](http://www.svgw.ch/) &gt; Regelwerk &gt; Shop or by email (info@svgw.ch).
[^113d-2]: [SR 730.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8495)

#### Art. 113e

**Application**

Funding applications are to be submitted to the SFOE.

#### Art. 113f

**Funding amount**

<sup>1</sup>

The amount of funding for new installations is based on the capacity of the processing installation in Nm<sup>3</sup> of methane per hour and is calculated proportionately according to production category:

- a. for the first 100Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr: CHF 10,000 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr;
- b. for 101–400 Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr: CHF 5,000 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr;
- c. for all others: CHF 2 000 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr.

<sup>2</sup>

The amount of funding in the case of a substantial expansion is based on the difference between the capacity after the substantial expansion and the original capacity and is calculated proportionately according to production category:

- a. for the first 100Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr: CHF 4,000 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr;
- b. for 101–400 Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr: CHF 2,000 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr;
- c. for all others: CHF 800 per Nm<sup>3</sup> methane/hr.

<sup>3</sup> The funding amount per installation shall be maximum 30 per cent of the actual and allowable costs, with an upper limit of CHF 2.8 million.

<sup>4</sup> Water purification installations and installations under Article 113*d* paragraph 2 letter b receive 15 per cent of the funding amounts stated in paragraphs 1 and 2.

<sup>5</sup>

If two applications are submitted for the same installation, only one amount is paid out. This is divided as follows:

- a. Production: 85 per cent;
- b. Processing and feed-in: 15 per cent.

<sup>6</sup> Applications for amounts under CHF 80 000 will not be considered.

### Section 1*d* Funding for Installations Using Solar Thermal Energy to Generate Process Heat

[^chap9-sec1d-1]

[^chap9-sec1d-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 113g

**Eligibility**

<sup>1</sup>

Installations using solar thermal energy to generate process heat are eligible for funding if they meet the following criteria:

- a.

  They produce heat for:

  - 1\. primarily commercial and industrial processes for the manufacture, pro-cessing or refining of products; or
  - 2\. the provision of services.

- b. They have a nominal thermal collector output of at least 20kW.
- c. They have a device for measuring the actual solar heat yield.
- d. They use panels that meet the requirements listed in the Kollektorliste 12/2021[^113g-1], Version 01/2025.
- e.

  They have a certificate issued by an independent organisation demonstrating that they are correctly integrated into the processes supported; this includes:

  - 1\. the hydraulic connection;
  - 2\. the consumption profile and the temperature level of production and consumption;
  - 3\. the design and integration in a thermal storage system,
  - 4\. the stagnation plan;
  - 5\. the anticipated annual solar yield; this must be determined using a dynamic simulation programme; and
  - 6\. the concept for measuring the effectively utilised solar yield.

<sup>2</sup>

The following are not eligible for funding:

- a. installations run by ETS participants;
- b. installations receiving other federal funding.

<sup>3</sup> Operators of installations that have entered into a reduction obligation in accordance with Article 31 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act may be eligible for funding if the emission reductions from the funded solar thermal installations are not covered by the greenhouse gas efficiency target under Article 67 or the measures target under Article 68.

[^113g-1]: Explanations about the ‘Kollektorliste’ can be found at [www.kollektorliste.online](http://www.kollektorliste.online).

#### Art. 113h

**Application**

Funding applications are to be submitted to the SFOE.

#### Art. 113i

**Funding amount**

The funding amount comprises a basic contribution of CHF 2,400 and a contribution of CHF 1,000 per kW of nominal thermal collector output.

#### Art. 113j

**Reclaiming financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> If the actual solar yield averaged over three years after commissioning is less than 80 per cent of the expected yield under Article 113*g* paragraph 1 letter e number 5, a proportion of the funding amount shall be reclaimed.

<sup>2</sup> In cases of hardship, repayment may be waived in whole or in part.

#### Art. 113k

**Monitoring and publication of data**

The SFOE may commission scientific monitoring of an installation in receipt of funding and publish the data collected and analysis findings.

### Section 2 Funding of Technologies for the Reduction of Greenhouse Gas Emissions

#### Art. 114

**Guarantee**

<sup>1</sup>

The Confederation guarantees loans for equipment and processes in accordance with Article 35 paragraph 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act if:

- a. there are market opportunities for the equipment and processes;
- b. the borrower can credibly demonstrate creditworthiness;
- c. the lender takes the guarantee into account in determining the interest on the loan; and
- d.[^114-1] none of the criteria under Article 35 paragraph 3 letters a–c are affected to a considerable degree.

<sup>2</sup> It only guarantees loans to a borrower based in Switzerland and granted by a bank in accordance with the Federal Act of 8 November 1934 on Banks and Savings Banks[^114-2] (BankA) or by another appropriate lender based in Switzerland.[^114-3]

<sup>3</sup> The guarantee may secure all or part of the loan but may not exceed CHF 3 million.

[^114-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^114-2]: [SR 952.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11561)
[^114-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 115

**Granting of the guarantee**

<sup>1</sup> On application, the FOEN shall grant the borrower a guarantee if the requirements of Article 114 are met.

<sup>2</sup>

The application for granting the guarantee must include:

- a. information about the borrower’s organisational form and financial structure;
- b. technical documentation of the project, including a description of the equipment and processes and planned development and marketing;
- c. a description of the project’s business model;
- d. information regarding the extent to which the equipment and processes meet the requirements of Article 114.

<sup>3</sup> The FOEN may request additional information if it is required for assessing the application.

<sup>4</sup> It may require collateral to secure the guarantee in well-founded cases.[^115-1]

[^115-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).

#### Art. 116

**Notification obligation and reporting**

<sup>1</sup>

A borrower who has received a loan guarantee must inform the FOEN without delay during the duration of the guarantee about:

- a. changes that could have an effect on the guarantee;
- b. changes in contact information.

<sup>2</sup>

It must submit a report every quarter to the FOEN on:[^116-1]

- a. the status of the guaranteed loan;
- b.[^116-2] the course of business and its expected development; and
- c.[^116-3] the liquidity and financial structure.

<sup>3</sup> It provides the business report, balance sheet and statement of financial performance to the FOEN annually. These must be submitted no later than three months following their completion.[^116-4]

[^116-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^116-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^116-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^116-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).

#### Art. 117

**Implementation**[^117-1]

<sup>1</sup> DETEC appoints a steering committee to administer the technology fund and by means of an administrative contract, a guarantees committee and an administrative office. It determines the principles for awarding guarantees and for the organisation.

<sup>2</sup> The steering committee has strategic leadership over the technology fund.

<sup>3</sup> The guarantees committee assesses at the request of the administrative office the guarantee requests addressed to the FOEN.

<sup>4</sup> The administrative office operates the technology fund. It is responsible in particular for the assessment of guarantee requests, administration of the guarantees and the processing of guarantee cases as well as auditing reporting under Article 116. It submits a report on the technology fund’s activities and financial situation to the steering committee.

<sup>5</sup> The administrative office invoices guarantee holders for fees for the evaluation of guarantee requests and carrying out controls on guarantee holders for the term of the guarantee. The fee for the evaluation of the guarantee request is calculated using a flat rate and is based on number 9 of the Annex to the Ordinance on the Fees charged by the Federal Office for the Environment of 3 June 2005[^117-2] (FeeO-FOEN). The annual guarantee fee is calculated according to costs (Art. 4 FeeO-FOEN); it amounts to no more than 0.9 per cent of the guarantee amount per year.[^117-3]

[^117-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^117-2]: [SR 814.014](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9451)
[^117-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 118

**Financing**

<sup>1</sup> The resources for the technology funds are provided in the budget.

<sup>2</sup> The Federal Assembly decides on the funding commitments for granting the guarantees.

<sup>3</sup> The sum of the guarantees may never exceed 750 million francs.[^118-1]

[^118-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 3 Distribution to the Public

#### Art. 119

**Portion of the revenue for the public**[^119-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The portion of the levy revenue for the public includes the portion for the public of:

- a. the revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy not refunded two years prior to the collection year because the conditions under Article 32*b* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act were not met;
- b. the collection year’s estimated annual revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy in accordance with Article 36 paragraph 1 letter b of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act minus the difference to the portion estimated two years before the collection year;
- c. the funds that, two years prior to the collection year, exceeded CHF 150 million in accordance with Article 33*a* paragraph 2 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act; and
- d. the funds that, up to the end of the second year before the collection year, were not used for the purposes stated in Article 33*a* paragraph 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>2</sup> The funds under paragraph 1 letter d shall be added to the portion of the revenue for the public every five years.

[^119-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 120

**Distribution**

<sup>1</sup> The portion of the revenue for the public is distributed by insurers in each collection year on behalf of, and under the supervision of, the FOEN.[^120-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The following are deemed insurers:

- a. providers of mandatory health insurance under the Federal Act of 18 March 1994[^120-2] on Health Insurance (HInsA);
- b. providers of military insurance under the Federal Act of 19 June 1992[^120-3] on Military Insurance (MilIA).

<sup>3</sup>

Insurers distribute the portion of the revenue to the public in even payments to all persons who in the collection year:

- a. are subject to an insurance obligation under the HInsA or under Article 2 paragraph 1 or 2 MilIA; and
- b. are domiciled or habitually resident in Switzerland.

<sup>4</sup> Distributions to persons who are only intermittently insured by an insurer during the collection year are made in proportion to the duration of their stay.

<sup>5</sup> The insurers settle the amounts by deducting the distributions from the premiums due in the collection year.

[^120-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^120-2]: [SR 832.10](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10133)
[^120-3]: [SR 833.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10252)

#### Art. 121

**Payouts to the insurers**

<sup>1</sup> The portion of the revenue for the public is proportionately paid out to insurers by 30 June of the collection year.

<sup>2</sup> The amount for each insurer is calculated according to the number of persons it has insured who meet the requirements of Article 120 paragraph 3 as of 1 January of the collection year.

<sup>3</sup> The difference between the amounts paid out and the sum of the actual distributed amounts is balanced in each subsequent year.

#### Art. 122

**Organisation**

<sup>1</sup>

Each insurer shall notify the Federal Office of Public Health (FOPH) by 20 March of the collection year regarding:

- a. the number of persons it has insured who, as of 1 January of the collection year, meet the requirements of Article 120 paragraph 3;
- b. the sum actually distributed in the previous year.

<sup>2</sup> Insurers shall inform the insured persons regarding the amounts to be distributed when they inform them of new premiums for the collection year. In addition they must provide the insured persons with a factsheet prepared by the FOEN on the redistribution procedure.[^122-1]

[^122-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 123

**Insurers’ remuneration**

To cover the expense of implementing this Ordinance and the Ordinance of 12 November 1997[^123-1] on the Incentive Tax on Volatile Organic Compounds, insurers shall receive 30 cents per insured person who, as of 1 January of the collection year, meets the requirements of Article 120 paragraph 3.

[^123-1]: [SR 814.018](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9456)

### Section 4 Distribution to the Private Sector

#### Art. 124

**Portion of the revenue for the business community**[^124-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The portion of the levy revenue for the business community includes the portion for the business community of:

- a. the revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy not refunded two years prior to the collection year because the conditions under Article 32*b* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act were not met;
- b. the collection year’s estimated annual revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy in accordance with Article 36 paragraph 1 letter b of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act minus the difference to the portion estimated two years before the collection year;
- c. the funds that, two years prior to the collection year, exceeded CHF 150 million in accordance with Article 33*a* paragraph 2 of the CO2 Act; and
- d. the funds that, up to the end of the second year before the collection year, were not used for the purposes stated in Article 33*a* paragraph 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

<sup>2</sup> The funds under paragraph 1 letter d shall be added to the portion of the revenue for the business community every five years.

[^124-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 124a

**Exclusion from distribution of revenue share**[^124a-1]

<sup>1</sup> Operators with a reduction obligation who use the same OASI accounting number for installations at different sites are excluded from the distribution of the business community’s revenue share only to the amount of the wages of the persons employed at sites for which they are exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy (partial exclusion).

<sup>2</sup> In order to receive the share of revenue to which it is entitled, an operator with partial exclusion must report the relevant wage sums to the OASI compensation office by 15 April of the collection year.

<sup>3</sup> Operators whose reduction obligation ends prematurely are entitled to the business community’s revenue share from the following year onwards. This revenue is distributed by the FOEN. The funds used for this purpose may come from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy revenue of another year.

<sup>4</sup>

Operators pursuant to paragraph 3 must report the following to the FOEN within three months of being requested to do so:

- a. the total wage bill pertinent to distribution;
- b. account details;
- c. name of the OASI compensation office.

[^124a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 125

**Distribution**

<sup>1</sup> The portion of the revenue for the business community is distributed to employers in accordance with the directives of the Federal Social Insurance Office by the OASI compensation offices (compensation offices) in each collection year on behalf of and under the supervision of the FOEN and with the involvement of the Central Compensation Office.[^125-1]

<sup>2</sup> The compensation offices shall distribute the portion of the revenue for the business community by 30 September of the collection year. If justified, the FOEN may extend these deadlines on application.[^125-2]

<sup>3</sup> They distribute the portion of the revenue for the business community in proportion to the employees’ qualifying salary for OASI two years before the collection year. Salaries subsequently corrected following employer reviews are not taken into account.

<sup>4</sup> The compensation offices distribute the portion of the revenue for the business community by offsetting them against employers’ contributions due in the collection year or by a pay-out to employers. Amounts of 50 francs or more that cannot be offset will be paid out. In the event of changes being made, amounts of 50 francs or more will be offset or paid out.[^125-3]

<sup>5</sup> The auditors for the compensation offices shall review the distribution of the portion of the revenue for the business community in the final audit and submit a report thereon to the FOEN in accordance with the directives issued by the Federal Social Insurance Office.[^125-4]

[^125-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^125-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).
[^125-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^125-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

#### Art. 126

**Organisation**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall notify the compensation offices annually of the distribution factor.

<sup>2</sup> The compensation offices shall inform employers who are eligible to make a claim about the distribution factor and the paid-out sums annually.

#### Art. 127

**Remuneration of the compensation offices**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN determines the remuneration of the compensation offices in consultation with the Federal Social Insurance Office.

<sup>2</sup> The remuneration is based on a cost code, taking into account the number of employers with which the compensation offices concerned are required to settle.

## Chapter 9*a* Funding of Measures to Reduce Greenhouse Gas Emissions in Aviation

[^chap9a-1]

[^chap9a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 127a

**Principles**

<sup>1</sup>

The following measures shall be used to reduce greenhouse gas emissions in aviation:

- a. the proceeds from penalties imposed for non-compliance with the obligation to blend low-emission, renewable or renewable synthetic aviation fuels (Art. 28*g* para. 8 CO<sub>2</sub> Act[^127a-1]);
- b. revenue from the auctioning of emission allowances for aircraft (Art. 37*a* para. 1 let. b CO<sub>2</sub> Act[^127a-2]);
- c. funds to finance under Article 103*b* paragraph 2 of the Aviation Act of 21 December 1948[^127a-3] and under Article 37*d* of the Federal Act of 22 March 1985[^127a-4] on the Use of the Earmarked Mineral Oil Tax and Other Funds Earmarked for Road and Air Transport (MinOA), in particular from the earmarked portion of the net revenue from the excise duty levied on aviation fuels and of the surcharges levied on aviation fuels (Art. 1 para. 2 MinOA).

<sup>2</sup>

They are used in particular for:

- a. developing and increasing the production of renewable aviation fuels at home and abroad;
- b. developing and applying technologies to increase the energy efficiency of aircraft;
- c. developing and applying procedures to increase the energy efficiency of flight operations;
- d. transferring knowledge between science, industry and society in the field of reducing greenhouse gas emissions in aviation.

<sup>3</sup> The Federal Office of Civil Aviation (FOCA) may use the funds referred to in paragraph 1 for federal policy research in accordance with Article 16, paragraph 2, letters a and c of the Federal Act of 14 December 2012[^127a-5] on the Promotion of Research and Innovation.

[^127a-1]: The reference was amended on 1 May 2025 in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).
[^127a-2]: The reference was amended on 1 May 2025 in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).
[^127a-3]: [SR 748.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9033)
[^127a-4]: [SR 725.116.2](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8462)
[^127a-5]: [SR 420.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/6865)

#### Art. 127b

**Form and procedure for awarding financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> Financial assistance shall be awarded in the form of non-repayable contributions, interest-free loans or guarantees.

<sup>2</sup> The FOCA shall decide on the form of funding according to the project’s funding requirements.

<sup>3</sup> Financial assistance may be awarded within the framework of a call for proposals. A response to a call for proposals is considered an application.

<sup>4</sup> Applications should be submitted to the FOCA.

<sup>5</sup> The FOCA charges a fee for reviewing the application. The amount of the fee is based on the Ordinance of 28 September 2007[^127b-1] on the Fees charged by the Federal Office of Civil Aviation.

<sup>6</sup> The FOCA may make the awarding of financial assistance dependent on the achievement of interim targets.

[^127b-1]: [SR 748.112.11](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9040)

#### Art. 127c

**Requirements for awarding financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup>

Measures are considered according to the degree to which they meet the following criteria:

- a. significant reduction in greenhouse gas emissions;
- b. cost efficiency in relation to climate impact;
- c. low environmental impact;
- d. high market opportunities;
- e. high probability of success;
- f. high added value in Switzerland;
- g. creditability of emission reductions to Switzerland;
- h. identification of partners across the entire value chain;
- i. contribution to knowledge preservation and expansion.

<sup>2</sup> The criteria are assessed for technologies and processes including future scaling.

<sup>3</sup> Applicants must demonstrate that they have made a reasonable contribution of their own towards the implementation of the measure.

<sup>4</sup> If financial assistance is applied for in the form of an interest-free loan, the applicant must credibly demonstrate their creditworthiness.

#### Art. 127d

**Amount of financial assistance and criteria for prioritisation**

<sup>1</sup> The amount of the contributions, loans and guarantees shall be determined by the degree to which the criteria und Article 127*c* paragraphs 1–3 are met.

<sup>2</sup> If the submitted or expected applications exceed the available funds, financial assistance shall be granted taking into account the criteria set out in Article 127*c* paragraphs 1–3.

#### Art. 127e

**Guarantees**

<sup>1</sup> A guarantee may be provided for third-party loans if the lender takes the guarantee into account when setting the interest rate of the loan.

<sup>2</sup> The guarantee may secure all or part of the guaranteed loan. It may not exceed CHF 100 million and may be granted for a maximum period of 10 years.

<sup>3</sup> Guarantees shall only be provided for loans granted by a bank in accordance with the (BankA)[^127e-1] or by another appropriate lender based in Switzerland.

[^127e-1]: [SR 952.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11561)

#### Art. 127f

**Notification obligation and reporting**

<sup>1</sup> The FOCA must be informed immediately of any changes that could affect the securing of financial assistance.

<sup>2</sup>

Once the specified interim targets have been achieved and the measure implemented, an interim report or final report must be submitted to the FOCA. The reports must contain at least the following information:

- a. details of the implementation status of the measure;
- b. a cost analysis.

<sup>3</sup> The interim report and the final report must be approved by the FOCA.

<sup>4</sup>

The borrower of a guaranteed loan must report to the FOCA at least once a year on:

- a. how the loan is being used;
- b. the course of business and its expected development, providing the annual report, balance sheet, income statement and audit report within the deadlines specified in the Swiss Code of Obligations[^127f-1];
- c. any other aspects required by the FOCA for the granting of financial assistance granted.

[^127f-1]: [SR 220](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/5818)

#### Art. 127g

**Implementation**

The FOCA may engage an external body to implement the funding measures and consult a panel of experts independent of the measure.

## Chapter 9*b* Use of Revenue from Auctioning of Emission Allowances for Installations

[^chap9b-1]

[^chap9b-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 1 Measures to Avoid Damage

#### Art. 127h

**Funding eligibility**

<sup>1</sup> Financial assistance for adaptation measures (Art. 37*b* para. 1 let. a CO<sub>2</sub> Act) shall be granted for planning and implementation of measures that contribute significantly either directly or indirectly to the avoidance of measures to persons and property of considerable value.

<sup>2</sup>

In particular, funding is provided for adaptation measures to prevent:

- a. Impairment to health due to increasing heat stress;
- b. Damage to persons and property due to the consequences of thawing permafrost and melting glaciers;
- c. Damage to persons and property caused by more frequent and intense precipitation events and their consequences, such as flooding, increased surface run-off and soil movements;
- d. Damage to agriculture, forestry, the energy industry and urban water management due to more frequent and prolonged periods of drought;
- e. Impairment to ecosystem services due to changes in habitats and species composition.

<sup>3</sup> No financial assistance shall be granted for measures that do not comply with the federal government’s energy or climate policy.

<sup>4</sup> Applications for financial assistance must be submitted to the FOEN by 31 March.

<sup>5</sup> Financial assistance may be granted to develop measures in accordance with paragraph 1 within the framework of calls for proposals.

#### Art. 127i

**Amount of financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> The amount of financial assistance is awarded on the basis of the benefit and impact of the measure. It shall be a maximum of 50 per cent of the allowable costs.

<sup>2</sup> Allowable costs are the costs necessary and appropriate for the cost-effective and expedient implementation of the measure. They may not exceed the additional costs incurred by the adaptation measure.

### Section 2 Measures to Decarbonise Installations

#### Art. 127j

**Funding eligibility**

<sup>1</sup> Financial assistance for measures to decarbonise installations (Art. 37*b* para. 1 let. b CO<sub>2</sub> Act) shall be granted to operators that are required to take part in the ETS under Article 16 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act and who have not been exempted from this requirement in accordance with Article 41.

<sup>2</sup> No financial assistance shall be granted for measures that do not make a significant contribution to the decarbonisation of an installation, such as the replacement of fossil fuels with other fossil fuels or measures for the supply of district heating networks.

<sup>3</sup> If the measures are likely to lead to higher electricity consumption, electricity from non-fossil sources must be used to cover the increase in consumption and this must be documented with guarantees of origin.

<sup>4</sup> Applications for financial assistance must be submitted to the FOEN by 31 March.

<sup>5</sup> The FOEN may require verification of the information provided in the application at the operator's expense.

#### Art. 127k

**Amount of financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> Financial assistance shall not exceed 50 per cent of the allowable costs.

<sup>2</sup> Allowable costs are the investment costs directly necessary to implement the measure economically and efficiently.

#### Art. 127l

**Prioritisation criteria**

If the submitted or expected applications exceed the funds available, financial assistance shall be granted according to the following criteria in the order in which they appear:

- a. amount of financial assistance applied for per avoided tonne of CO<sub>2</sub>eq or per targeted tonne of negative emissions throughout the duration of effect;
- b. scope of the targeted reduction in greenhouse gas emissions or the targeted negative emissions in tonnes of CO<sub>2</sub>eq;
- c. risk of greenhouse gas emissions being transferred abroad;
- d. reduction in the environmental impact throughout the entire life cycle of installations and products.

### Section 3 Reporting and Payment of Financial Assistance

#### Art. 127m

**Notification obligation and reporting**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN must be informed immediately of any changes that may affect the granting of financial assistance.

<sup>2</sup>

Once the set interim targets have been reached or the measures implemented, a report must be submitted to the FOEN. This must contain as a minimum:

- a. details about the implementation status of the measures;
- b. a cost analysis with copies of invoices.

<sup>3</sup> The report must be approved by the FOCA.

<sup>4</sup>

The installation operator in the ETS must submit an evaluation report three years after the measures have been implemented. This must contain details about:

- a. the extent of the annually achieved reduction in greenhouse gas emissions or the annually achieved impact from the use of negative emissions technologies in tonnes CO<sub>2</sub>eq in the last three years;
- b. any deviations from the originally planned measures giving reasons for these and describing the corrective measures planned.

<sup>5</sup> The FOEN may require the evaluation report to be verified at the operator’s cost.

#### Art. 127n

**Payment of financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall pay out the financial assistance once it has approved the report.

<sup>2</sup> In the case of measures with interim targets, the financial assistance shall be paid out to the extent that the measure has been implemented.

#### Art. 127o

**Reclaiming financial assistance**

If the actual impact of the measure is less than 80 per cent of the impact stated in the application, the FOEN may demand a pro rata repayment of the financial assistance paid out.

#### Art. 127p

**Publication of information**

The FOEN shall publish on its website information on the measures supported, maintaining manufacturing and business confidentiality. This shall include:

- a. name and address of the recipient of financial assistance;
- b. the amount of financial assistance;
- c. the nature of the measures.

## Chapter 10 Funding and Information

[^chap10-1]

[^chap10-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### Section 1 Funding for Training and Professional Development and for Public Outreach

#### Art. 128

**Funding**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall promote the training and professional development of persons engaged in activities related to climate protection as well as platforms and other public outreach work in the field of climate protection.

<sup>2</sup>

Within the scope of authorised financial assistance, it shall provide grants to public bodies and institutions and to private organisations that in the field of climate protection:

- a. offer training and professional development; or
- b. inform and advise the public.

<sup>3</sup>

Projects worthy of support are educational and communication projects, in particular those which:

- a. demonstrate how they contribute to achieving the aims of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act;
- b. are designed to have an impact;
- c. can be multiplied.

#### Art. 128a

**Amount of financial assistance**

<sup>1</sup> A maximum of 40 per cent of the allowable costs shall be awarded in financial assistance.

<sup>2</sup> Allowable costs are those costs necessary and appropriate for the economic and purposeful implementation of the project.

### Section 2 Information

#### Art. 129

**Information provided by the FOEN**

The FOEN shall inform the public and advises the authorities, companies and private individuals particularly about:

- a. the consequences of climate change;
- b. measures for reducing greenhouse gas emissions or increasing carbon sink capacity;
- c. measures for coping with the consequences of increased greenhouse gas concentrations in the atmosphere.

#### Art. 129a

**Reporting on climate-related financial risks**

<sup>1</sup> The Swiss Financial Market Supervisory Authority (FINMA) shall report annually to the public in aggregate form on the results of its review of climate-related financial risks for supervised entities.

<sup>2</sup> The Swiss National Bank (SNB) shall report annually to the public in aggregated form on the results of its review of climate-related financial risks to the stability of the Swiss financial system.

<sup>3</sup> If FINMA or the SNB take any measures based on the results of their reviews, they shall also mention these in their annual reports.

### Section 3 Funding of Electric Drive Technologies

#### Art. 129b

**Funding recipients**

<sup>1</sup>

Financial assistance under Article 41*a* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act may be awarded to:

- a. transport companies with a licence under Article 6 of the Federal Act of 20 March 2009[^129b-1] on Passenger Transport;
- b. companies providing services on licensed routes on the basis of an operating agreement under Article 20 of the Ordinance of 4 November 2009[^129b-2] on Passenger Transport.

<sup>2</sup> Applications for funding must be submitted to the Federal Roads Office (FEDRO).

[^129b-1]: [SR 745.1](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8886)
[^129b-2]: [SR 745.11](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8887)

#### Art. 129c

**Requirements for funding**

Financial assistance shall be awarded for buses and ships if they are used for at least 75 per cent of their time in licensed transport and are owned by the funding recipient after commissioning.

#### Art. 129d

**Vehicles eligible for funding**

<sup>1</sup>

Financial assistance shall be awarded for the following buses and boats:

- a. buses that are solely battery-powered;
- b. buses with fuel cells (hydrogen);
- c. trolleybuses;
- d. new boats with electric drive systems, including fuel cells (hydrogen);
- e. boats that are being converted to an electric drive system, including fuel cells (hydrogen).

<sup>2</sup> No financial assistance shall be awarded for vehicles that replace vehicles that are already electrified or have not yet been fully depreciated.

#### Art. 129e

**Payment**

Financial assistance shall be paid out once the company has demonstrated that the vehicles have been commissioned.

#### Art. 129f

**Verification of vehicle use**

<sup>1</sup> Companies must report unsolicited the current use of vehicles to the FOT five years after initial use.

<sup>2</sup> If there is a difference in use of more than 10 per cent between jointly ordered transport and other licensed transport or greater use outside of licensed transport, the amounts received shall be repaid pro rata or in full.

## Chapter 11 Implementation

#### Art. 130

**Implementation authorities**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN shall implement this Ordinance. Paragraphs 2–9 and Annex 14 number 2.1 remain reserved.[^130-1]

<sup>2</sup> The SFOE shall implement the provisions relating to the reduction of carbon emissions from passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles. In this it is supported by FEDRO.[^130-2]

<sup>3</sup> The FOCBS shall implement the provisions relating to the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.

<sup>4</sup>

The FOEN shall implement:

- a. in consultation with the SFOE: the provisions on attestations for domestic emission reductions and increasing the carbon sink capacity and on the promotion of technologies for the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions;
- b. in consultation with the SFOE, the State Secretariat for Economic Affairs and the Federal Department of Foreign Affairs: the provisions on the attestations for emission reductions and increasing the carbon sink capacity outside Switzerland.[^130-3]

<sup>4bis</sup> The SFOE shall implement the provisions on global financial assistance with the long-term reduction of carbon emissions from buildings, on assistance with the direct use of geothermal energy, on assistance for installations producing renewable gases and on assistance for installations using solar thermal energy for process heat.[^130-4]

<sup>5</sup> In consultation with the SFOE, the FOEN shall implement provisions relating to the promotion of basic and advanced training.

<sup>6</sup> The SFOE and private organisations commissioned by the SFOE or the FOEN shall support the FOEN in implementing the provisions relating to commitments to reduce greenhouse gas emissions.

<sup>7</sup> The FOCA shall support the FOEN in implementing the provisions on emissions trading for aircraft operators.[^130-5]

<sup>8</sup> It shall enforce the obligation to provide and blend low-emission, renewable and renewable synthetic fuels in accordance with Articles 28*f* and 28*g* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act and to fund the measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions from aviation under Articles 127*a–*127*g*.[^130-6]

<sup>9</sup> FEDRO is responsible for promoting electric drive technologies in accordance with Articles 129*b–*129*f*.[^130-7]

[^130-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^130-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 1 Nov. 2017, in force since 1 Jan. 2018 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)).
[^130-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^130-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^130-5]: Inserted by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^130-6]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^130-7]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 130a

**Information and documentation systems**[^130a-1]

<sup>1</sup>

The following procedures shall be conducted electronically via the FOEN’s information and documentation systems:

- a. procedures for issuing attestations for emission reductions or increases in the carbon sink capacity (Art. 5–11);
- b. procedures for participating in the ETS (Arts 40–46*f* and Arts 50–54);
- c. procedures for the reduction obligation (Arts 66–79);
- d.[^130a-2] procedures for the granting of financial assistance for measures to prevent damage and for measures to decarbonise installations (Arts 127*h*–127*p*).

<sup>2</sup> If the information and documentation systems in individual areas are not yet set up to conduct electronic procedures, submissions must be made by post.

<sup>3</sup> In derogation from paragraph 1, the FOEN may issue orders by post.

[^130a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).
[^130a-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

#### Art. 131

**Greenhouse gas inventory**

<sup>1</sup> The FOEN maintains the greenhouse gas inventory.

<sup>2</sup>

Based on the greenhouse gas inventory, it calculates whether the reduction target under Article 3 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act has been met. The emission allowances surrendered by installation operators participating in the ETS from the European Union are taken into account if:

- a. the emissions of these installations as recorded in the Swiss ETS are higher than the total quantity of Swiss emission allowances for installations in the Swiss ETS; and
- b. the total emissions of Switzerland exceed the reduction target under Article 3 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.[^131-1]

<sup>3</sup> These emission allowances are taken into account in the domestic target to the extent of the additional emissions caused in accordance with paragraph 2 after deducting the emission-reduction certificates surrendered. The FOEN discloses this in the reporting on target achievement.[^131-2]

<sup>4</sup> The total quantity of Swiss emission allowances is calculated as the sum of the available quantity of emission allowances for installations in accordance with Article 18 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act and the emission allowances transferred in accordance with paragraph 1 of Articles 48, 48*a*, 48*b* and 48*c*, of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act minus the cancelled emission allowances in accordance with Article 19 paragraph 5 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.[^131-3]

<sup>5</sup> When the target achievement is assessed in accordance with Article 3 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act, the absolute greenhouse gas balance of the land use sector for the entire area of Switzerland is taken into account.[^131-4]

[^131-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^131-2]: Inserted by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).
[^131-3]: Inserted by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^131-4]: Inserted by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 132

**Remuneration for enforcement**[^132-1]

<sup>1</sup> Remuneration for the costs of enforcement by the Federal Administration shall be maximum CHF 23.4 million and shall be paid from the revenue from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.

<sup>2</sup> The FOEN shall determine the costs of enforcement each year for the purposes of remuneration.

[^132-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

#### Art. 133

**Controls and disclosure obligations**

<sup>1</sup> Implementation authorities may at any time carry out controls without prior notification, particularly of ETS participants, installation operators with reduction obligations, CHP plant operators, companies and persons obliged to pay the CO2 levy and persons that have applied for a refund of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy.[^133-1]

<sup>2</sup>

The implementation authorities must on request:

- a. be given all information required for implementation of this Ordinance;
- b. be provided with all books, business papers, electronic data and documents required for implementation of this Ordinance.

[^133-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 134

**Data processing**

<sup>1</sup>

The data collected for implementation of this Ordinance, including personal data, shall be made available to the implementation authorities concerned if required for implementation. In particular:[^134-1]

- a.[^134-2] the FOCBS shall provide FEDRO and the SFOE with the import data required to implement Chapter 3 of this Ordinance, and FEDRO shall provide the SFOE with the data additionally required to implement Chapter 3;
- b.[^134-3]

  the FOEN shall provide the SFOE, the Federal Department of Foreign Affairs and the State Secretariat for Economic Affairs with the data required to assess:

  - 1\. project outlines (Art. 6 para. 4) and applications for issuing attestations (Art. 7),
  - 2\. applications to determine a reduction obligation, and
  - 3\. monitoring reports (Arts 9 and 91);

- c.[^134-4]

  the FOCBS shall provide the FOEN with the data required to assess:

  - 1\. the fulfilment of an offsetting obligation for motor fuels,
  - 2\. monitoring reports (Arts 9, 52, 72 and 91), and
  - 3\. applications for issuing attestations (Arts 7, 12 and 12*a*);

- [tab] d.[^134-5]the FOEN shall provide the FOCBS with the data required to refund the CO<sub>2</sub> levy;
- e.[^134-6]

  the FOCA shall provide the FOEN with the data required to assess:

  - 1\. the obligation to participate (Art. 46*d*),
  - 2\. monitoring plans (Art. 51), and
  - 3\. monitoring reports (Art. 52);

- f.[^134-7]

  the SFOE shall provide the FOEN with the data required to assess:

  - 1\. monitoring reports (Arts 52 and 72), and
  - 2\.[^134-8] target agreements (Art. 46 para. 1<sup>bis</sup>, 67 and 68).

<sup>2</sup> The FOCBS and the General Directorate of Customs and the Swiss Organisation for Compulsory Stockpiling of Oil Products (Carbura) may exchange data in order to implement provisions relating to the offsetting carbon emissions from motor fuels.[^134-9]

<sup>3</sup> The FOEN shall pass on personal data that it no longer permanently requires to the Swiss Federal Archives for storage in accordance with the Archiving Act of 26 June 1998[^134-10] Data deemed not worthy of archiving by the Swiss Federal Archives shall be destroyed.[^134-11]

[^134-1]: Amended by No I of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).
[^134-2]: Amended by No I of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).
[^134-3]: Amended by No I of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).
[^134-4]: Amended by No I of the O of 8 Oct. 2014, in force since 1 Dec. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)).
[^134-5]: Amended by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 134a

**Coordination with the European Union**[^134a-1]

The FOEN shall support the European Commission in accordance with Article 11 of the ETS Agreement[^134a-2]. In particular it shall transmit to it the information required for this purpose.

[^134a-1]: Inserted by No I of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

#### Art. 135

**Amendments to the Annexes**

DETEC shall amend:

- a. Annex 2: in accordance with the criteria of Article 6 paragraph 2 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act;
- b. Annex 3: in accordance with technical and economic development;
- b<sup>bis</sup>.[^135-1] Annex 3*a*: in accordance with technical and economic development;
- b<sup>ter</sup>.[^135-2] Annex 3*b*: in accordance with technical and economic development;
- c.[^135-3] Annex 4*a* number 2: for determining each year the average unladen weight of passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles registered for the first time in the previous calendar year;
- c<sup>bis</sup>.[^135-4] Annex 5: for determining each year the amounts in accordance with Article 13 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act;
- c<sup>ter</sup>.[^135-5] Annex 6: if the installation categories change based on comparable international regulations;
- d.[^135-6] …
- d<sup>bis</sup>.[^135-7] Annex 9 number 1: if Implementing Regulation (EU) 2021/447[^135-8] is amended or replaced;
- d<sup>ter</sup>.[^135-9] Annex 9 number 3: if Delegated Decision 2019/708/EU[^135-10] or Regulation (EU) 2023/956[^135-11] is amended or replaced;
- e. Annex 11: corresponding to increases in the rate of the levy (Art. 94 para. 1);
- f.[^135-12] Annex 14: if Regulation (EC) No 748/2009[^135-13] is amended.

#### Art. 135a

**Approval of decisions of minor importance**[^135a-1]

DETEC may approve technical and administrative decisions of minor importance of the ETS Agreement’s[^135a-2] Joint Committee.

## Chapter 12 Final Provisions

### Section 1 Repeal and Amendment of Current Legislation

#### Art. 136

**Repeal of current legislation**

The following Ordinances are repealed:

- 1\. CO<sub>2</sub> Crediting Ordinance of 22 June 2005[^136-1];
- 2\. CO<sub>2</sub> Ordinance of 8 June 2007[^136-2];
- 3\. DETEC Ordinance of 27 September 2007[^136-3] on the National Emissions Trading Registry;
- 4\. CO<sub>2</sub> Offsetting Ordinance of 24 November 2010[^136-4];
- 5\. Ordinance of 16 December 2011[^136-5] on the Reduction of Carbon Emissions from Passenger Cars.

#### Art. 137

**Amendment of current legislation**

…[^137-1]
