Annex 2[^t-2]

(Art. 146*l* let. b)

## Emission reductions achieved abroad not taken into account

[^t-2]: Revised by No II of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)), No II para. 1 of the O of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)) and No II para. 3 of the O of 4 May 2022, in force since 1 June 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)).

\1. The following emission reductions are not taken into account:

- a. certificates for emission reductions that were not achieved in one of the least developed countries (Least Developed Countries, LDC) on the list of the United Nations;
- b. certificates for emission reductions that were achieved from projects for biological carbon sequestration or geological carbon capture and sequestration;
- c. certificates for emission reductions that were achieved through using hydro power plants with an installed production capacity of over 20MW;
- d. other certificates for emission reductions that were not achieved through using renewable energy, the end user’s improved energy efficiency, methane flaring and avoidance of methane emissions at landfills, municipal waste recycling or waste incineration plants, recycling of agricultural waste, wastewater treatment or through composting;
- e. emission reduction certificates already used;
- f. certificates for emission reductions that were achieved from 1 January 2021 onwards.

\2. In addition, emission reduction certificates are not taken into account if:

- a. the emission reductions were achieved in violation of human rights;
- b. the emission reductions were achieved under conditions that had significant negative social or ecological effects;
- c. their counting would contravene Swiss foreign and development policy.

\3. Number 1 letter a does not apply to:

- a. emission reduction certificates from projects that have been registered before 1 January 2013 in accordance with Article 12 of the Kyoto Protocol of 11 December 1997[^annex2-lvlu1-1];
- b. emission reduction certificates for emission reductions achieved before 1 January 2013 from projects in accordance with Article 6 of the Kyoto Protocol of 11 December 1997.

[^annex2-lvlu1-1]: [SR 0.814.011](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/3476)

Annex 2a[^t-3]

(Art. 5 para 1 let. a)

## Domestic emission reductions and increases in the carbon sink capacity outside Switzerland for which no attestations are issued

[^t-3]: Inserted by No II para. 1 of the O of 4 May 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 29 Sept. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)) and of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

\1. No international attestations shall be issued for a foreign emission-reduction project or programme if the emission reductions or the increase in the carbon sink capacity have been achieved by:

- a. investing in using fossil thermal or motor fuels for energy production or in the extraction of fossil energy sources; the foregoing does not apply to investments that contribute to an improvement in energy efficiency, without changing anything in the system for using fossil fuels to generate energy;
- b. using nuclear energy;
- c. using hydropower plants with an installed production capacity of over 20MW;
- d. running projects in large industrial plants that do not correspond to the state of the art available on the global market;
- e. conducting activities in the waste sector without material or energy use or reduction of waste;
- f. biological CO<sub>2</sub> sequestration projects;
- g. reducing deforestation;
- h. degrading forests;
- i. not extracting fossil fuels;
- j. conducting activities that conflict with environment and human rights conventions that Switzerland has ratified;
- k. conducting activities that have considerable negative social or ecological effects;
- l. conducting activities that conflict with the concerns of Switzerland’s foreign or development policy;
- m. using biochar, except for use in building materials, provided that sustainable production of biochar and environmentally sound treatment of construction waste are ensured.

\2. For activities in the waste sector with delayed use of the waste as a source of energy, 55 per cent of the emission reductions are only certified when the waste is actually used as a source of energy.

\3. No international attestations shall be issued for a project or programme abroad unless the affected interest groups are consulted.

Annex 3[^t-4]

(Art. 5 para. 1 let. a)

## Emission reductions and increases in the carbon sink capacity in Switzerland for which no attestations are issued

[^t-4]: Amended by No II para. 2 of the O of 4 May 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

No attestations are issued for a domestic emission-reduction project or programme if the emission reductions or increases in the carbon sink capacity have been achieved by:

- a. using nuclear energy;
- b. research and technologies at an early developmental stage or information and consultation;
- c using biogenic renewable thermal and motor fuels for which there is no guarantee of origin in the guarantee of origin system for thermal and motor fuels;
- d. replacing fossil energy sources with fossil energy sources (e.g. in boilers, vehicles and hybrid vehicles);
- e. ...
- f. using electricity as a fuel substitute for process heating, with the exception of its use in heat pumps or if the electricity used can be shown to originate from renewable sources;
- g. under-use or avoiding use;
- h.

  using biochar, with the exception of its use as:

  - 1\. fertiliser, provided the biochar used meets the requirements of the Fertilisers Ordinance of 1 November 2023[^annex3-lvlu1-1] at the time the application is received and the maximum annual application rate,
  - 2\. construction material, provided that it can be ensured that the biochar was produced sustainably;

- i. using ad- and absorption technologies to produce cold or heat, with the exception of the decentralised use of sufficiently available waste heat in accordance with Article 2 letter e of the Energy Promotion Ordinance of 1 November 2017[^annex3-lvlu1-2].

[^annex3-lvlu1-1]: [SR 916.171](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/10645)
[^annex3-lvlu1-2]: [SR 730.03](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/8507)

Annex 3a[^t-5]

(Art. 6 let. 3)

## Requirements for the calculation of emission reductions and the monitoring concept for projects and programmes in connection with local heating networks

[^t-5]: Inserted by No II para. 1 of the O of 21 Sept. 2018 ([AS 2018 3477](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2018/566)). Amended by No II para. 2 of the O of 29 Sept. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)). Revised by correction of 10 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 640](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/640)), No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248), [267](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/267)) and No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### 1 Scope of application

This Annex applies to projects and programmes if they involve:

- a. constructing a new local heating network with one or more carbon-neutral heat sources;
- b. expanding or densifying an existing local heating network with predominantly carbon-neutral heat sources;
- c. replacing one or more centralised fossil heat sources in an existing local heating network with one or more predominantly carbon-neutral heat sources.

### 2 Definitions

In this Annex:

- a. *local heating network* means a network to distribute heat with centralised heat sources and decentralised consumers;
- b. *new consumers* means heat consumers who connect to a new or existing local heating network after the start of project implementation (Art. 5 para. 3);
- c. *existing consumers* means heat consumers that were already connected to an existing local heating network before the start of implementation of the project;
- d. *new buildings* means buildings that are under construction at the time of connection to the local heating network and which are not existing consumers.

### 3 Requirements for the calculation of emission reductions

### 3.1 Metrological requirements

In order to calculate the emission reductions of projects and programmes, the following in particular shall be measured:

- a. the consumption of all centralised fossil heat sources;
- b. the electricity consumption of the heat pumps;
- c.

  the quantities of heat used by all heat consumers, whereby the following quantities of heat must be shown separately:

  - – quantities of heat that are distributed to new buildings,
  - – quantities of heat that are distributed to installation operators exempted from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy under Article 96 paragraph 2.

### 3.2 System boundaries

The system boundaries for the project or programme must encompass:

- a. the centralised heat sources;
- b. the network for distributing heat;
- c. the heat consumers;
- d. the incoming energy flows;
- e. the emissions resulting from the project or programme.

### 3.3 Reference scenario

\1. The description of the project or programme must include at least two plausible alternative scenarios for the project or the programme.

\2. The scenarios shall be calculated for a maximum of 20 years.

\3. The probability of the individual scenarios occurring must be indicated together with the most probable scenario. This applies as the reference scenario.

### 3.4 Calculation of the reference emissions

The total annual emissions in the reference scenario are calculated as follows:

*RE**<sub>y</sub>* *= (RE**<sub>new,y</sub>* *+ RE**<sub>existing,y</sub>* *+ RE*<sub>ETS,y</sub>*)* (1)

whereby:

- RE<sub>y</sub> are the reference scenario emissions in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]
- RE<sub>new,y</sub> are the reference scenario emissions from new consumers in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq], see equation (2)
- RE<sub>existing,y</sub> are the reference scenario emissions from existing consumers in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq], see equation (3)
- RE<sub>ETS,y</sub> is the parameter used to prevent emissions here and in the emissions trading system being counted twice; this parameter must be set at 0.
- If the project draws heat from a heat source located within the perimeter of an installation whose operator participates in the emissions trading system, the parameter has the value of the emission allowances allocated for this heat in year y [tCO2eq]; this value is determined at the time the application is made to assess whether the project qualifies and is only changed during the crediting period if there are changes in the emissions trading system that make an adjustment necessary.

The terms are calculated as follows:

*RE**<sub>new,y</sub>* *= ∑**<sub>i</sub>* *W**<sub>new,i,y</sub>* *\* EF**<sub>WV</sub>*<sub>, y, z</sub> (2)

whereby:

- W<sub>new,i,y</sub> is the expected heat supply to new consumers in year y [MWh]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.2.
- i

  are all new consumers with the exception of:

  - – new buildings,
  - – buildings that were already heated by a carbon-neutral system before connection to the local heating network, and
  - – installations whose operators are exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy under Article 96 paragraph 2.

- EF<sub>WV, y, z</sub> is the standard emission factor for the local heating network in year y, calculated as follows:
- 5 &gt; y – z: 0.198 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh;
- 5 ≤ y – z &lt; 9: 0.154 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh;
- 9 ≤ y – z &lt; 14: 0.116 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh;
- 14 ≤ y – z &lt; 20: 0.081 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh;
- z Calendar year in which the implementation of the project commenced in accordance with Article 5 paragraph 3

*RE**<sub>existing,y</sub>* *= ∑**<sub>k</sub>* *W**<sub>existing,k,y</sub>*\* *EF**<sub>existing</sub>* \* *RF**<sub>existing, v,y</sub>* \**1/(1–WVN)* (3)

whereby:

- <sub>k</sub> are all existing consumers with the exception of installations whose operators are exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy under Article 96 paragraph 2;
- W<sub>existing,k,y</sub> are the expected heat supplies to existing consumers in year y [MWh]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.2.
- EF<sub>existing</sub>
- RF<sub>existing,y,v</sub>

  is the reference factor for year y; this is:

  - 100% when y – v &lt; 20
  - 80% when y – v ≥ 20 and &lt; 24
  - 60% when y – v ≥ 24 and &lt; 29
  - 40% when y – v ≥ 29

- [tab]

  whereby:

  - y is the year for which the emission reductions are calculated;
  - v is the year in which the oldest fossil-fuel heat source to be replaced was installed.

- WVN is the standard deduction for heat losses from the heat distribution network, amounting to 10%.

### 3.5 Calculation of the project or programme emissions

The annual emissions for the project and the annual emissions for each project in a programme are calculated as follows:

*PE**<sub>y</sub>* *= EF**<sub>heating oil</sub>* *\* M**<sub>heating oil,y</sub>* <sub>/ 1000</sub> *+ EF**<sub>gas</sub>* *\* M**<sub>gas,y</sub>* *+ EF**<sub>electricity</sub>* *\* M**<sub>electricity,y</sub>* *+ PE**<sub>ETS,y</sub>* (4)

whereby:

- PE<sub>y</sub> are the expected emissions from the project in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]
- M<sub>heating oil,y</sub> is the expected quantity of heating oil burned to operate the centralised heat source or sources in year y [l]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.4.
- M<sub>gas,y</sub> is the expected quantity of gas burned to operate the centralised heat source or sources in year y [Nm<sup>3</sup> or MWh]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.5.
- M<sub>electricity,y</sub> is the expected quantity of electrical energy used to operate centralised heat pumps in year y [kWh]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.6.
- EF<sub>gas</sub> is the emission factor for natural gas in accordance with Annex 10 in tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/Nm<sup>3</sup> or in tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh depending on which unit is used for M<sub>gas</sub>. The factor 0.0036 TJ/MWh must be used to convert the tCO<sub>2</sub>/TJ unit into the tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/MWh unit.
- EF<sub>heating oil</sub> is the emission factor for heating oil; this amounts to 2.65 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/1000 l.
- [tab] PE<sub>ETS,y</sub>is the parameter used to prevent emissions here and in the emissions trading system being counted twice; this parameter must be set at 0.
- If the local heating network draws heat from a heat source located within the perimeter of an installation whose operator participates in the emissions trading system, the parameter has the value of the emission allowances for this heat allocated in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]; this value shall be specified each year in the monitoring report; it corresponds to the value of the emission allowances issued to the operator of the installations in the emissions trading system.
- EF<sub>electricity</sub> is the emission factor for electricity; it amounts to 29.6 \* 10–6 tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/kWh.

### 3.6 Calculation of annual emission reductions

The annual emission reductions for projects are calculated as follows:

- *ER**<sub>y</sub>* *= RE**<sub>y</sub>* *– PE**<sub>y</sub>* (5)

whereby:

- ER<sub>y</sub> are the emission reductions in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]
- RE<sub>y</sub> are the reference scenario emissions in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]
- PE<sub>y</sub> are the project emissions of the local heating network in year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq]

### 4 Requirements for the monitoring concept

### 4.1 List of heat consumers with documented heat supplies

\1. A list of all consumers must be included with the monitoring report. The list must contain the following information:

- a. names and addresses of the consumers;
- b. year in which the consumers were connected to the local heating network;
- c. quantity of heat in MWh supplied to the consumers in the monitoring period, broken down by calendar year; the quantity shall be calculated in accordance with number 2.4.

\2. If the consumers are installation operators that are exempt from the CO<sub>2</sub> levy under Article 96 paragraph 2, the list must contain the reference scenario emissions in tCO<sub>2</sub>eq in addition to the information in number 1. The emissions shall be calculated in accordance with number 3.4.

### 4.2 Measuring the quantity of heat supplied to consumers

When measuring the heat supplied (W<sub>new,l,y</sub>) (W<sub>existing,l,y</sub>) the following requirements must be met:

- a. the heat supplied to the consumer *l* in year *y* must be measured;
- b. a heat meter must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in kilowatt hours (kWh) or megawatt hours (MWh);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously;
- e. quality assurance must meet the requirements of the Measuring Instruments Ordinance of 15 February 2006[^annex3a-lvlu1-lvl4-lvl42-1] (MIO) and the related implementing provisions issued by the Federal Department of Justice and Police (FDJP); and
- f. the measurement must be made at the point at which the heat is transferred to the consumer.

[^annex3a-lvlu1-lvl4-lvl42-1]: [SR 941.210](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11180)

### 4.3 Age of the centralised fossil heat source that is to be replaced

When determining the reference factor, the year of manufacture or of installation of the replaced or extended fossil-fuelled boiler must be taken into account.

### 4.4 Measuring the quantity of heating oil

When measuring the quantity of heating oil (M<sub>heating oil,y</sub>) all of the following requirements must be met:

- a. the quantity of heating oil burned to operate the centralised fossil heat source or heat sources in year y must be measured;
- b. a heating oil meter or a heating oil stock balance must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in litres (l);
- d. the measurement must be made for each monitoring period or, if this is longer than a calendar year, for each calendar year;
- e. quality assurance is achieved by calibrating the fuel oil meter, otherwise a plausibility check must be carried out using alternative data sources.

### 4.5 Measuring the quantity of gas

When measuring the quantity of gas (M<sub>gas,y</sub>) all of the following requirements must be met:

- a. the quantity of gas burned to operate the centralised fossil heat source or heat sources in year y must be measured;
- b. a gas meter must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in standard cubic metres (Nm<sup>3</sup>) or megawatt hours (MWh);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously;
- e. quality assurance must meet the requirements of the MIO and the related implementing provisions issued by the FDJP.

### 4.6 Measuring the quantity of electrical energy

When measuring the quantity of electrical energy (M<sub>El,y</sub>), all the following requirements must be met:

- a. the measured quantity of electrical energy to operate heat pumps in the heating system in year y must be measured;
- b. an electricity meter must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in kilowatt hours (kWh) or megawatt hours (MWh);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously;
- e. quality assurance must meet the requirements of the MIO and the related implementing provisions issued by the FDJP.

### 4.7 Anticipated project emissions: Preventing double-counting with the emissions trading system (PE<sub>ET,y</sub>)

\1. If a project draws heat from a heat source that is located within the perimeter of an installation whose operator participates in the emissions trading system, the value of this parameter is equivalent to the emission allowances for this heat allocated in the year y [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq].

\2. This value shall be specified each year in the monitoring report as the emission allowances issued to the operator of the installations in the emissions trading system.

Annex 3b[^t-6]

(Art. 6 para. 3[^t-7])

## Requirements for calculating the reduction in emissions and for the monitoring concept for landfill gas projects and programmes

[^t-6]: Inserted by No II para. 1 of the O of 21 Sept. 2018 ([AS 2018 3477](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2018/566)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)). Correction of 10 Nov. 2023 ([AS 2023 640](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/640)).
[^t-7]: The reference was amended in application of Art. 12 para. 2 of the Publications Act of 18 June 2004 ([SR 170.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/4975)).

### 1 Scope of application

The requirements of this Annex apply to landfill gas projects and programmes provided:

- a. these involve landfills or former landfills that produce methane emissions without the planned lean gas treatment and that contain a sufficiently high proportion of organic waste;
- b. the planned lean gas treatment is not already required by law or by official order; and
- c. the planned lean gas treatment corresponds as a minimum to the state of the art and has been optimised according to the current and future composition of the landfill gas.

### 2 Definitions

In this Annex:

- a. *flare efficiency (FE)* means the percentage of methane effectively burned during flaring or generally oxidised in gas treatment processes;
- b. *aerobic degradation* means the microbial degradation of organic matter under aerobic conditions;
- c. *anaerobic degradation* means the microbial degradation of organic matter under anaerobic conditions;
- d. *landfill* means a waste disposal facility in which waste is deposited under controlled conditions;
- e. *landfill gas* means gas formed by the biological conversion of organic matter contained in landfills;
- f. *intermittent flaring* means gas formed by the biological conversion of organic matter contained in landfills;
- g. *oxidation factor (OX)* means the proportion of methane in the landfill gas that oxidises in the boundary layer before escaping into the atmosphere;
- h. *suction efficiency (SE)* means the proportion of landfill gases treated by a degassing system;
- i. *lean gas treatment* means a process for oxidising landfill gas with a methane concentration of less than 25 vol.%. Oxidation may occur in a flare or in another technical device;
- j. *existing degassing systems* means collection systems for landfill gas used for feeding the lean gas treatment process which existed before implementation begins under Article 5 paragraph 2;
- k. *new degassing systems* means collection systems for as yet uncollected landfill gas used for feeding the lean gas treatment process which are installed after implementation begins under Article 5 paragraph 2.

### 3 Requirements for calculating emission reductions

### 3.1 System boundaries

\1. The system boundaries of the project or programme must encompass the landfill and the fossil-fuel emissions from the lean gas treatment.

\2. The supply routes for the deposited waste must lie outside the system boundary.

### 3.2 Determining an oxidation factor

The following decision tree must be used to determine the value of the oxidation factor parameter (OX) required to calculate the emission reductions:

![](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/filestore/fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/cc/2012/856/20260101/en/xml/image/image1.png)

### 3.3 Calculation of methane reduction

Methane reduction can be calculated *ex ante* based on measurement data from the previous one to three years or calculated using the following formula:

*ER**<sub>ex-ante,y,flare</sub>* *= (AE – OX) \* SE \* FOD**<sub>CH4,y</sub>* *\* GWP**<sub>CH4</sub>* *– PE**<sub>y</sub>* (1)

whereby:

- ER<sub>ex-ante,y,flare</sub> are the estimated emission reductions from lean gas treatment in year y (tCO<sub>2</sub>eq);
- GWP<sub>CH4</sub> is the greenhouse gas potential of methane in accordance with Annex 1;
- AE is the flare efficiency;
- OX is the oxidation factor;
- SE is the suction efficiency;
- FOD<sub>CH4,y</sub> is the quantity of methane calculated using a ‘first order decay’ formula that is produced by the landfill in year y (t CH<sub>4</sub>); see equation (2);
- PE<sub>y</sub> are the project emissions in year y.

*FOD**<sub>CH4,y</sub>* *= (16/12)\*F\*DOC**<sub>f</sub>* *\* ∑**<sub>x</sub>* *∑**<sub>j</sub>* *A**<sub>j,x</sub>**\*DOC**<sub>j</sub>**\*Exp(-k**<sub>j</sub>**(y–x))\*(1–Exp(-k**<sub>j</sub>**))* (2)

whereby:

- y is the year for which the methane emissions are calculated;
- x is the year in which the landfill was filled with a specific quantity of waste Aj,x of category j, which falls between OY and y;
- 16/12 is the molecular weight ratio of CH<sub>4</sub> to C;
- F= 0.5 percentage of methane in the methane/carbon dioxide mixture in the landfill gas;
- DOC<sub>f</sub> is the percentage of biodegradable carbon that is degraded under anaerobic conditions (% by mass);
- A<sub>j,x</sub> is the quantity of waste in waste category j that was deposited in year x (t waste);
- OY is the first year in which waste was deposited (the year the landfill was opened);
- j is the waste category;
- DOC<sub>j</sub> is the percentage of degradable organic carbon in the relevant waste category (t C / t waste);
- k<sub>j</sub> is the degradation constant for the relevant waste category j (l/year).

### 3.4 Ex-post calculation of the reduction in methane

For new and existing degassing systems, the reduction in methane is calculated *ex post* as follows:

*ER**<sub>ex-post,y,flare</sub>* *= (AE – OX) \* GWP**<sub>CH4</sub>* *\* V**<sub>DG,y</sub>* *\* c**<sub>CH4</sub>* *\* D**<sub>CH4</sub>* *– PE**<sub>y</sub>* (3)

whereby:

- ER<sub>ex-post,y,flare</sub> are the eligible emission reductions determined ex post with the aid of the emissions measured during the lean gas treatment in year y (tCO<sub>2</sub>eq);
- AE is the flare efficiency;
- OX is the oxidation factor;
- GWP<sub>CH4</sub> is the greenhouse gas potential of methane in accordance with Annex 1;
- V<sub>DG,y</sub> is the volume flow of landfill gas that is measured at the entry to the lean gas treatment process in year y (Nm<sup>3</sup>); this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4;
- c<sub>CH4</sub> is the methane content in the landfill gas (% by volume); this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4;
- D<sub>CH4</sub> is the methane density under standard conditions (0.0007202 tCH<sub>4</sub>/Nm<sup>3</sup>);
- PE<sub>y</sub> are the project emissions in year y.

### 3.5 Calculation of the project emissions

The project emissions from operating the lean gas treatment process are calculated as follows based on the energy sources used:

*PE**<sub>y</sub>* *= EF**<sub>Gas</sub>* *\* M**<sub>gas,y</sub>* (4)

whereby:

- EF<sub>gas</sub> is the emission factor for the gas used [tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/Nm<sup>3</sup>]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.
- M<sub>gas,y</sub> is the expected quantity of gas burned in year y [Nm<sup>3</sup>]; this parameter is replaced in the monitoring by the value measured in accordance with number 4.

### 4 Requirements for the monitoring concept

\1. For projects and programmes in accordance with this Annex, the monitoring report must be accompanied by the measurement results and documents listed in numbers 4.1–4.6.

\2. The calculation of the emission reductions must be substantiated by the measurements.

### 4.1 Flare efficiency

The flare efficiency (FE) value must be determined in the monitoring report as follows:

- a. the percentage of methane that is effectively burned during flaring or generally oxidised in gas treatment processes must be stated;
- b.

  the following procedure applies:

  - 1\. A value of 90% must be used as the standard value for the combustion efficiency of a closed flare.
  - 2\. Applicants may also use the manufacturer’s specifications, provided it can be shown that these have been complied with.
  - 3\. Applicants may make their own measurements.

- c. flare efficiency must be stated as a percentage (%);
- d. its value must be determined each year.

### 4.2 Volume flow of landfill gases

When determining the volume flow (V<sub>DG,y</sub>) all of the following requirements must be met:

- a. the volume flow of the landfill gases must be determined;
- b. measuring devices for determining volume flow must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in standard cubic metres (Nm<sup>3</sup>);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously;
- e. the form and regularity for calibrating the measuring devices must be specified in the first monitoring report.

### 4.3 Methane content of landfill gases

When measuring the methane content (c<sub>CH4</sub>) all of the following requirements must be met:

- a. the methane content in the landfill gas must be measured;
- b. a methane measuring sensor must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded as per cent by volume (Vol-%);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously;
- e. the form and duration for calibrating the measuring devices must be specified in the first monitoring report.

### 4.4 Newly installed degassing systems

A clear explanation must be provided of how the collection system has been changed and what degassing systems in accordance with number 2 letter k are designated as new degassing systems.

### 4.5 Emission factor for gas

When determining the emission factor for the gas used (EF<sub>gas</sub>), all the following requirements must be met:

- a. the Swiss greenhouse gas inventory or a comparable publication must be used as the data source. For liquid gas (butane, propane), Annex 10 must be used;
- b. the factor must be recorded in tonnes of carbon dioxide equivalent per standard cubic metre (tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/Nm<sup>3</sup>) or in the case of liquid gas (butane, propane) in tonnes of carbon dioxide equivalent per tonne (tCO<sub>2</sub>eq/t).

### 4.6 Quantity of gas

When determining the quantity of gas (M<sub>gas,y</sub>), all of the following requirements must be met:

- a. the quantity of gas burned for the lean gas treatment in year y must be determined;
- b. volume flow measuring devices or the delivery documents for gas cylinders must be used as the data source;
- c. the measurement must be recorded in standard cubic metres (Nm<sup>3</sup>) or by indicating the number of gas cylinders delivered, together with their content (l);
- d. the measurement must be made continuously or when each delivery of gas cylinders is made;
- e. quality assurance must be based on the manufacturer’s specifications.

Annex 4[^t-8]

Art. 25 para. 2

## Calculation of carbon emissions from vehicles lacking the information listed in Article 25 paragraph 2

[^t-8]: Amended by No I para. 1 of the O of 20 Nov. 2024, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2024 705](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2024/705)).

### 1 Key

-

  The following abbreviations are used in the formulas below:

  - CO<sub>2</sub>: Carbon emissions (combined) in g/km
  - m: unladen weight of the vehicle in kg
  - p: maximum engine power in kW

### 2 Calculation of carbon emissions from cars

- 2\.1

  Petrol engine and manual transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.050 m + 0.371 p + 37.751

- 2\.2

  Petrol engine and automatic transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.077 m + 0.226 p + 14.107

- 2\.3

  Petrol engine and hybrid electric drive system:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.025 m + 0.392 p + 53.679

- 2\.4

  Diesel engine and manual transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.086 m + 0.160 p – 19.698

- 2\.5

  Diesel engine and automatic transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.093 m + 0.089 p – 21.938

- 2\.6

  Diesel engine and hybrid-electrical-drive system:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.072 m + 0.170 p – 18.692

- 2\.7

  Plug-in-hybrid-electrical-drive system:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = – 0.025 m + 0.205 p + 56.308

- 2\.8 The carbon emissions of cars with internal combustion engines that are powered neither by petrol nor diesel are calculated using the corresponding equations for petrol-powered vehicles, depending on the transmission system used.
- 2\.9 In the case of solely electric cars and cars with a fuel-cell drive system, a carbon emission value of 0 g/km applies.

### 3 Calculation of the carbon emissions of vans and light articulated vehicles

- 3\.1

  Diesel engine and manual transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.133 m + 0.512 p – 113.494

- 3\.2

  Diesel engine and automatic transmission:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.133 m – 61.014

- 3\.3

  Petrol engine:

  - [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> = 0.017 m + 0.954 p + 61.697

- 3\.4 The carbon emissions of vans and light articulated vehicles not covered by numbers 3.1, 3.2 or 3.3 are calculated using the corresponding equations for cars in accordance with number 2.

### 4 Rounding of the carbon emissions

-

  The carbon emissions are rounded to the first digit following the decimal point as follows:

  - a. If the second digit following the decimal point is 4 or less, then the number is rounded down.
  - b. If the second digit following the decimal point is 5 or greater, then the number is rounded up.

Annex 4a[^t-9]

(Art. 28)

## Calculation of the individual target

[^t-9]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Amended by No II para. 2 of the O of 2 April 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)). Revised by No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### 1 Calculation of the individual target for passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles

- 1\.1 For small importers, the individual carbon emissions target is calculated individually for each vehicle using the following formula and rounded to three decimal places:
- Individual target for the vehicle: *z + a \* (m – M**<sub>t-2</sub>**) g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/km*
- 1\.2 For large importers, the individual target for the average carbon emissions is calculated individually for each fleet of new vehicles using the following formula and rounded to three decimal places:
- Individual target for the new vehicle fleet: *z + a \* (M**<sub>i,t</sub>* *– M**<sub>t-2</sub>**) g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/km*
- 1\.3

  The following parameters apply for the formulae in Nos 1.1 and 1.2:

  - z target value for carbon emissions in accordance with Article 10 paragraph 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act:
  - for passenger cars: 93.6 g CO<sub>2</sub>/km in the years 2025–2029; 49.5 g CO<sub>2</sub>/km from 2030
  - for vans and light articulated vehicles: 153.9 g CO<sub>2</sub>/km in the years 2025–2029; 90.6 g CO<sub>2</sub>/km from 2030
  - a slope of the target value line:
  - for passenger cars: –0.0144 in the years 2025–2029; –0.0076 ab 2030
  -

    for vans and light articulated vehicles:

    - In the years 2025–2029: 0.1064 for vehicles or vehicle fleets with an unladen weight of more than M<sub>t–2</sub>; 0.0848 for those with an unladen weight less than or equal to M<sub>t–2</sub>
    - From 2030: 0.1064 for vehicles or vehicle fleets with an unladen weight of more than M<sub>t–2</sub>; 0.0499 for those with an unladen weight less than or equal to M<sub>t–2</sub>

  - m unladen weight of passenger cars or, where applicable, of vans or of light articulated vehicles in kg
  - M<sub>i,t</sub> average unladen weight of the importer’s passenger cars registered for the first time in the reference year or, where applicable, vans or light articulated vehicles of the large importers in kg, rounded to three decimal places
  - M<sub>t-2</sub> average unladen weight of the passenger cars registered for the first time in Switzerland in the penultimate calendar year before the reference year or, where applicable, vans or light articulated vehicles in kg.

### 2 Average unladen weight

### 2.1 Passenger cars

-

  The average unladen weight of passenger cars registered for the first time in the following years was:

  - a. 2015: 1,532 kg;
  - b. 2016: 1,563 kg;
  - c. 2017: 1,588 kg;
  - d. 2018: 1,601 kg;
  - e. 2019: 1,636 kg;
  - f. 2020: 1,674 kg;
  - g. 2021: 1693 kg;
  - h. 2022: 1,727 kg;
  - i. 2023: 1,767 kg;
  - j. 2024: 1,777 kg.

### 2.2 Vans and light articulated vehicles

-

  The average unladen weight of vans and light articulated vehicles registered for the first time in the following years was:

  - a. 2018: 2,056 kg;
  - b. 2019: 2,067 kg;
  - c. 2020: 2,089 kg;
  - d. 2021: 2,094 kg;
  - e. 2022: 2,117 kg;
  - f. 2023: 2,110 kg;
  - g. 2024: 2,130 kg.

### 3 Calculation of individual targets for heavy-duty vehicles

- 3\.1 The individual carbon emissions target for small importers is calculated on the basis of the following formula for each vehicle and is rounded to three decimal places:
- Individual target for the vehicle in grams of CO<sub>2</sub> per tonne kilometre: *MPW**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* (1–rf) \* AWCO**<sub>2sg</sub>* *g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/tkm*
- 3\.2 The individual carbon emissions target for large importers is calculated on the basis of the following formula for each new vehicle fleet and is rounded to three decimal places:
- Individual target for the vehicle in grams of CO<sub>2</sub> per tonne kilometre: *∑**<sub>sg</sub>* *Ant**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* MPW**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* (1–rf) \* AWCO**<sub>2sg</sub>* *g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/tkm*
- 3\.3

  The following parameters apply for the formulae in Nos 3.1 and 3.2:

  - Sh<sub>sg</sub> Share of the sub-groups in the importer’s new vehicle fleet
  - MPW<sub>sg</sub> Mileage and payload weighting factor (Annex I No 2.6 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242[^annex4a-lvlu1-lvl3-1])
  - rf Reduction factor for the average carbon emissions:
  - in the years 2025–2029: 15 per cent
  - from 2030: 30 per cent
  - AWCO<sub>2sg</sub> 4-UD: 307.23
  - 4-RD: 197.16
  - 4-LH: 105.96
  - 5-RD: 84.00
  - 5-LH: 56.60
  - 9-RD: 110.98
  - 9-LH: 65.16
  - 10-RD: 83.26
  - 10-LH: 58.26

[^annex4a-lvlu1-lvl3-1]: See footnote to Art. 25*a* para. 1 let. a.

Annex 4b[^t-10]

(Art. 26*b*)

## Reduction in carbon emissions from the use of renewable synthetic motor fuels

[^t-10]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Reduction calculation for passenger cars, delivery vans and light articulated vehicles

*RedSF = SF \* EFref \* 1 000 000 / LM g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/km*

- [tab] CO<sub>2</sub> reduction by crediting each type of synthetic motor fuel as a total in g CO<sub>2</sub>/km
- SF Amount of synthetic fuel to be credited according to the assigned guarantees of origin in accordance with Article 92*c*, in kWh of energy contained
- EFref Emission factor of the fossil fuel to be replaced in accordance with Annex 10, converted to t CO<sub>2</sub>/kWh
- LM Average lifetime mileage in km: 175 000 km

### 2 Reduction calculation for heavy-duty vehicles

*RedSF = SF \* EFref \* 1 000 000 / (avgTL \* NoV) g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/tkm*

- RedSF CO<sub>2</sub> reduction by crediting each type of synthetic motor fuel as a fleet average in g CO<sub>2</sub>/tkm
- SF Amount of synthetic fuel to be credited according to the assigned guarantees of origin in accordance with Article 92*c*, in kWh of energy contained
- EFref Emission factor of the fossil fuel to be replaced in accordance with Annex 10, converted to t CO<sub>2</sub>/kWh
- avgLM

  Average total lifetime mileage of all vehicles in the new vehicle fleet. It is the weighted average of the values of the sub-groups, weighted according to the shares of the sub-groups in the new car fleet. The total lifetime mileage of the individual sub-groups is as follows:

  - 4-UD: 1,113,000tkm
  - 4-RD: 1,736,280tkm
  - 4-LH: 5,090,120tkm
  - 5-RD: 5,600,868tkm
  - 5-LH: 9,689,400tkm
  - 9-RD: 3,209,080tkm
  - 9-LH: 9,380,000tkm
  - 10-RD: 4,882,808tkm
  - 10-LH: 9,689,400tkm

- Nov. Number of vehicles in the new vehicle fleet

Annex 4c[^t-11]

(Art. 27)

## Calculation of carbon emissions

[^t-11]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Average carbon emissions of new vehicle fleets of large importers

### 1.1 New vehicle fleet comprising passenger cars, vans or light articulated vehicles

- 1\.1.1 The average carbon emissions of a new vehicle fleet comprising passenger cars, delivery vans or light articulated vehicles are calculated on the basis of the following formula and are rounded to three decimal places:
- *MCO**<sub>2</sub>* *= (1 – ZLEV) \* [(∑**<sub>v</sub>* *CO**<sub>2v</sub>* */ NoV] – RedSF / Nov. g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/km*
- 1\.1.2

  The following parameters apply:

  - MCO<sub>2</sub> Average carbon emissions of the new vehicle fleet in g CO<sub>2</sub>/km
  - ZLEV Reduction resulting from the prescribed shares of low-emission or zero-emission passenger cars, vans or light articulated vehicles being exceeded in percentage points (Art. 26*c*)
  - CO<sub>2v</sub> Carbon emissions of the individual vehicles in the new vehicle fleet, taking into account any reductions resulting from eco-innovations (Art. 26) and the use of natural gas and biogas (Art. 26*a*)
  - NoV Number of vehicles in the new vehicle fleet
  - RedSF CO<sub>2</sub> Carbon reduction resulting from the use of renewable synthetic fuels in g CO<sub>2</sub>/km (Art. 26*b*)

- 1\.1.3

  To calculate the share of low-emission and zero-emission vehicles, the vehicles are weighted according to their emission values as follows:

  - [tab] Passenger cars: Weighting = 1–CO<sub>2v</sub>\*0.7/50
  - [tab] Vans and light articulated vehicles Weighting = 1–CO<sub>2v</sub>/50
  - [tab] Vehicles with a negative weighted value are not classed as low-emission or zero-emission.

### 1.2 New fleet of heavy-duty vehicles

- 1\.2.1 The average carbon emissions of a new fleet of heavy-duty vehicles are calculated on the basis of the following formula and are rounded to three decimal places:
- *MCO**<sub>2</sub>* *= (1 – ZLEV) \* [∑**<sub>sg</sub>* *(Sh**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* MPW**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* MCO**<sub>2sg</sub>* *)] – RedSF g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/tkm*
- 1\.2.2

  The following parameters apply:

  - MCO<sub>2</sub> Average carbon emissions in the new vehicle fleet in grams CO<sub>2</sub> per tonne kilometre
  - ZLEV Reduction resulting from the prescribed shares of zero-emission heavy-duty vehicles points being exceeded in percentage (Art. 26*c*)
  - Ant<sub>sg</sub> Share of sub-groups in the new vehicle fleet
  - MPW<sub>sg</sub> Mileage and payload weighting factor for sub-group (Annex I No 2.6 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242[^annex4c-lvlu1-lvl1-lvl12-1]
  - MCO<sub>2sg</sub> Average carbon emissions per sub-group in the new vehicle fleet, calculated on the basis of the formula in Annex I No 2.2 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242 from the values per vehicle in accordance with Article 25*a* paragraph 1 letter b, taking into account any reductions as a result of the use of natural gas or biogas (Art. 26*a*)
  - RedSF CO<sub>2</sub> reduction resulting from the use of renewable synthetic fuels in g CO<sub>2</sub>/tkm (Art. 26*b*)

[^annex4c-lvlu1-lvl1-lvl12-1]: See footnote to Art. 25*a* para. 1 let. a.

### 2 Carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle

- 2\.1 The carbon emissions of a heavy-duty vehicle on the basis of the following formula and are rounded to three decimal places:
- *CO**<sub>2</sub>* *= MPW**<sub>sg</sub>* *\* CO**<sub>2v</sub>* *– RedSF g CO**<sub>2</sub>**/tkm*
- 2\.2

  The following parameters apply:

  - CO<sub>2</sub> Carbon emissions of the vehicle in g CO<sub>2</sub>/tkm
  - MPW<sub>sg</sub> Mileage and payload weighting factor for sub-group (Annex I No 2.6 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242[^annex4c-lvlu1-lvl2-1])
  - CO<sub>2v</sub> Carbon emissions of the vehicle calculated on the basis of Annex I No 2.2 of Regulation (EU) 2019/1242 from the values in Article 25*a* paragraph 1, taking into account any reductions as a result of the use of natural gas or biogas(Art. 26*a*)
  - RedSF CO<sub>2</sub> reduction resulting from the use of renewable synthetic fuels in g CO<sub>2</sub>/tkm in accordance with Annex 4*b*

[^annex4c-lvlu1-lvl2-1]: See footnote to Art. 25*a* para. 1 let. a.

Annex 5[^t-12]

(Art. 29 para. 1)

## Penalty amounts for exceeding individual targets (Art. 13 para. 1 of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act)

[^t-12]: Amended by No I para. 1 of the O of 20 Nov. 2024 ([AS 2024 705](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2024/705)). Revised by No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### 1 Penalty amounts for passenger cars, vans and light articulated vehicles

-

  For each gram of CO<sub>2</sub> per kilometre (from 0.1 gram) above the individual target, the penalty amounts to be paid if the individual target is exceeded are as follows:

  - a. for the reference year 2024: CHF 95;
  - b. for the reference year 2025: CHF 95;
  - c. for the reference year 2026: CHF 95.

### 2 Penalty amounts for heavy-duty vehicles

-

  For each gram of CO<sub>2</sub> per tonne kilometre by which the individual target is exceeded (from 0.1 gram), the penalty to be paid is:

  - a. for the reference year 2025: CHF 4,250;
  - b. for the reference year 2026: CHF 4,250.

Annex 6[^t-13]

(Art. 40 para. 1)

## Installation operators required to participate in the ETS

[^t-13]: Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)), of 25 Nov. 2020 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)) and of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

An installation operator that engages in at least one of the following activities must participate in the ETS:

- 1\. combustion of fossil-fuel or partial fossil-fuel fuels with a total rated thermal input of over 20MW; excluded is the combustion of fossil fuels or partial fossil fuels in installations whose main function is the disposal of municipal waste in accordance with Article 3 letter a of the ADWO[^annex6-lvlu1-1];
- 2\. refining of mineral oil when operating combustion units with a total rated thermal output above 20MW;
- 3\. coke production;
- 4\. roasting or sintering, including pelletising metal ore, including sulphide ore;
- 5\. production of iron or steel (primary or secondary fusion) including continuous casting, with a capacity of over 2.5 tonnes per hour;
- 6\. production or processing of ferrous metals including ferro-alloys in which combustion units with a total rated thermal input of over 20MW are operated. Processing includes in particular processing in rolling mills, reheating furnaces, annealing furnaces, forging plants, foundries and coating and pickling plants;
- 7\. production of primary aluminium or aluminium oxide;
- 8\. production of secondary aluminium in which combustion units with a total rated thermal input of over 20MW are operated;
- 9\. production or processing of non-ferrous metals, including production of alloys, refining, foundry casting, etc., in which combustion units with a total rated thermal input, including the energy sources used as reducing agents, of over 20MW are operated;
- 10\. production of cement clinker in rotary kilns with a production capacity of over 500 tonnes per day or in other furnaces with a production capacity of over 50 tonnes per day;
- 11\. production of lime or calcination of dolomite or magnesite in rotary kilns or in other furnaces with a production capacity of over 50 tonnes per day;
- 12\. manufacture of glass including glass fibre with a melting capacity of over 20 tonnes per day;
- 13\. manufacture of ceramic products by firing, in particular roofing tiles, bricks, refractory bricks, tiles, stoneware or porcelain, with a production capacity of over 75 tonnes per day;
- 14\. manufacture of mineral wool insulation material using glass, rock or slag with a melting capacity of over 20 tonnes per day;
- 15\. drying or calcination of gypsum or production of plaster boards and other gypsum products with a total production capacity for fired gypsum or dried secondary gypsum of over 20 tonnes per day;
- 16\. production of pulp from timber or other fibrous materials;
- 17\. production of paper or cardboard with a production capacity of over 20 tonnes per day;
- 18\. production of carbon black involving the carbonisation of organic substances such as oils, tars, cracker and distillation residues with a production capacity of over 50 tonnes per day;
- 19\. production of nitric acid;
- 20\. production of adipic acid;
- 21\. production of glyoxal and glyoxylic acid;
- 22\. production of ammonia;
- 23\. production of bulk organic chemicals by cracking, reforming, partial or full oxidation or by similar processes, with a production capacity of over 100 tonnes per day;
- 24\. production of hydrogen (H<sub>2</sub>) and synthesis gas with a production capacity of over 5 tonnes per day;
- 25\. production of soda ash (Na<sub>2</sub>CO<sub>3</sub>) and sodium bicarbonate (NaHCO<sub>3</sub>);
- 26\. production of niacin;
- 27\. capture of greenhouse gas emissions in installations in the ETS for the purposes of transport and geological storage;
- 28\. transport of captured greenhouse gas emissions from installations in the ETS in stationary transport installations;
- 29\. geological storage of greenhouse gas emissions originating in installations in the ETS.

[^annex6-lvlu1-1]: [SR 814.600](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9574)

Annex 7[^t-14]

[^t-14]: Repealed by No. II para. 4 of the O of 2 April 2025, with effect from 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

Annex 8[^t-15]

*(*Art. 45 para. 1 and 48 para. 1<sup>bis</sup>)

## Calculation of the maximum available quantity of emission allowances for installation operators participating in the ETS and calculation of the quantity in circulation

[^t-15]: Amended by No II para. 2 of the O of 24 Nov. 2021 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Maximum quantity of emission allowances available to installation operators participating in the ETS

The maximum total quantity of emission allowances available each year to all Installation operators participating in the ETS as a whole is calculated as follows:

- For the years 2025–2027:
- [tab] *Cap**<sub>i</sub>* *= [∑ ØFZ + ∑ Øemissions] \* [0.717 – (i-2024) \* 0.043]*
- For the years 2028–2030:
- [tab] *Cap**<sub>i</sub>* *= [∑ ØFZ + ∑ Øemissions] \* [0.588 – (i-2027) \* 0.044]*
- Cap<sub>i</sub> Maximum available quantity of Swiss emission allowances for installation operators for the year i
- ∑ ØFZ Sum of the average emission allowances allocated annually in the 2008–2012 period to installations that were already taken into account in the ETS in the 2008–2012 period and have again been taken into account in the ETS starting in 2013
- ∑ Øemissions Sum of the average greenhouse gases emitted annually in the 2009–2011 period by installations and the greenhouse gas emissions that have been newly taken into account in the ETS starting in 2013

### 2 Calculation of the quantity in circulation

- 2\.1 The quantity in circulation under Article 48 paragraph 1<sup>bis</sup> is the quantity of emission allowances resulting from the supply of emission allowances for installations minus the demand for emission allowances for installations.
- 2\.2

  The supply of emission allowances for installations is the sum of the follow-ing emission allowances:

  - a. 157,741 emission allowances that were not used in 2008–2012 and carried over to the 2013–2020 period for operators of installations pursuant to Article 138 paragraph 1 letter a;
  - b. the emission allowances for installations that were allocated free of charge in the period from 2013 to the previous year;
  - c. the emission allowances for installations that were auctioned in the period from 2013 to the previous year.

- 2\.3 The demand for emission allowances for installations is the result of the following subtraction: the relevant greenhouse gas emissions of the installations in the period from 2013 to the previous year pursuant to Article 55 minus the emission-reduction certificates delivered to cover the greenhouse gas emissions for the years 2013–2020.

Annex 9[^t-16]

(Arts 46 para. 1 and 46*b* paras 1, 2 and 4)

## Calculation of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge for installation operators in the ETS

[^t-16]: Revised by No II of the O of 8 Oct. 2014 ([AS 2014 3293](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2014/573)), No II para. 1 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)), of 25 Nov. 2020, in force since 1 Jan. 2021 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)), No II para. 1 of the O of 24 Nov. 2021 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)) and No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### 1 Benchmarks

- 1\.1 The quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge annually is based on the following product benchmarks:

<tr><td>Sulphite pulp, thermo-mechanical and mechanical pulp</td><td>(…)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Pulp made of recycled paper</td><td>(…)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Newsprint paper</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

<tr><td>Coated fine paper</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

<tr><td>Adipic acid</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

<tr><td>Phenol/Acetone</td><td>(…)</td></tr>
<tr><td>S-PVC</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

<tr><td>Soda ash</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

<tr><td>Mineral wool</td><td>(…)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Gypsum paperboard</td><td>(…)</td></tr>

- 1\.2 If no product benchmark applies, then the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge annually is calculated based on the heat benchmark as follows:
-

  (…) emission allowances per TJ of measurable heat, whereby only measurable heat produced or measurable heat imported from other installations whose operators participate in the ETS is eligible to be allocated emission allowances free of charge, provided that this heat is not produced using nuclear energy, and:

  - [tab] ais used within the system boundaries of the installation operator participating in the ETS for manufacturing products, for generating mechanical energy other than that used to generate electricity, for heating or for cool-ing, but not to generate electricity; or
  - b. is exported to third parties outside the ETS, with the exception of exports for generating electricity and transmitting imported heat.

- 1\.3 If neither a product benchmark nor a heat benchmark applies, then the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge annually is calculated on the basis on the following thermal-fuel benchmark:
-

  (…) emission allowances per TJ of energy input if within the system boundaries of the installation operator participating in the ETS:

  - a. non-measurable heat is produced from the combustion of energy sources and is used for manufacturing products, for generating mechanical energy other than that used to generate electricity, for heating or for cooling, but not to generate electricity; or
  - b. non-measurable heat is produced by safety flaring.

- 1\.4

  If none of the benchmarks described in numbers 1.1–1.3 is applicable, then the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge annually for emissions that result directly or indirectly from the production process is multiplied by the following factor:

  - – for the years 2021–2027: by a factor of 0.97
  - – from 2028: by a factor of 0.91.

- 1\.5 For using gases that come from processes and contain a high proportion of partially oxidised carbon (residual gas), there is an additional free-of-charge allocation to compensate for higher carbon emissions and lower efficiency in using residual gases in comparison with natural gas. This allocation is only made if the residual gas is created outside of a sub-installation with product benchmark and is used within the installation in the ETS to generate measurable or non-measurable heat or for the production of electricity.
- [tab] 1.5*a* Heat generated using electricity in accordance with number 1.2 or 1.3 entitles the producer to the free allocation of emission allowances.
- 1\.6 No emission allowances are allocated free of charge for heat resulting from the manufacture of nitric acid.
- 1\.7 If heat used within a sub-installation with a product benchmark is imported from third parties not in the ETS, if the heat originates from the manufacture of nitric acid or if it is generated using nuclear energy, the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge that is calculated according to the product benchmark shall be reduced by the product of this quantity of heat and the heat benchmark of (…) emission allowances per TJ.
- [tab] 1.7*a* If residual gases are flared in a sub-installation with a product benchmark and the resulting heat is not used, the quantity of emission allowances allocated free of charge, calculated according to the product benchmark, shall be reduced by the carbon emissions generated. This does not apply to safety flaring.
- 1\.8 In the case of installations whose main purpose is to dispose of special waste in accordance with Article 3 letter c ADWO[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl1-1], the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated annually free of charge of shall be calculated in accordance with number 1.3 for the support fuels used and in accordance with number 1.4 for the emissions from the combustion of the special waste. The heat benchmark under number 1.2 does not apply.

[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl1-1]: [SR 814.600](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/9574)

### 2 General calculation of the quantity of emission allowances allocated free of charge

- 2\.1 The quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge is calculated per sub-installation for each year of participation in the ETS, subject to numbers 3 and 5, according to the following formula:
- [tab] *All**<sub>i</sub>* *= BM \* AR \* Cl**<sub>i</sub>* *\* CBAM**i* *\* SKF**<sub>i</sub>*
- All<sub>i</sub> Allocation in year i
  - BM Benchmark
  - AR Activity rate (referring to the relevant benchmark)
  - Cl<sub>i</sub> 1 or adjustment factor in year i according to No 3.1
  - CBAMi or adjustment factor in year i according to No 3.1*a*
  - SKFi Cross-sectoral correction factor in year i

- 2\.2 The benchmark is determined per sub-installation on the basis of the benchmark hierarchy described in numbers 1.1–1.4.
- 2\.3

  The activity rate refers to the relevant benchmark. It is set initially for each sub-installation (historical activity rate) and corresponds to:

  - – for the 2021–2025 allocation period: the arithmetical mean of the annual values in the years 2014–2018;
  - – for the 2026–2030 allocation period: the median of the annual values in the years 2019–2023.

- 2\.4 In the absence of annual values for two full calendar years in the reference period in accordance with number 2.3, the annual value for the first full calendar year after the relevant installations start operating corresponds to the historical activity rate. If the start of operations takes place after 1 January 2021 for the 2021–2025 allocation period or after 1 January 2026 for the 2026–2030 allocation period, the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge for the period between the start of operations and 31 December of the same year shall be calculated using the actual activity rate for this period.
- 2\.5 In the event of permanent changes to activity rates during a reference period under number 2.3, which would result in an annual adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge under number 5 of more than 100,000 emission allowances, the activity rates for the entire reference period under number 2.3 shall be adjusted for these permanent changes when conducting the calculation in accordance with paragraph 2.1.

### 3 Adjustment factors

- 3\.1

  For sectors and sub-sectors that are not listed in the Annex to Decision 2019/708/EU[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl3-1], the quantities calculated in accordance with number 2 are multiplied by the following adjustment factors:

  - 3\.1.1 for 2021: 0.3
  - 3\.1.2 for 2022: 0.3
  - 3\.1.3 for 2023: 0.3
  - 3\.1.4 for 2024: 0.3
  - 3\.1.5 for 2025: 0.3
  - 3\.1.6 for 2026: 0.3
  - 3\.1.7 for 2027: 0.225
  - 3\.1.8 for 2028: 0.15
  - 3\.1.9 for 2029: 0.075
  - 3\.1.10 for 2030: 0

- 3\.1*a*

  For the production of goods that are not listed in Annex I of Regulation (EU) 2023/956[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl3-2], the quantities calculated in accordance with number 2 shall be multiplied by the following adjustment factors:

  - 3\.1*a*.1 for 2026: 0.975
  - 3\.1*a*.2 for 2027: 0.95
  - 3\.1*a*.3 for 2028: 0.9
  - 3\.1*a*.4 for 2029: 0.775
  - 3\.1*a*.5 for 2030: 0.515

- 3\.2 If an installation operator delivers heat to a third party, the heat consumer’s adjustment factors under numbers 3.1 and 3.1*a* shall apply.
- 3\.3 The adjustment factor under number 3.1 is 0.3 for measurable heat if this is distributed via a network and used for heating water, space heating or space cooling in buildings or at locations whose operator does not participate in the ETS; the foregoing does not apply to measurable heat that is directly or indirectly used to manufacture products or generate electricity.
- 3\.4 The adjustment factor under number 3.1 is 1 for the manufacture of niacin and for installations whose main purpose is the disposal of hazardous waste as defined in Article 3 letter c ADWO.

[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl3-1]: See footnote to Art. 135 let. d<sup>ter</sup>.
[^annex9-lvlu1-lvl3-2]: See footnote to Art. 135 let. d<sup>ter</sup>.

### 4 …

### 5 Adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge

### 5.1 Adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge in accordance with Article 46*b* paragraph 1

- 5\.1.1 The calculated quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge shall be adjusted if the absolute value of the relative deviation between the arithmetical mean of the activity rates for the two previous years and the his-torical activity rate exceeds 15 per cent. The absolute value of the relative deviation is calculated as follows:
- *abs(X**<sub>i</sub>**) = abs(aAR**<sub>i</sub>* *– hAR) / hAR*
- abs(X<sub>i</sub>) = absolute value of the relative deviation in the year i
- aAR<sub>i</sub> = arithmetical mean of the activity rates in the two previous years i-1 and i-2;
- hAR = historical activity rate
- 5\.1.2

  For the adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge, the following applies as the relevant activity rate:

  - a. the arithmetical mean of the activity rates in the two previous years; or
  - b. the relevant activity rate already used for the previous year, if an adjustment was already made in the previous year and the absolute value of the relative deviation still amounts to more than 15 per cent, but does not at the same time exceed at least the next higher or next lower 5-per cent interval (for example 20–25 per cent, 25–30 per cent).

### 5.1*a* Adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge in accordance with Art. 46*b* paragraph 2

- [tab] 5.1*a*.1 The quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge, calculated on the basis of the heat benchmark or the fuel benchmark, shall be adjusted if the absolute value of the relative deviation between the arithmetic mean of the expected activity rates for the two preceding years and the historical activity rate exceeds 15 per cent. The absolute value of the relative deviation is calculated as follows:
- *abs(Y**<sub>i</sub>**) = abs(eAR**<sub>i</sub>* *– hAR) / hAR*
  - [tab] whereby: *eAR**<sub>i</sub>* *= [(∑ hEff**<sub>z</sub>* *\* P**<sub>z, i</sub>*<sub>-</sub>*<sub>1</sub>**) + uAR**<sub>i-1</sub>* *+ (∑ hEff**<sub>z</sub>* *\* P**<sub>z, i-2</sub>**) + uAR**<sub>i-2</sub>**] / 2*
  - [tab] abs(Y<sub>i</sub>) = absolute value of the relative deviation in the year i
  - [tab] eAR<sub>i</sub> = arithmetical mean of the activity rates in the two previous years i-1 and i-2;
  - [tab] <sub>hEffz</sub> = the average historical energy efficiency in the production of each product z manufactured within the system boundaries of a sub-installation during the reference period given in number 2.3, expressed in TJ per tonne
  - [tab] P<sub>z, i</sub> = production quantity of each product z manufactured within the system boundaries of a sub-installation, expressed in tonnes
  - [tab] uAR<sub>i</sub> = energy used in year i that is not directly linked to the manufacture of products z, expressed in TJ
  - [tab] hAR = historical activity rate

- 5\.1*a*.2

  The following activity rates apply when adjusting the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge:

  - a. the arithmetical mean of the activity rates expected in the preceding two years; or
  - b. the activity rate already applicable for the previous year, if an adjustment was already made in the previous year and the absolute value of the relative deviation remains greater than 15 per cent, but does not at the same time exceed the next higher or next lower 5-per-cent interval (e.g. 20–25 per cent, 25–30 per cent).

### 5.2 Adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge in accordance with Art. 46*b* para. 4

- 5\.2.1 The calculated quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge shall be adjusted annually if the absolute value of the relative deviation between the arithmetical mean of the values of any one of the parameters for the two previous years used in calculating the allocation amounts to more than 15 per cent of the historical value of the same parameter. The absolute value of the relative deviation is calculated as follows:
- *abs(Z**<sub>i</sub>**) = abs(aZP**<sub>i</sub>* *– hZP) / hZP*
- abs(Z<sub>i</sub>) = absolute value of the relative deviation in the year i
- aZP<sub>i</sub> = arithmetical mean of the values of a parameter in accordance with number 5.2.3 for the two previous years i-1 and i-2;
- hZP = historical parameter value in the reference period in accordance with number 2.
- 5\.2.2

  For the adjustment of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge, the relevant parameter value is:

  - a. arithmetical mean of the parameter values of the two previous years; or
  - b. the parameter value applicable in the previous year, if an adjustment was already made in the previous year and if the absolute value of the relative deviation remains greater than 15 per cent, but does not at the same time exceed the next higher or next lower 5 per cent interval (e.g. 20–25 per cent, 25–30 per cent).

- 5\.2.3

  The parameters taken into account when calculating the allocation are in particular:

  - a. the heat used within a product benchmark in accordance with number 1.7;
  - b. the emissions from flaring the residual gases within a product benchmark in accordance with number 1.7*a*.
