### 3 Amount of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy and levy rates for fuels for certain stationary uses

### 3.1 Amount of the CO<sub>2</sub> levy

-

  The CO<sub>2</sub> levy is CHF 120 per tonne of CO<sub>2</sub> if the fuels are used:

  - a. to power CHP plants, turbines or motors of stationary heat pumps for the generation of heat or alternating heat and cooling; or
  - b. to generate electricity in thermal plants.

### 3.2 Levy rates

- The fuels used in accordance with number 3.1 are subject to the levy rates under number 2.

Annex 12[^t-19]

(Arts 112–113*b*)

## Direct use of geothermal energy for the provision of heat

[^t-19]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 1 Nov. 2017 ([AS 2017 6753](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2017/757)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 24 Nov. 2021 ([AS 2021 859](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2021/859)), No III of the O of 23 Nov. 2022 ([AS 2022 771](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/771)) and No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Definitions

- 1\.1

  Prospecting covers investigations which serve to:

  - – directly or indirectly characterise the subsoil of a presumed geothermal reservoir, and
  - – determine the above-ground site and the underground landing point of an exploration well.

- 1\.2 Development covers exploration by drilling and extracting hot water and the possibility of returning the extracted water into the geothermal reservoir.

### 2 Allowable investment costs

- 2\.1

  Allowable costs with regard to prospecting are the costs of implementation, planning and project management as well as the applicant’s own work, pro-vided that they are actual costs and are directly necessary for the economic and appropriate implementation of the project and which cover:

  - a. the acquisition of new geodata in the prospecting area;
  - b. work required for the acquisition of new geodata;
  - c. analysis and interpretation.

- 2\.2

  Allowable costs with regard to development are the costs of implementation, planning and project management as well as the applicant’s own work, pro-vided that they are actual costs and are directly necessary for the economic and appropriate implementation of the project and which cover:

  - a. the preparation, construction and dismantling of the drilling site;
  - b. drilling, including pipework, cementation and completion for all planned production, injection, and monitoring wells;
  - c. borehole and reservoir stimulation;
  - d. borehole tests;
  - e. borehole measurements including instrumentation;
  - f. circulation tests;
  - g. analyses of substances found;
  - h. geological monitoring, data analysis and interpretation.

- 2\.3 Planning and project management costs shall be taken into account up to a maximum of 15 per cent of the allowable implementation costs. Costs incurred prior to submission of the application are allowable.
- 2\.4 The applicant's own work, such as planning or implementation work, is allowable only if it is customary and can be proven by means of a detailed work report.
- 2\.5 The costs incurred within the framework of official processes concerning prospecting and development do not constitute allowable costs.

### 3 Procedure to support prospecting

- 3\.1 Application
-

  The application must provide information on the technical, economic, legal, safety, environmental protection and organisational aspects of the project, and in particular on:

  - a. the current state of knowledge in the exploration area including all exist-ing processed geodata, analyses and interpretations;
  - b. the geological prospecting planned for determining the sites and landing points of a well and the location and characterisation of a geothermal well, and the expected added value in terms of an increase in the probability of successful development;
  - c. use concepts for successful prospecting and preliminary feasibility studies;
  - d. detailed schedules and cost estimates with deviations of no more than 20 per cent;
  - e. the measures planned to identify hazards and risks to health, occupational and operational safety, and the environment, in particular drinking water resources, and the measures planned to reduce these risks to a level that is as low, reasonable and practicable as possible.

- 3\.2 Examination of the application
- 3\.2.1 The SFOE shall appoint a representative of the Federal Office of Topography (swisstopo) to the independent panel of experts, in particular to assess the geoscientific project components and the added value for exploration in Switzerland.
- 3\.2.2

  The panel of experts shall examine and assess the application on the basis of the information referred to under number 3.1 and in particular with regard to:

  - a. the planned prospecting work and project management;
  - b. the technical and qualitative state of the planned work and the degree of innovation;
  - c. the question of how much the prospecting work increases the probability of finding and closing a geothermal reservoir;
  - d. the added value for exploring the substrate of Switzerland for geothermal reservoirs;
  - e. the management of risks to health, occupational and operational safety and the environment.

- 3\.2.3

  If the panel of experts assesses the project positively, it shall in particular make a recommendation to the SFOE on:

  - a. the expected increase in the probability of finding a geothermal reservoir;
  - b. the deadlines for the project stages;
  - c. the amount of the development contribution to be granted;
  - d. the appointment of a representative of swisstopo as project supervisor.

- 3\.3 Contract
-

  Where the contribution for prospecting can be granted, the following points in particular shall be regulated in the contract under Article 113 paragraph 5:

  - a. the milestones to be reached by the applicant and the deadlines to be met;
  - b. the applicant’s obligation to inform the SFOE, in particular with regard to financial reports, final accounts and any changes to the project;
  - c. the scope, conditions and due date of the contribution for prospecting;
  - d. subject to cantonal monopolies, the transfer, free of charge, of the installation to the Confederation and the granting to the Confederation of the right to purchase the property if a project is discontinued and not used for any other purpose;
  - e. the disclosure of all financial data necessary to calculate any losses or gains under Article 113*b*;
  - f. reasons leading to termination of the contract;
  - g. further requirements.

- 3\.4 Project implementation and completion
- 3\.4.1 The project engineer carries out the planned prospecting work.
- 3\.4.2 The project supervisor oversees the project during prospecting work; they evaluate the results of the prospecting work. They may consult the panel of experts in order to fulfil their tasks. They report regularly to the SFOE and the panel of experts.
- 3\.4.3 If the milestones or deadlines under number 3.3 letter a are not met, the SFOE may terminate the contract immediately.
- 3\.4.4 After completion of the work, the panel of experts shall evaluate the results of the prospecting work for the SFOE and assess them with regard to the expected increase in the probability of finding a suspected geothermal reservoir.

### 4 Procedure to support development

- 4\.1 An application for support for development may only be submitted if prospecting has been carried out beforehand in the area concerned and a prospect-ng report regarding the probability of a presumed geothermal reservoir is available.
- 4\.2 Application
-

  The application must provide information on the technical, economic, legal, safety, environmental protection and organisational aspects of the project, and in particular on:

  - a. the detailed drilling, completion, measuring and testing programme for all planned drilling;
  - b. detailed schedules and cost estimates with deviations of no more than 20 per cent;
  - c. the expected properties of the presumed geothermal reservoir, in particu-lar its temperature in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties;
  - d. the planned alternative use of the wells and the geothermal reservoir if the results do not meet expectations, including on the plans for their direct and indirect use, providing details in particular of their economic impact;
  - e. the measures planned to identify hazards and risks to health, occupational and operational safety, and the environment, in particular drinking water resources, and the measures planned to reduce these risks to a level that is as low, reasonable and as practicable as possible;
  - f. the innovations planned to open up geothermal reservoirs in Switzerland in a reliable and promising manner;
  - g. the importance of development works in relation to the exploration of Switzerland’s subsoil for geothermal reservoirs;
  - h. the intended legal form or name and business of the operating installation operator, the shareholders and their ownership interest;
  - i. the financing and administrative costs of the development, construction, extension, operation and dismantling phases;
  - j. the valorisation of the geothermal reservoir on the basis of a direct-use concept, a description of the potential heat consumers and their integration into the project, including the expected reductions in carbon emissions.

- 4\.3 Examination of the application
- 4\.3.1 The SFOE shall appoint a representative of swisstopo to the independent panel of experts, in particular to assess the geoscientific project components and the added value for exploration in Switzerland.
- 4\.3.2

  The panel of experts shall examine and assess the application on the basis of the information referred to under number 4.2 and in particular with regard to:

  - a. the expected properties of the geothermal reservoir, in particular with regard to the temperature in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties;
  - b. the technical and qualitative state of the planned work and the degree of innovation;
  - c. the added value for exploring the substrate of Switzerland for geothermal reservoirs;
  - d. the management of risks to health, occupational and operational safety and the environment.

- 4\.3.3

  If the panel of experts assesses the application positively, it shall in particular make a recommendation to the SFOE on:

  - a. the expected temperature of the reservoir in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties;
  - b. the deadlines for the project stages;
  - c. the amount of the development contribution to be granted;
  - d. the appointment of a member of the panel of experts as project supervisor.

- 4\.4 Contract
-

  Where the development contribution may be granted, the following points in particular shall be settled in the contract under Article 113 paragraph 5:

  - a. the milestones to be reached by the applicant and the deadlines to be met;
  - b. the applicant’s obligation to inform the SFOE, in particular with regard to financial reports, final accounts and any changes to the project;
  - c. scope, conditions and due date of the development contribution;
  - d. subject to cantonal monopolies, the transfer, free of charge, of the installation to the Confederation and the granting to the Confederation of the right to purchase the property if a project is discontinued and not used for any other purpose;
  - e. the disclosure of all financial data necessary to calculate any losses or gains under Article 113*d*;
  - f. reasons leading to termination of the contract;
  - g. further requirements.

- 4\.5 Project implementation and completion
- 4\.5.1 The project engineer carries out the planned development work.
- 4\.5.2 The person appointed by the panel of experts to accompany the project over-sees the project during development work and evaluates the results in particu-lar with regard to the temperature and transport properties of the reservoir. They may consult the panel of experts in order to fulfil their tasks. They shall report regularly to the SFOE and the panel of experts.
- 4\.5.3 If the milestones or deadlines under number 4.4 letter a are not met, the SFOE may terminate the contract immediately.
- 4\.5.4 The panel of experts shall evaluate the results of the development work no later than six months after completion of the development work.
- 4\.5.5 The SFOE shall inform the project engineer of the results of the assessment, in particular those relating to the geothermal reservoir.

### 5 Geodata

- 5\.1 The applicant shall make the respective geodata available free of charge to swisstopo and to the canton where the prospecting and development is taking place in accordance with the technical specifications of swisstopo no later than six months after the data was collected.
- 5\.2 swisstopo may use and process these geodata in accordance with the objectives of the Geoinformation Act of 5 October 2007[^annex12-lvlu1-lvl5-1] and the Geological Survey Ordinance of 21 May 2008[^annex12-lvlu1-lvl5-2]; the cantons with prospecting and development sites may do so in accordance with their respective regulations.
- 5\.3

  It shall make the primary and the processed primary geodata available to the public after completion of the survey within the periods given below:

  - a. in the case of prospecting: 24 months;
  - b. in the case of development: 12 months.

[^annex12-lvlu1-lvl5-1]: [SR 510.62](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7338)
[^annex12-lvlu1-lvl5-2]: [SR 510.624](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7350)

Annex 12a[^t-20]

(Arts 112–113*b*)

## Indirect use of hydrothermal resources for heat supply

[^t-20]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 May 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Definitions

- Development is the drilling that completes the geothermal cycle for indirect use and enables the extraction or reinjection of water from the geothermal reservoir.

### 2 Allowable investment costs

- 2\.1

  The following costs are allowable in development for indirect use: construction, planning and project management costs and the applicant’s own contributions, provided that they have actually been incurred and are directly necessary for the cost-effective and appropriate:

  - a. the preparation, construction and dismantling of the drilling site;
  - b. drilling, including pipework, cementation and completion for all planned production, injection, and monitoring wells;
  - c. borehole and reservoir stimulation;
  - d. borehole tests;
  - e. borehole measurements including instrumentation;
  - f. circulation tests;
  - g. analyses of substances found;
  - h. geological monitoring, data analysis and interpretation.

- 2\.2 Planning and project management costs shall be taken into account up to a maximum of 15 per cent of the allowable implementation costs. Costs incurred prior to submission of the application are allowable.
- 2\.3 The applicant’s own work, such as planning or implementation work, is al-lowable only if it is customary and can be proven by means of a detailed work report.
- 2\.4

  The following are not allowable:

  - a. costs incurred in the course of official procedures relating to the development for indirect use;
  - b. investment costs for the planning and construction of surface installations that enable indirect use, in particular heat pumps.

### 3 Procedure to support development

- 3\.1 Application
-

  The application must include the final report on the exploration carried out in accordance with Annex 12, which describes the current situation and the condition of the exploration well, as well as the characteristics of the selected and discovered geothermal resource, and explains the reasons why the planned di-rect use is not feasible. It must also provide information on the technical, economic, legal, safety, environmental and organisational aspects of the project, clearly highlighting the differences from the project for direct use, in particular regarding:

  - a. the detailed drilling, completion, measuring and testing programme for all planned drilling;
  - b. detailed schedules and cost estimates with deviations of no more than 20 per cent;
  - c. the expected properties of the geothermal reservoir, in particular its temperature in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties, as well as any uncertainties about these properties;
  - d. the planned use of the wells and the geothermal reservoir if the results do not meet expectations;
  - e. the measures planned to identify hazards and risks to health, occupational and operational safety, and the environment, in particular drinking water resources, and the measures planned to reduce these risks to a level that is as low, reasonable and as practicable as possible;
  - f. the intended legal form and name or business of the operating installation operator if different from in the case of direct use;
  - g. the financing and administrative costs of the development, construction, extension, operation and dismantling phases, including a list of any funding received for the original direct-use project;
  - h. the valorisation of the geothermal reservoir on the basis of an indirect-use concept, special features of the heat pumps, in particular coefficient of performance, energy consumption and energy origin, and a description of the potential heat consumers and their integration into the project, including the expected reductions in carbon emissions.

- 3\.2 Examination of the application
- 3\.2.1 The SFOE shall appoint a representative of swisstopo to the independent panel of experts, in particular to assess the geoscientific project components and the added value for exploration in Switzerland.
- 3\.2.2

  The panel of experts shall examine and assess the application on the basis of the information referred to under number 3.1 and in particular with regard to:

  - a. the expected properties of the geothermal reservoir, in particular with regard to the temperature in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties;
  - b. the technical and qualitative state of the planned work;
  - c. management of risks to health and safety, operations and the environment.

- 3\.2.3

  If the panel of experts assesses the project positively, it shall in particular make a recommendation to the SFOE on:

  - a. the expected temperature of the reservoir in the borehole at the level of the reservoir and its transport properties;
  - b. the deadlines for the project stages;
  - c. the amount of the development contribution to be granted;
  - d. the appointment of a member of the panel of experts as project supervisor.

- 3\.3 Contract
-

  Where the development contribution may be granted, the following points in particular shall be settled in the contract under Article 113 paragraph 5:

  - a. the milestones to be reached by the applicant and the deadlines to be met;
  - b. the applicant’s obligation to inform the SFOE, in particular with regard to financial reports, final accounts and any changes to the project;
  - c. scope, conditions and due date of the development contribution;
  - d. subject to cantonal monopolies, the transfer, free of charge, of the installation to the Confederation and the granting to the Confederation of the right to purchase the property if a project is discontinued and not used for any other purpose;
  - e. the disclosure of all financial data necessary to calculate any losses or gains under Article 113*c*;
  - f. reasons leading to termination of the contract;
  - g. further requirements.

- 3\.4 Project implementation and completion
- 3\.4.1 The project engineer carries out the planned development work.
- 3\.4.2 The person appointed by the panel of experts to accompany the project oversees the project during development work and evaluates the results in particular with regard to the temperature and transport properties of the reservoir. They may consult the panel of experts in order to fulfil their tasks. They shall report regularly to the SFOE and the panel of experts.
- 3\.4.3 If the milestones or deadlines under number 3.3 letter a are not met, the SFOE may terminate the contract immediately.
- 3\.4.4 No later than six months after completion of the development work, the panel of experts shall evaluate the results of the development work and report to the SFOE.
- 3\.4.5 The SFOE shall inform the project engineer of the results of the assessment, in particular those relating to the geothermal reservoir.

### 4 Geodata

- 4\.1 The applicant shall make the respective geodata available free of charge to swisstopo and to the canton where the prospecting and development is taking place in accordance with the technical specifications of swisstopo no later than six months after the data was collected.
- 4\.2 swisstopo may use and process these geodata in accordance with the objectives of the Geoinformation Act of 5 October 2007[^annex12a-lvlu1-lvl4-1] and the Geological Survey Ordinance of 21 May 2008[^annex12a-lvlu1-lvl4-2]; the cantons with prospecting and development sites may do so in accordance with their respective regulations.
- 4\.3 It shall make the primary and the processed primary geodata available to the public within 12 months of the survey being completed.

[^annex12a-lvlu1-lvl4-1]: [SR 510.62](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7338)
[^annex12a-lvlu1-lvl4-2]: [SR 510.624](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/7350)

Annex 13[^t-21]

(Arts 46*d,* 49*g*, 53)

## Aircraft operators required to participate in the ETS

[^t-21]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Revised by No II para. 3 of the O of 4 May 2022 ([AS 2022 311](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2022/311)) and No II para. 1 of the O of 29 Sept. 2023, in force since 1 Jan. 2024 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)).

- 1\.

  Aircraft operators are required to participate in the ETS if they operate the following flights:

  - a. domestic flights in Switzerland;
  - b. flights from Switzerland to member states of the EEA including flights to the outermost regions;
  - c. flights from Switzerland to the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland (UK).

- 1*a*.

  Outermost regions are:

  - a. Guadeloupe;
  - b. French Guiana;
  - c. Martinique;
  - d. Mayotte;
  - e. Réunion;
  - f. Saint Martin;
  - g. Azores;
  - h. Madeira;
  - i. Canary Islands.

- 2\.

  The following flights are exempted:

  - a. flights conducted exclusively for the transport on official mission of a reigning monarch and his/her immediate family, heads of state, heads of government and government ministers, where this is substantiated by an appropriate status indicator in the flight plan;
  - b. military, customs and police flights;
  - c. flights relating to search and rescue, fire-fighting flights, humanitarian flights and emergency medical service flights;
  - d. flights conducted exclusively under visual flight rules as defined in Annex 2 of the Convention of 7 December 1944[^annex13-lvlu1-1] on International Civil Aviation;
  - e. flights terminating at the aerodrome from which the aircraft has taken off and during which no planned intermediate landing has been made;
  - f. training flights conducted exclusively in order to acquire or maintain a licence, or a rating in the case of cockpit flight crew, where this is substantiated by an appropriate remark in the flight plan provided that the flights are not for the transport of passengers and/or cargo or for the positioning or ferrying of aircraft;
  - g. flights conducted exclusively for the purpose of scientific research;
  - h. flights conducted exclusively for the purpose of checking, testing or certifying aircraft or airborne or ground-based equipment;
  - i. flights conducted by aircraft with a certified maximum take-off mass of less than 5,700 kilograms;
  - j. flights conducted by commercial operators in each of three consecutive four-month periods if they conduct fewer than 243 flights in accordance with number 1 or if their total annual carbon emissions are below 10,000 tonnes;
  - k. flights conducted by non-commercial operators if the total annual carbon emissions from the flights conducted by these operators in accordance with number 1 are below 1,000 tonnes;
  - l. …

- 3\. The exemptions cited in number 2 letters j and k do not apply to aircraft operators subject to the European ETS.
- 4\. For the allocation of flights to the four-month periods cited in number 2 letter j, the local take-off time of each flight applies.

[^annex13-lvlu1-1]: [SR 0.748.0](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/3110)

Annex 14[^t-22]

(Art. 46*d* paras 1 and 2, 51 paras 1, 2 and 4, 52 paras 1, 6 and 7, 53 paras 1 and 2, 55 para. 2 and 130 para. 1)

## Competent authority for ETS participants

[^t-22]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019, in force since 1 Jan. 2020 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)).

### 1 Installation operators

- For installation operators participating in the ETS, the FOEN is the competent authority.

### 2 Aircraft operators

- 2\.1 For aircraft operators obliged to participate in the ETS, the state competent for their administration is determined in accordance with the Ordinance (EC) No 748/2009[^annex14-lvlu1-lvl2-1].
- 2\.2

  It is determined by:

  - a. which state issued the operating licence; or
  - b. the highest attributed estimated value for carbon emissions of the respective aircraft operator in comparison with the other states.

- 2\.3 In the case of administration by Switzerland, the FOEN is the competent authority.

[^annex14-lvlu1-lvl2-1]: See footnote to Art. 135 let. f.

Annex 15[^t-23]

(Arts 46*e* and 46*h*)

## Calculation of the maximum available quantity of emission allowances and the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge for the use of renewable or low-emission aviation fuels

[^t-23]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Revised by No II para. 2 of the O of 29 Sept. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)) and No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

- **1.** **Calculation of the maximum quantity of emission allowances available each year for all aircraft operators**
- **1.1** **Basis for the calculation**
- 1\.1.1

  The maximum quantity of emission allowances available each year for all aircraft operators shall be calculated on the basis of the following benchmark values (BM):

  - a. for 2020–2023: 0.000642186914222035 emission allowances per tonne-kilometre (BM<sub>2020</sub>)
  - b. for 2024: 0.000481640185666526 emission allowances per tonne-kilometre (BM<sub>2024</sub>)
  - c. for 2025: 0.000321093457111017 emission allowances per tonne-kilometre (BM<sub>2025</sub>)

- 1\.1.2

  Whereby:

  - – tonne-kilometres (tkm): flight distance [km] \* payload [t].
  - – flight distance: great circle distance between the aerodrome of departure and the aerodrome of arrival plus 95km.
  - – payload: total weight of cargo, mail, passengers and baggage that is transported.

- 1\.1.3

  The following applies when calculating the payload:

  - a. The number of passengers is the number of persons on board less the crew.
  - b.

    The aircraft operator may:

    - – use the weight contained in its mass and balance documentation (actual weight or standard weight for passengers and checked baggage); or
    - – use a standard value of 100kg for each passenger and their checked baggage.

- **1.2.** **Calculation for individual years**
- The maximum quantity of emission allowances available each year for all aircraft operators shall be calculated as follows:
- 1\.2.1 In 2020 maximum available quantity
- *Cap**<sub>2020</sub>* *= ∑tkm**<sub>CH-ETS</sub>* *\* BM**<sub>2020</sub>* *\* 100 / 82*
- Cap<sub>2020</sub> emissions cap for the year 2020
- ∑tkm<sub>CH-ETS</sub> sum of tonne-kilometres in 2018 in the Swiss ETS (excluding flights to outermost regions)
- BM<sub>2020</sub> benchmark for the year 2020
- 1\.2.2 Annual maximum available quantity in 2021–2023
- *Cap**<sub>202x</sub>* *= Cap**<sub>2020</sub>* *– x \* 0.022 \* Cap**<sub>2020</sub>*
- Cap<sub>202x</sub> emissions cap for the year 202x; whereby x = 1, 2, 3.
- 1\.2.3 Annual maximum available quantity 2024–2027
- *Cap**<sub>202x</sub>* *= consolidated cap**<sub>2023</sub>* *+ virtual cap(OR)**<sub>2023</sub>* *– (x – 3) \* 0.043 \* (consolidated cap**2020* *+ virtual cap(OR)**<sub>2020</sub>**)*
- Cap<sub>202x</sub> emissions cap for the year 202x; whereby x = 4, 5, 6, 7.
- consolidated cap<sub>2023</sub> = 0.934 \* 0.97 \* Cap2020
- virtual cap(OR)<sub>2023</sub> = 0.934 \* ∑tkmOR \* BM<sub>2020</sub> \* 0.97 / 0.82
- *∑tkm**OR* = sum of tonne-kilometres in 2018 from flights to the outermost regions (OR)
- consolidated cap<sub>2020</sub> = 0.97 \* Cap<sub>2020</sub>
- virtual cap(OR)<sub>2020</sub> = ∑tkmOR \* BM<sub>2020</sub> \* 0.97 / 0.82
- 1\.2.4 Annual maximum available quantity from 2028
- *Cap**<sub>y</sub>* *= Cap**<sub>2027</sub>* *– (y – 2027) \* 0.044 \* (consolidated cap**2020* *+ virtual cap(OR)**<sub>2020</sub>**)*
- Cap*<sub>y</sub>* emissions cap for the year y; whereby y = 2028, 2029, 2030.
- Cap<sub>2027</sub> emissions cap for the year 2027
- **2.–4.** **…**

### 5 Calculation of the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge for the use of renewable or low-emission aviation fuels (Art. 46*h*)

- 5\.1

  The emission allowances to be allocated free of charge under Article 46*h* paragraph 1 are available for the used of the following renewable or low-emission aviation fuels:

  - [tab] a. renewable synthetic aviation fuels for which the emission factor is zero in accordance with Annex 16, paragraph 3.3 letter b;
  - [tab] b. renewable biogenic aviation fuels as defined in Article 2 number 34 of Directive (EU) 2018/2001[^annex15-lvlu1-lvl1-1], for which the emission factor specified in Annex 16 number 3.3 letter a is zero;
  - [tab] c. other renewable or low-emission aviation fuels whose energy content does not derive from fossil sources and which can be counted towards the blending obligation under Article 28*f* of the CO<sub>2</sub> Act.

- 5\.2 The quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge to an aircraft operator for the use of renewable or low-emission aviation fuels in the previous year (‘SAF allowances’) is calculated as follows:
- *AllocationSAF* *<sub>allowances</sub>* *= ∑ cost differences to be offset / price of an emission allowance.*
- 5\.3 The cost differences between renewable or low-emission aviation fuels and fossil aviation fuel are calculated as follows for each renewable or low-emission aviation fuel:
- *Cost difference = [P**<sub>z</sub>* *– (P**<sub>f</sub>* *+ P**<sub>ETS</sub>**)] \* Q*
  - [tab] P<sub>z</sub>: Price per tonne of renewable or low-emission aviation fuel
  - [tab] P<sub>f</sub>: Price per tonne of fossil aviation fuel
  - [tab] P<sub>ETS</sub>: ETS savings per tonne of renewable or low-emission aviation fuel
  - [tab] Q: Quantity of renewable or low-emission aviation fuel in tonnes

- 5\.4

  The cost differences calculated in accordance with number 5.3 shall be offset to the following extent:

  - [tab] a. for aviation fuels in number 5.1 letter a: 95 per cent
  - [tab] b. for aviation fuels in number 5.1 letter b: 70 per cent;
  - [tab] for aviation fuels in number 5.1 letter c: 50 per cent.

- 5\.5 The prices of renewable or low-emission aviation fuels, the price of fossil aviation fuel and the price of an emission allowance are determined by the previous year’s data published annually by the European Commission in the Official Journal of the European Union.

[^annex15-lvlu1-lvl1-1]: Directive (EU) 2018/2001 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 December 2018 on the promotion of the use of energy from renewable sources (recast), OJ L 328, 21.12.2018, p. 82; as last amended by Directive (EU) 2024/1711, OJ L 2024/1711, 26.6.2024.

Annex 16[^t-24]

(Art. 51)

## Requirements for the monitoring plan

[^t-24]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 25 Nov. 2020 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)), of 29 Sept. 2023 ([AS 2023 581](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2023/581)), of 2 April 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)) and No II of the O of 19 Nov. 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2026 ([AS 2025 788](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/788)).

### 1 Monitoring plan for installation operators

-

  The monitoring plan must specify how the installation operator ensures that:

  - a. standardised or other established procedures are used for the measurement or calculation of greenhouse gas emissions and energy consumption;
  - b. the greenhouse gas emissions and energy consumption are as completely, consistently and accurately recorded as is technically and operationally possible and economically feasible;
  - c. the measurement, calculation and documentation of greenhouse gas emissions and energy consumption are traceable and transparent;
  - d. the data required to assess an adjustment to the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge under Article 46*b* is fully, consistently and precisely recorded and is comprehensible.

### 2 Monitoring plan for aircraft operators

- 2\.1

  The monitoring plan must ensure that:

  - a. that all flights for which carbon emission data have to be collected are included and that the carbon emissions for each flight are calculated accurately. The emissions are calculated in accordance with number 3;
  - b. the collection of data required to determine any further climate impact of individual flights in order to establish the climate impact of other emissions from aviation.

- 2\.2

  The monitoring plan must include the following information:

  - a. the information required to identify the aircraft operator;
  - b. the information required to identify the aircraft operated and fuel type assigned to each type of aircraft;
  - c. a description of the methodology for ensuring the complete acquisition of data for all aircraft for which data have to be recorded;
  - d. a description of the methodology for ensuring the complete recording of all flights for which data have to be recorded;
  - e. a description of the methodology for calculating the carbon emissions for each flight;
  - f. a description of the methodology for calculating the share of renewable and low-emission fuels;
  - g. a description of the methodology for calculating any further climate impacts of individual flights.

- 2\.3

  In the case of aircraft operators that generate carbon emissions of more than 25,000 tonnes per year, the monitoring plan must also include the following information:

  - a. a procedure for recording the fuel consumption of each aircraft;
  - b. a methodology for eliminating data gaps.

- 2\.4 In the event of a change in the status of the aircraft operator in accordance with Article 52 paragraph 5 (qualification as a small emitter) the monitoring plan must be resubmitted to the FOEN for evaluation.

### 3 Calculation of the carbon emissions of aircraft

- 3\.1 The carbon emissions in tonnes are calculated according to the following formula:
- *Carbon emissions [f CO**<sub>2</sub>**] = fuel consumed [f fuel] x emission factor [f CO**<sub>2</sub>**/f fuel].*
- 3\.2 The following emission factors [t CO<sub>2</sub>/t fuel] apply to the following fuels:
- Kerosene (jet A-1 or jet A): 3.16
- Jet B: 3.10
- Aviation gasoline (AvGas): 3.10
- 3\.3

  The emission factor of the following fuels is zero:

  - a. renewable fuels from biomass, provided the biomass used meets the sustainability criteria in accordance with Article 29 of Directive (EU) 2018/2001[^annex16-lvlu1-lvl3-1];
  - b. renewable synthetic fuels whose energy content originates from renewable energy sources other than biomass and which meet the requirements of Article 29*a* of Directive (EU) 2018/2001.

- 3\.4 To calculate and report the emission factor of a fuel blend, the emission factor under number 3.2 is multiplied by the share of fossil fuel in the blend.

[^annex16-lvlu1-lvl3-1]: See footnote to Annex 15 No 5.1 let. b.

Annex 17[^t-25]

(Art. 52)

## Requirements for the monitoring report

[^t-25]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 25 Nov. 2020 ([AS 2020 6081](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2020/1009)) and of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Monitoring report for installation operators

- 1\.1

  The monitoring report must contain:

  - a. information about the greenhouse gas emissions and energy consumption and their development;
  - b. information about the data required to assess an adjustment to the quantity of emission allowances to be allocated free of charge under Article 46*b*;
  - c. accounting for the sources of energy;
  - d. information about any changes in production capacities;
  - e. quantities (primary data) and parameters applied in calculating the green-house gas emissions and energy consumption;
  - f. operating times of measuring systems, details of measurement failures and how they are taken into account and comprehensible measurement results;
  - g. proof that the renewable content of the energy sources used is indicated on the invoices and that the corresponding guarantees of origin have been assigned to the ETS in the guarantee of origin system for fuels, provided that the use of these energy sources is to be claimed in the ETS with a lower emission factor than that for fossil fuels;
  - h. proof of the biomass content of energy sources which are not recorded in the guarantee of origin system for fuels, or of materials used in processes, provided that their use in the ETS is to be claimed with a lower emission factor than that for fossil energy sources.

- 1\.2 The data must be shown in a summary table with comparative data of the previous years. The FOEN shall issue guidelines on the form of the monitoring report.

### 2 Monitoring report for aircraft operators

- 2\.1

  The monitoring report must contain:

  - a. the information required to identify the aircraft operator;
  - b. the information required to identify the verifier responsible for verifying the monitoring report unless the aircraft operator is exempted from the verification obligation as a small emitter;
  - c. a reference to the approved monitoring plan and a description and substantiation of any deviations from the underlying monitoring plan;
  - d. the information required to identify the aircraft used;
  - e. the total number of flights recorded;
  - f. the emission factor and fuel consumption for each fuel type;
  - g. the total of all carbon emissions for flights for which data have to be recorded and which were conducted by the operator in the calendar year, broken down by state of departure and state of arrival and broken down by Swiss ETS and European Union ETS;
  - h. in the event of data gaps, a description of the reasons for the data gaps, the method used to estimate the substitute data and the emissions calculated on the basis of it;
  - i. for each aerodrome pair, the ICAO aerodrome designation and the number of flights for which the data have to be recorded and the respective annual emissions;
  - j. the CO<sub>2</sub> equivalents of the other climate-impacting effects of flights for which data must be collected and which were operated by the operator during the calendar year, calculated in accordance with Article 56*a* of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2018/2066[^annex17-lvlu1-lvl2-1], by departure and arrival airport.

- 2\.2 Small emitters under Article 55 paragraph 1 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2018/2066 may estimate their fuel consumption using an instrument for small emitters in accordance with Article 55 paragraph 2 of Implementing Regulation (EU) 2018/2066.
- 2\.3 The following special provisions apply to the use of fuels in accordance with Annex 16 number 3.3:
- 2\.3.1 In the case of fuel blends, the aircraft operator may report the share of fuels in accordance with Annex 16 number 3.3 as 100 percent fossil, or may determine the share of fuels in accordance with Annex 16, number 3.3 as accurately as possible.
- 2\.3.2 Aircraft operators must allocate fuels used in accordance with Annex 16 number 3.3 to those flights which are subject to the levy under Article 55 paragraph 2, in proportion to their total emissions from Switzerland, provided that the delivery to the aircraft is not made in physically identifiable batches.
- 2\.3.3 With regard to the thresholds for participation in the ETS, for qualification as a small emitter, and for exemption from the verification requirement, the emission factors specified in Annex 16 number 3.2 shall apply to the fuels specified in Annex 16 number 3.3.
- 2\.3.4

  Aircraft operators must demonstrate that:

  - a. the share of fuels pursuant to Annex 16 number 3.3 and allocated to flights aggregated by airport pairs does not exceed the blending limit for these fuels as established by a recognised international standard;
  - b. for the fuels pursuant to Annex 16 number 3.3, the renewable content is shown on the invoices and the corresponding guarantees of origin have been assigned to the ETS in the guarantee of origin system for fuels, provided they wish to have these fuels counted towards their ETS obligations.

[^annex17-lvlu1-lvl2-1]: Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) 2018/2066 of 19 December 2018 on the monitoring and reporting of greenhouse gas emissions pursuant to Directive 2003/87/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council and amending Commission Regulation (EU) No 601/2012, OJ L 334 of 31.12.2018, p. 1; last amended by Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/2493, OJ L, 2024/2493, 27.9.2024.

Annex 18[^t-26]

(Art. 52)

## Verification of the monitoring reports of aircraft operators and requirements to be met by the verifier

[^t-26]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 13 Nov. 2019 ([AS 2019 4335](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2019/753)). Revised by No II para. 1 of the O of 2 April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

### 1 Duties of the verification body and the aircraft operator

- 1\.1 The verification body shall verify the reliability, credibility and accuracy of the monitoring systems, the submitted data and other information as specified in Annex 18 number 2. In particular it shall ensure that the data permit the calculation of the carbon emissions.
- 1\.2 The aircraft operator shall grant the verification body access to all data and documentation that are required for the verification procedure. In particular, the aircraft operator shall obtain from Eurocontrol the necessary flight operator data or equivalent data and make them available to the verification body.

### 2 Specific requirements concerning verification

- 2\.1

  The verification body shall ensure that all flights have been taken into account:

  - a. for which the aircraft operator is responsible;
  - b. that have in fact been conducted;
  - c. for which data have to be recorded in accordance with this Ordinance.

- 2\.2 For this purpose the verification body shall use the flight plan data and the data obtained by the aircraft operator from Eurocontrol or other sources.

### 3 Verification steps

The verification of monitoring reports shall be carried out as follows:

- 3\.1 analysis of all activities carried out by the aircraft operator (strategic analysis);
- 3\.2 performance of random checks in order to determine the reliability of the submitted data and other information (process analysis);
- 3\.3 analysis of error risk relating to the utilised data, and examination of the procedures for limiting error risk (risk analysis);
- 3\.4 preparation of a verification report in which it is stated whether the monitoring report meets the requirements of this Ordinance. The verification report shall list all relevant aspects of the activities carried out within the scope of the verification procedure.

### 4 Requirements to be met by the verifier

- 4\.1

  The verification body must be accredited for the mandated verification activity in accordance with:

  - a. the Accreditation and Designation Ordinance of 17 June 1996[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-1]; or
  - b. Ordinance (EC) No 765/2008[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-2] and Implementing Regulation (EU) 2018/2067[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-3].

- 4\.2 The verification body must be independent of the aircraft operator and perform its duties professionally and objectively.
- 4\.3 The verification body must be able to demonstrate that it possesses the required professional abilities to verify carbon emission data in the civil aviation sector and is familiar with the way in which all information for the monitoring report is produced, in particular with respect to the acquisition, calculation and transmission of data.
- 4\.4 The verification body must be familiar with all the relevant provisions and applicable legal and administrative regulations.

[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-1]: [SR 946.512](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/vocabulary/legal-taxonomy/11469)
[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-2]: Ordinance (EC) No 765/2008 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 July 2008 setting out the requirements for accreditation and market surveillance relating to the marketing of products and repealing Ordinance (EEC) No 339/93 of the Council, OJ L 218 of 13.8.2008, p. 30, last amended by Regulation (EU) 2019/1020, OJ L 169 of 25.6.2019, p. 1.
[^annex18-lvlu1-lvl4-3]: Commission Implementing Ordinance (EU) 2018/2067 of 19 December 2018 on the verification of data and on the accreditation of verifiers pursuant to Directive 2003/87/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, OJ L 334 of 31.12.2018, p. 94, last amended by Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/1321, OJ L, 2024/1321, 13.5.2024.

Annex 19[^t-27]

(Arts 5 para. 2, 55 para. 1<sup>bis</sup> and 66*a* para. 2)

## Carbon storage and chemical bonding

[^t-27]: Inserted by No II para. 3 of the O of 2. April 2025, in force since 1 Jan. 2025 ([AS 2025 248](https://fedlex.data.admin.ch/eli/oc/2025/248)).

The following requirements must be met in the storage and chemical bonding of carbon dioxide:

- a. Storage and chemical bonding must be shown to be permanent and must be traceable.
- b. The permanence of storage and chemical bonding must be checked annually. Any leakage counts as carbon emissions and must be reported to the FOEN.
- c. Any leakage that occurs when transporting removed carbon dioxide counts as carbon emissions and must be reported to the FOEN.
- d. Where carbon dioxide is stored in a geological repository, the site must be ap-proved in Switzerland and recorded in the land register or must be an ap-proved site abroad pursuant to Directive 2009/31/EC[^annex19-lvlu1-1].

[^annex19-lvlu1-1]: See footnote to Art. 55 para. 1<sup>bis</sup> let. b.
